================================================================================
E-COMMERCE BACKEND — COMPLETE IMPLEMENTATION BLUEPRINT
A language-agnostic specification, with the full reference implementation
================================================================================
Document version : 1.0
Blueprint date   : 2026-11-26
Reference stack  : TypeScript 5 / Node.js 22 / Fastify 5 / PostgreSQL 16 / Redis 7
Target audience  : anyone rebuilding this service in another language or
                   framework (Python/FastAPI, Java/Spring Boot, Go, C#, ...)
Companion file   : ecommerce-backend-source.txt (raw source dump)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
HOW TO USE THIS DOCUMENT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
This document is a build specification, not just documentation. It is organised
so that a reader can implement the system from scratch without seeing the
TypeScript sources:

  Part 1  Scope, technology, architecture, directory layout, file catalogue
  Part 2  Domain model, ports, use cases, authorization
          2.1 domain primitives        2.5 ports
          2.2 aggregates + invariants 2.6 use cases (UC-01..UC-16)
          2.3 domain events           2.7 authorization policy
          2.4 error catalogue + HTTP mapping
  Part 3  Contracts, adapters, operations, porting
          3.1 HTTP conventions        3.7 operations (scripts, image, CI)
          3.2 endpoint specifications 3.8 Python/FastAPI porting guide
          3.3 persistence model       3.9 Java/Spring Boot porting guide
          3.4 adapter algorithms      3.10 build order
          3.5 cross-cutting behaviour 3.11 acceptance checklist
          3.6 testing strategy        3.12 known limitations
  Appendix A  Annotated directory tree with file sizes
  Appendix B  Full verbatim source of every project file

Read Parts 1–2 before writing code. Part 3 holds the contracts to implement.
Sections 3.8 and 3.9 map every reference piece onto Python or Java
frameworks. Appendix B is the escape hatch: when a rule here is ambiguous, the
reference implementation in the appendix is authoritative.

Conventions used below:

  MUST / MUST NOT  a rule the implementation has to honour
  SHOULD           a rule with a documented, acceptable alternative
  "Result"         a success-or-expected-failure value, never an exception
  "port"           an interface owned by the application layer
  "adapter"        an implementation of a port, owned by infrastructure
  ISO instant      UTC timestamp, ISO-8601 with milliseconds, e.g.
                   2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z

Where this document says "reference", it means the TypeScript implementation in
Appendix B; behaviour — not syntax — is what must be reproduced.

================================================================================
PART 1 — SCOPE, TECHNOLOGY, ARCHITECTURE, LAYOUT, FILE CATALOGUE
================================================================================

1.1 PRODUCT SCOPE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
A single-tenant e-commerce backend covering the complete purchase path:

  identity        register, log in, inspect own profile, role-based access
  catalogue       create (admin), read one, list all products
  inventory       one stock record per product, split free vs reserved stock
  cart            one open cart per customer, add/remove lines, price snapshots
  orders          place from cart, read, cancel, pay
  checkout        place + charge + commit, idempotent, compensating on decline
  events          transactional outbox, at-least-once delivery, DLQ, consumer
  operations      health, readiness, Prometheus metrics, graceful shutdown

Out of scope for this version (do not invent them while porting; they are listed
as explicit future work in Part 12/13): refresh tokens, email verification,
password reset, MFA, shipping/delivery states, refunds, reconciliation jobs,
multi-currency conversion, multi-warehouse stock, search, pagination, admin UI.

The service is stateless in HTTP terms: all durable state lives in PostgreSQL
(or the in-memory fallback), all session state lives in the signed token.

1.2 REFERENCE TECHNOLOGY AND ITS ROLE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Language/runtime   TypeScript 5.x, ESM, Node.js >= 22
  HTTP               Fastify 5 (schema-free routing; Zod validates at the edge)
  Validation         Zod 4, request-body and path-param schemas only
  Persistence        PostgreSQL 16 via Prisma 6 (`@prisma/client`)
  Cache/idempotency  Redis 7 via ioredis 5 (optional; in-memory default)
  Messaging          Kafka (KRaft, apache/kafka 3.9) via kafkajs 2 (optional)
  Tokens             JWT HS256 via `jose` 6
  Passwords          scrypt from the Node standard library (`node:crypto`)
  Metrics            prom-client 15
  Tests              Vitest 5
  Lint/format        ESLint 10 + typescript-eslint 8, Prettier 3
  Build              `tsc` to `dist/`, run with plain `node`

Nothing in the domain or application layer imports any of the libraries above.
That is the central design constraint, and the porting guide depends on it: the
inner layers are ordinary data + functions, so they translate almost verbatim.

1.3 ARCHITECTURE: CLEAN ARCHITECTURE WITH FUNCTIONAL CORE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Four layers, one direction of dependency:

  +-------------------------------------------------------------+
  |  main (composition root)                                    |
  |  reads config, chooses adapters, starts HTTP + worker       |
  +-----------------------------+-------------------------------+
                                | may import everything inward
  +-----------------------------v-------------------------------+
  |  infrastructure (adapters)                                  |
  |  Fastify routes/controllers, Prisma, Redis, Kafka, scrypt,  |
  |  JWT, metrics, health, in-memory adapters                   |
  +-----------------------------+-------------------------------+
                                | may import application + domain
  +-----------------------------v-------------------------------+
  |  application (use cases + ports)                            |
  |  orchestrates domain rules, transactions, idempotency       |
  +-----------------------------+-------------------------------+
                                | may import domain only
  +-----------------------------v-------------------------------+
  |  domain (entities, value objects, rules, events, errors)     |
  |  pure: no I/O, no clock, no ids, no frameworks               |
  +-------------------------------------------------------------+

Dependency rule (MUST):

  domain          -> domain only
  application     -> application, domain
  infrastructure  -> application, domain (+ third-party adapters)
  main            -> anything inward, and nothing imports main

Additional prohibitions enforced by the reference architecture test:

  - domain and application MUST NOT import Fastify, Zod, Prisma, ioredis,
    kafkajs, jose, prom-client, or any `node:*` module.
  - domain and application MUST NOT read `process.env`, call `Date.now()`,
    `new Date()`, or `randomUUID()` directly. Time and identity are ports.
  - infrastructure MUST NOT import main.
  - Business rules MUST NOT live in routes, controllers, or repositories.

Design style: plain structural types and pure functions. No classes, no
decorators, no dependency-injection framework. Composition happens once, in
`main/container.ts`, by passing dependencies as function arguments.

Expected failures are values, not exceptions:

  Result<T, E> = { ok: true, value: T } | { ok: false, error: E }

A use case returns `Result`. Only genuinely unexpected faults (a driver crash)
may throw; the HTTP edge converts those to a generic 500.

1.4 RUNTIME TOPOLOGY
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Single process serves HTTP and runs the outbox worker. Optional external
services are selected by environment variables at start-up:

  DATABASE_URL  unset -> in-memory repositories (state lost on restart)
                set   -> Prisma repositories + serializable transactions
  REDIS_URL     unset -> in-process cache, idempotency, rate limiting
                set   -> Redis-backed adapters shared across replicas
  KAFKA_BROKERS unset -> events printed to stdout, no consumer
                set   -> Kafka producer behind the outbox + consumer group

No use case changes when an adapter changes. Tests exploit this: the whole suite
runs with the in-memory adapters, and the same tests can be re-pointed at real
PostgreSQL/Redis through environment variables.

Request lifecycle (HTTP):

  socket
    -> Fastify onRequest hook: parse bearer token, verify, set request.principal
    -> onSend hook: x-request-id + security headers
    -> global preHandler: rate limit (except /health, /ready, /metrics)
    -> route-specific preHandler: requireAuth / requireAdmin / auth limiter
    -> controller: Zod-parse params/body, ownership policy, call use case
    -> presenter: domain object -> HTTP JSON shape
    -> error mapper: error.type -> status code (or generic 500)
    -> onResponse hook: record metrics

Write lifecycle (the part most examples omit):

  use case
    -> unitOfWork.execute(async () => {
         domain rules (pure)
         repository.save(...)             (joins the transaction)
         stageEvent(...)                  (outbox row, same transaction)
         return ok(...) | err(...)        (err rolls everything back)
       })
    -> outbox worker later reads due rows and publishes them
    -> consumer records event id in inbox before handling

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Layout below is the reference layout. Any language can mirror it almost
one-to-one; Part 11 gives concrete Python and Java variants.

  ecommerce-backend/

  |   |-- domain/
  |   |   |-- shared/       domain-error.ts, domain-event.ts, result.ts
  |   |   |-- customer/     customer.ts, customer-id.ts, customer-errors.ts,

  |   |   |-- cart/         cart.ts, cart-item.ts, cart-errors.ts
  |   |   |-- order/        order.ts, order-item.ts, order-status.ts,
  |   |   |                 order-events.ts, order-errors.ts

  |   |   `-- auth/         account.ts, role.ts, auth-errors.ts
  |   |-- application/

  |   |   |                 register/, login/

  |   |   |-- cart/         add-item/, remove-item/, get-cart/

  |   |   `-- ports/        clock, id-generator, unit-of-work, cache,
  |   |                     rate-limiter, idempotency-store, inbox-store,
  |   |                     outbox, event-publisher, payment-gateway,
  |   |                     password-hasher, token-service,
  |   |                     repositories/{customer,account,product,inventory,
  |   |                                   cart,order}-repository.ts
  |   |-- infrastructure/
  |   |   |-- auth/         scrypt-password-hasher.ts, jwt-token-service.ts
  |   |   |-- cache/        in-memory-cache.ts
  |   |   |-- config/       environment.ts, config.ts
  |   |   |-- events/       console-event-publisher.ts
  |   |   |-- health/       health-check.ts
  |   |   |-- http/         fastify.ts, http-errors.ts, auth-guards.ts,
  |   |   |                 health-routes.ts, rate-limit.ts,
  |   |   |                 controllers/, routes/, schemas/, presenters/
  |   |   |-- idempotency/  in-memory-idempotency-store.ts
  |   |   |-- inbox/        in-memory-inbox-store.ts
  |   |   |-- kafka/        kafka-messages.ts, kafka-topics.ts,
  |   |   |                 kafka-event-publisher.ts, order-events-consumer.ts
  |   |   |-- observability/ metrics.ts
  |   |   |-- outbox/       outbox-worker.ts
  |   |   |-- payments/     in-memory-payment-gateway.ts
  |   |   |-- persistence/
  |   |   |   |-- in-memory/store.ts
  |   |   |   |-- repositories/ in-memory-*.ts (7 adapters)
  |   |   |   `-- prisma/   prisma-client.ts, prisma-session.ts,
  |   |   |                 prisma-mappers.ts, prisma-*-repository.ts (7),
  |   |   |                 prisma-inbox-store.ts, prisma-dependencies.ts
  |   |   |-- rate-limit/   in-memory-rate-limiter.ts
  |   |   |-- redis/        redis-client.ts, redis-cache.ts,
  |   |   |                 redis-idempotency-store.ts, redis-rate-limiter.ts
  |   |   |-- system/       system-clock.ts, uuid-generator.ts
  |   |   `-- transactions/ in-memory-unit-of-work.ts, prisma-unit-of-work.ts
  |   `-- main/
  |       |-- container.ts  composition root
  |       |-- server.ts     entry point
  |       `-- grant-admin.ts operator command
  `-- tests/
      |-- architecture/dependency-rules.test.ts
      |-- unit/domain/*.test.ts
      |-- unit/application/*.test.ts
      |-- unit/infrastructure/*.test.ts
      |-- integration/prisma/prisma-persistence.test.ts
      |-- integration/redis/redis-adapters.test.ts
      |-- e2e/http/*.test.ts
      `-- support/test-environment.ts

Naming conventions in the reference (worth keeping in any language):

  - one folder per use case or aggregate, one file per concept;
  - `*.types.ts` holds the input/output/dependency/error unions of a use case;
  - ports are named after the capability, never the technology
    (`PasswordHasher`, not `BcryptService`);
  - adapters are prefixed with their technology
    (`prisma-order-repository`, `redis-cache`, `in-memory-unit-of-work`);
  - HTTP files are split into routes (path -> handler), controllers
    (validate + authorize + call), schemas (shape), presenters (domain -> JSON).

1.6 FILE CATALOGUE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Every file that is part of the blueprint, with its responsibility. Files marked
"mechanical" are repetitive adapters; their behaviour is specified in Part 7.

DOMAIN (pure business rules; no imports from outside `domain`)

  domain/shared/result.ts
      Result<T,E> plus `ok()` / `err()` helpers. Every fallible function returns
      this instead of throwing.
  domain/shared/domain-error.ts
      DomainError<TType> = { type: string; message: string }. Each module
      narrows it to a union of literal `type` values.
  domain/shared/domain-event.ts
      DomainEvent<TName, TPayload> = { name, occurredAt, payload }.
  domain/customer/customer-id.ts
      Type alias `CustomerId = string` (newtype in stricter languages).
  domain/customer/customer.ts
      Customer type; createCustomer (trim, lowercase email, regex, 1..120 name);
      renameCustomer.
  domain/customer/customer-errors.ts
      invalid_email | invalid_customer_name.
  domain/customer/customer-events.ts
      customerRegistered(customer, occurredAt) -> `customer.registered`
      { customerId, email }.
  domain/product/product-id.ts
      Type alias `ProductId = string`.
  domain/product/money.ts
      Money value object; createMoney (integer, >=0, ISO-4217 uppercase),
      addMoney (currency must match), multiplyMoney, formatMoney.
  domain/product/product.ts
      Product type; createProduct (name 1..160 after trim, price > 0).
  domain/product/product-errors.ts
      invalid_product_name | invalid_amount | invalid_currency |
      currency_mismatch.
  domain/inventory/inventory-id.ts
      Type alias `InventoryId = string`.
  domain/inventory/inventory-item.ts
      available/reserved split; createInventoryItem, checkAvailability (pure),
      reserveStock, releaseStock, commitReservation, restock.
  domain/inventory/inventory-errors.ts
      invalid_quantity | insufficient_stock.
  domain/cart/cart.ts
      Cart aggregate; createCart, addItem (merge, single currency, max 99),
      removeItem, clearCart, cartTotal, cartItemCount.
  domain/cart/cart-item.ts
      CartItem { productId, quantity, unitPrice }; MAX_ITEM_QUANTITY = 99;
      createCartItem; cartItemTotal.
  domain/cart/cart-errors.ts
      invalid_cart_quantity | item_not_in_cart | cart_currency_mismatch.
  domain/order/order.ts
      Order aggregate; createOrder (total derived from lines, at least one),
      payOrder, cancelOrder.
  domain/order/order-item.ts
      OrderItem with lineTotal; createOrderItem; orderItemFromCartItem.
  domain/order/order-status.ts
      "pending_payment" | "paid" | "cancelled"; isPendingPayment.
  domain/order/order-events.ts
      order.created, order.paid, order.cancelled factories.
  domain/order/order-errors.ts
      empty_order | invalid_order_item (creation);
      order_already_paid | order_cancelled (transitions).
  domain/payment/payment.ts
      Payment aggregate; createPayment (amount > 0), succeedPayment,
      failPayment (resolve exactly once).
  domain/payment/payment-status.ts
      "pending" | "succeeded" | "failed"; isPending.
  domain/payment/payment-errors.ts
      invalid_payment_amount | payment_already_resolved.
  domain/auth/role.ts
      ROLES = ["customer","admin"] (closed set); Role; isRole.
  domain/auth/account.ts
      MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH = 12, MAX_PASSWORD_LENGTH = 128; validatePassword;
      createAccount (non-empty hash, known role); assignRole.
  domain/auth/auth-errors.ts
      invalid_password | invalid_role.

APPLICATION (use cases + ports; no frameworks, no I/O primitives)

  application/auth/principal.ts
      Principal { customerId, email, role } — the verified caller.
  application/auth/access-policy.ts
      canActForCustomer(principal, customerId),
      canAccessOrder(principal, order), FORBIDDEN error constant.
  application/auth/session.ts
      AuthenticatedSession { customer, role, accessToken }.
  application/auth/register/register.types.ts
      Register input/error/dependency/function types.
  application/auth/register/register.ts
      Validate password, hash before transaction, create customer + account +
      customer.registered atomically, issue token after commit.
  application/auth/login/login.types.ts
      Login types.
  application/auth/login/login.ts
      Load by email, verify against hash or null, uniform invalid_credentials,
      issue token.
  application/customer/get-customer/{get-customer.ts,*.types.ts}
      Read profile or customer_not_found.
  application/product/create-product/{...}
      Create product + inventory record in one transaction.
  application/product/get-product/{...}
      Read-through cache (60 s), fallback to repository, cache failures ignored.
  application/product/list-products/{...}
      Return all products.
  application/cart/add-item/{...}
      Load product + stock, check availability without reserving, merge into
      cart (create if absent), save.
  application/cart/remove-item/{...}
      Remove a line or item_not_in_cart.
  application/cart/get-cart/{...}
      Read cart, return cart + derived total/itemCount (or cart_not_found).
  application/order/create-order/{...}
      Transaction: snapshot prices, reserve stock, save order, clear cart,
      stage order.created.
  application/order/get-order/{...}
      Read order or order_not_found.
  application/order/cancel-order/{...}
      Transaction: release stock, cancel, stage order.cancelled.
  application/payment/process-payment/{...}
      Read order, charge provider outside transaction, then transaction:
      commit reservation, mark paid, stage order.paid; reconciliation error if
      the post-charge write fails.
  application/checkout/{checkout.ts,checkout.types.ts}
      Idempotent composition of create-order + process-payment + cancel-order.
  application/outbox/stage-event.ts
      Insert an OutboxMessage for a DomainEvent (must run in the transaction).
  application/outbox/backoff.ts
      MAX_DELIVERY_ATTEMPTS = 5; retryDelayMs = min(60s, 1s * 2^(attempt-1)).
  application/outbox/dispatch-outbox.ts
      Read due messages (batch 50), publish, mark published/failed/DLQ.
  application/outbox/dispatch-outbox.types.ts
      DispatchOutbox types + OutboxDispatchSummary.
  application/ports/clock.ts                 Clock.now(): ISO instant
  application/ports/id-generator.ts          IdGenerator.nextId(): string
  application/ports/unit-of-work.ts          execute(work) with rollback on err
  application/ports/cache.ts                 get/set/delete with TTL
  application/ports/rate-limiter.ts          consume(key) -> decision
  application/ports/idempotency-store.ts     find/save/acquire/release
  application/ports/inbox-store.ts           markProcessed/hasProcessed
  application/ports/outbox.ts                OutboxMessage + repository contract
  application/ports/event-publisher.ts       publish(event, envelope)
  application/ports/payment-gateway.ts       charge(request) -> reference
  application/ports/password-hasher.ts       hash/verify (verify accepts null)
  application/ports/token-service.ts         issue/verify access tokens
  application/ports/repositories/customer-repository.ts
      save, findById, findByEmail
  application/ports/repositories/account-repository.ts
      save, findByCustomerId
  application/ports/repositories/product-repository.ts
      save, findById, findAll
  application/ports/repositories/inventory-repository.ts
      save, findByProductId
  application/ports/repositories/cart-repository.ts
      save, findById, findByCustomerId
  application/ports/repositories/order-repository.ts
      save, findById, findByCustomerId

INFRASTRUCTURE (adapters; third-party libraries allowed here only)

  infrastructure/config/environment.ts
      Zod schema for all env vars, defaults, production JWT-secret rule,
      parses CSV lists, returns Result<Config, ConfigError>.
  infrastructure/config/config.ts
      Loads `.env` once (Node `process.loadEnvFile`) and parses it.
  infrastructure/auth/scrypt-password-hasher.ts
      scrypt hashing/verification with embedded parameters, timing-safe
      comparison, fake work for unknown accounts.
  infrastructure/auth/jwt-token-service.ts
      HS256 issue/verify with pinning, issuer/audience, 5 s tolerance.
  infrastructure/http/fastify.ts
      Builds the app: request ids, security headers, metrics hooks, auth
      extraction, route registration, global error handler. Never listens.
  infrastructure/http/auth-guards.ts
      onRequest token extraction, `principal` request decoration, requireAuth,
      requireAdmin, principalOf.
  infrastructure/http/http-errors.ts
      error.type -> HTTP status table + safe error sender.
  infrastructure/http/rate-limit.ts
      Global limiter preHandler, stricter auth limiter, headers, fail-open.
  infrastructure/http/health-routes.ts
      /health, /ready, /metrics.
  infrastructure/http/schemas/auth-schemas.ts
      register/login body shapes (strict objects).
  infrastructure/http/schemas/product-schemas.ts
      create-product body, product id params.
  infrastructure/http/schemas/cart-schemas.ts
      customer id params, cart item params, add-item body.
  infrastructure/http/schemas/order-schemas.ts
      order id params, create-order/checkout body.
  infrastructure/http/schemas/customer-schemas.ts
      customer id params.
  infrastructure/http/presenters/auth-presenter.ts
      Session -> { customer, role, accessToken { token, tokenType, expiresAt } }.
  infrastructure/http/presenters/customer-presenter.ts
      Customer -> { id, email, name, createdAt }.
  infrastructure/http/presenters/product-presenter.ts
      Product -> { id, name, price, createdAt }.
  infrastructure/http/presenters/cart-presenter.ts
      Cart -> items + itemCount + total (derived by domain functions).
  infrastructure/http/presenters/order-presenter.ts
      Order -> full order shape with lineTotal per line.
  infrastructure/http/presenters/payment-presenter.ts
      Payment -> status, provider reference, failure reason.
  infrastructure/http/presenters/checkout-presenter.ts
      CheckoutOutcome -> { order, payment, replayed }.
  infrastructure/http/controllers/auth-controller.ts
      register 201, login 200, me 200.
  infrastructure/http/controllers/customer-controller.ts
      GET /customers/:id with ownership check.
  infrastructure/http/controllers/product-controller.ts
      create 201, list 200, get 200.
  infrastructure/http/controllers/cart-controller.ts
      get/add/remove, all 200, ownership checked before the use case.
  infrastructure/http/controllers/order-controller.ts
      create 201, get 200, cancel 200, pay 200; ownership checked.
  infrastructure/http/controllers/checkout-controller.ts
      Idempotency-Key parsing, ownership, 201 or 200 + replay header.
  infrastructure/http/routes/auth-routes.ts
      POST /auth/register, POST /auth/login, GET /auth/me.
  infrastructure/http/routes/customer-routes.ts
      GET /customers/:id.
  infrastructure/http/routes/product-routes.ts
      POST /products (auth+admin), GET /products, GET /products/:id.
  infrastructure/http/routes/cart-routes.ts
      GET/POST/DELETE cart paths (auth).
  infrastructure/http/routes/order-routes.ts
      POST /orders, GET /orders/:id, POST /orders/:id/cancel,
      POST /orders/:id/pay (auth).
  infrastructure/http/routes/checkout-routes.ts
      POST /checkout (auth).
  infrastructure/persistence/in-memory/store.ts
      One shared store of Maps for all aggregates; snapshot/restore support for
      the in-memory unit of work.
  infrastructure/persistence/repositories/in-memory-*.ts (7 files)
      Map-backed implementations of each repository port; clone on read/write
      so callers cannot mutate stored state by reference.
  infrastructure/persistence/prisma/prisma-client.ts
      Creates PrismaClient, connects, exposes check()/close().
  infrastructure/persistence/prisma/prisma-session.ts
      AsyncLocalStorage carrying the current transaction client.
  infrastructure/persistence/prisma/prisma-mappers.ts
      Row <-> domain translation; Date <-> ISO string; JSON snapshots.
  infrastructure/persistence/prisma/prisma-*-repository.ts (7 files)
      Prisma implementations of the six repositories + outbox.
  infrastructure/persistence/prisma/prisma-inbox-store.ts
      INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING dedupe, returns false when duplicate.
  infrastructure/persistence/prisma/prisma-dependencies.ts
      Builds the whole persistence adapter set + health/close.
  infrastructure/transactions/in-memory-unit-of-work.ts
      Serialised queue + snapshot/restore; err rolls back.
  infrastructure/transactions/prisma-unit-of-work.ts
      Serializable transaction, sentinel rollback, P2034 retry up to 3 times.
  infrastructure/cache/in-memory-cache.ts
      TTL map; expired entries are misses.
  infrastructure/idempotency/in-memory-idempotency-store.ts
      TTL records + atomic in-process claims.
  infrastructure/redis/redis-client.ts
      Single ioredis connection with check()/close().
  infrastructure/redis/redis-cache.ts
      JSON values with TTL; corrupt values are misses.
  infrastructure/redis/redis-idempotency-store.ts
      record/claim keys, SET NX EX, MULTI on save.
  infrastructure/redis/redis-rate-limiter.ts
      Lua INCR+EXPIRE+TTL, shared windows.
  infrastructure/rate-limit/in-memory-rate-limiter.ts
      Fixed-window counters, lazy expiry.
  infrastructure/inbox/in-memory-inbox-store.ts
      Set-backed dedupe for tests/local runs.
  infrastructure/outbox/outbox-worker.ts
      Interval polling, single in-flight batch, stop/runOnce.
  infrastructure/kafka/kafka-messages.ts
      Topic routing, partition key, message body, headers (pure functions).
  infrastructure/kafka/kafka-topics.ts
      Creates declared topics at start-up.
  infrastructure/kafka/kafka-event-publisher.ts
      Idempotent producer, connect/disconnect, publishes mapped messages.
  infrastructure/kafka/order-events-consumer.ts
      Consumer group, header-based id, inbox claim before handler.
  infrastructure/payments/in-memory-payment-gateway.ts
      Deterministic references, idempotent by key, decline hook.
  infrastructure/events/console-event-publisher.ts
      JSON line per event on stdout.
  infrastructure/observability/metrics.ts
      Prometheus registry: HTTP counter/histogram, checkout outcomes, outbox.
  infrastructure/health/health-check.ts
      HealthCheck type + runHealthChecks -> { status, checks }.
  infrastructure/system/system-clock.ts
      Clock -> new Date().toISOString().
  infrastructure/system/uuid-generator.ts
      IdGenerator -> randomUUID().

MAIN

  main/container.ts
      Composition root: choose adapters from options, build ports, build use
      cases, wrap checkout/outbox with metrics, build controllers, expose
      start()/close().
  main/server.ts
      Load config, build container, build HTTP app, start worker, listen,
      graceful shutdown on SIGINT/SIGTERM.
  main/grant-admin.ts
      CLI: find customer by email, assign admin role, save, print result.

TESTS (behaviour specified in Part 9)

  tests/architecture/dependency-rules.test.ts
  tests/support/test-environment.ts
  tests/unit/domain/{account,cart,inventory,inventory-availability,order}.test.ts
  tests/unit/application/{auth,checkout,checkout-concurrency,
      checkout-idempotency,outbox,outbox-backoff}.test.ts
  tests/unit/infrastructure/{idempotency-store,inbox-store,kafka-messages,
      rate-limiter,unit-of-work}.test.ts
  tests/integration/prisma/prisma-persistence.test.ts
  tests/integration/redis/redis-adapters.test.ts
  tests/e2e/http/{auth-flow,checkout-flow,platform-endpoints}.test.ts

PROJECT / OPS FILES

  tsconfig.json           strict compiler options, NodeNext modules
  tsconfig.build.json     build-only config (src, no tests)
  vitest.config.ts        node environment, tests/**/*.test.ts

================================================================================
PART 2 — DOMAIN MODEL, PORTS, APPLICATION USE CASES
================================================================================

2.1 DOMAIN PRIMITIVES
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Result
  A fallible operation returns either a success value or an expected failure.
  Success and failure are both data. There is no control-flow exception for
  business errors.

    Result<T, E> =
      | { ok: true,  value: T }
      | { ok: false, error: E }

  Implementations in other languages:
    Python  a dataclass/tuple (`Ok(value)` / `Err(error)`) or a local `Result`
            union; never raise for expected failures.
    Java    a sealed interface `Result<T,E>` with `Ok`/`Err`, or a record with
            an `isOk` flag. Do not use exceptions for declines/validation.
    Go      `(T, *E)` or a small generic `Result[T,E]`.

DomainError
    { type: string; message: string }

  `type` is a stable machine-readable code (snake_case). `message` is a human
  sentence that is safe to return to the caller. Each aggregate narrows `type`
  to a closed union; adding a new failure means adding a variant everywhere it
  is handled (the compiler enforces that in TypeScript).

Money
    { amountInMinorUnits: integer >= 0, currency: "XXX" }

  Rules:
    - No floating point, anywhere. 19.99 USD is 1999.
    - amount MUST be a safe integer; negative amounts are rejected.
    - currency is trimmed, uppercased, and MUST match ^[A-Z]{3}$ (ISO-4217).
    - addMoney refuses to combine different currencies (`currency_mismatch`).
    - multiplyMoney multiplies by a whole quantity (callers bound quantity).
    - Storage: two columns (`amountInMinorUnits INTEGER`, `currency TEXT`).
    - JSON: `{ "amountInMinorUnits": 1999, "currency": "USD" }`.
  Porting note: use `long`/`BigInteger` in Java or Python `int` (arbitrary
  precision) for minor units; keep the JSON field names exactly as above.

Identifiers
    All aggregate ids are opaque strings generated by the IdGenerator port
    (UUID v4 in production, sequential ids in tests). Never derive meaning from
    an id, never expose sequential database keys.

Timestamps
    All business timestamps are ISO-8601 UTC strings with milliseconds.
    The domain never reads the clock; `now` is passed in. Storage uses
    `timestamptz(3)`. JSON exposes the same ISO string.

Domain events
    { name: string, occurredAt: ISO instant, payload: object }

    name is `<aggregate>.<past-tense-verb>`. Events describe something that has
    already happened and contain only facts the outside needs.

2.2 AGGREGATES AND INVARIANTS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

CUSTOMER
  Fields: id, email, name, createdAt.
  Rules:
    - email = trim + lowercase; must match ^[^\s@]+@[^\s@]+\.[^\s@]+$
      (deliberately permissive; verification is a later feature).
    - name = trim; length 1..120.
    - Email uniqueness is a persistence/use-case rule (`email_already_registered`
      and a DB unique index). Do not rely on the regex for uniqueness.
  Errors: invalid_email, invalid_customer_name.
  Operations: createCustomer, renameCustomer (keeps id/createdAt).

ACCOUNT (credential record, one per customer)
  Fields: customerId (primary key), passwordHash, role, createdAt, updatedAt.
  Rules:
    - Password length 12..128 (no composition rules). 12 is the OWASP floor;
      128 caps the work an attacker can force in one request.
    - passwordHash MUST be non-empty. The domain never sees a plaintext.
    - role MUST be one of the closed set { "customer", "admin" }.
    - Self-registration can only produce `customer`, except for addresses in
      BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_EMAILS. Elevation is an operator command.
  Errors: invalid_password, invalid_role.
  Operations: validatePassword, createAccount, assignRole.

PRODUCT
  Fields: id, name, price(Money), createdAt.
  Rules:
    - name = trim; length 1..160.
    - price.amountInMinorUnits > 0 (a free product would make payment
      meaningless). Currency must be valid.
    - Availability is NOT a product property; it belongs to InventoryItem.
  Errors: invalid_product_name, invalid_amount, invalid_currency,
          currency_mismatch (from Money helpers).
  Operations: createProduct.

INVENTORY ITEM (one per product)
  Fields: id, productId, available, reserved, updatedAt.
  Meaning: `available` units may still be promised; `reserved` units are held
  for open orders. Total physical stock = available + reserved.

  Operations and rules:
    createInventoryItem(quantity >= 0)
        available = quantity, reserved = 0.
    checkAvailability(item, qty >= 1) -> true | insufficient_stock
        Pure read; never mutates. Used when pricing a cart.
    reserveStock(item, qty >= 1)
        Requires available >= qty; available -= qty; reserved += qty.
        Used when an order is placed.
    releaseStock(item, qty >= 1)
        Requires reserved >= qty; available += qty; reserved -= qty.
        Used when an order is cancelled.
    commitReservation(item, qty >= 1)
        Requires reserved >= qty; reserved -= qty (available unchanged).
        Used once payment succeeded: units are sold, not free again.
    restock(item, qty >= 1)
        available += qty. Used for deliveries.
  Errors: invalid_quantity, insufficient_stock.

  Invariants: available >= 0, reserved >= 0, integers only.

CART (one open cart per customer)
  Fields: id, customerId, items[CartItem], createdAt, updatedAt.
  CartItem: productId, quantity(1..99), unitPrice(Money) — the price at the time
  the line was added. A later price change MUST NOT rewrite existing carts.

  Rules:
    - A cart may be empty; totals are then null (no currency is implied).
    - Adding an existing product merges quantities; merged quantity must stay
      <= 99. The line's unitPrice becomes the newly supplied price.
    - All lines MUST share one currency; otherwise `cart_currency_mismatch`.
    - Adding to a cart never reserves stock; it only checks availability.
    - cartTotal = sum(unitPrice * quantity), null when empty.
    - cartItemCount = sum(quantity).
  Errors: invalid_cart_quantity, item_not_in_cart, cart_currency_mismatch.
  Operations: createCart, addItem, removeItem, clearCart, cartTotal,
              cartItemCount.

ORDER
  Fields: id, customerId, items[OrderItem], total(Money), status,
          createdAt, updatedAt, paidAt|null, cancelledAt|null.
  OrderItem: productId, quantity(>=1), unitPrice(Money), lineTotal(Money).
  lineTotal = unitPrice * quantity. Total is derived from the lines; a caller
  can never supply a total.

  Status machine (closed set):
        create
          |
          v
    pending_payment --- pay() ---> paid
          |
        cancel()
          |
          v
      cancelled

    Allowed:
      pending_payment -> paid       (sets paidAt, updatedAt)
      pending_payment -> cancelled  (sets cancelledAt, updatedAt)
    Rejected:
      paid     -> pay        order_already_paid
      paid     -> cancel     order_already_paid ("A paid order cannot be cancelled")
      cancelled-> pay        order_cancelled
      cancelled-> cancel     order_cancelled

  Rules:
    - At least one line (`empty_order`).
    - Each line quantity is a positive integer (`invalid_order_item`).
    - Line prices are snapshots; editing a product afterwards cannot change an
      existing order.
  Errors: empty_order, invalid_order_item, order_already_paid, order_cancelled.
  Operations: createOrder, payOrder, cancelOrder.

PAYMENT
  Fields: id, orderId, amount(Money), status, providerReference|null,
          failureReason|null, createdAt, updatedAt.
  Status: "pending" | "succeeded" | "failed".

  Rules:
    - amount > 0 (`invalid_payment_amount`).
    - A payment starts pending and resolves exactly once.
    - succeedPayment sets providerReference; failPayment sets failureReason.
    - Resolving a non-pending payment -> `payment_already_resolved`.
  Errors: invalid_payment_amount, payment_already_resolved.
  Operations: createPayment, succeedPayment, failPayment.

2.3 DOMAIN EVENTS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  customer.registered
      payload { customerId: string, email: string }
      emitted by register, in the registration transaction.

  order.created
      payload { orderId, customerId, totalInMinorUnits, currency }
      emitted by create-order, in the order transaction.

  order.paid
      payload { orderId, customerId, providerReference }
      emitted by process-payment, in the commit transaction.

  order.cancelled
      payload { orderId, customerId }
      emitted by cancel-order, in the cancellation transaction.

Event `occurredAt` equals the use case's single `now` value. Events travel as
value objects; only the outbox stores/sends them.

2.4 COMPLETE ERROR CATALOGUE AND HTTP MAPPING
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Error type                     Origin                         HTTP
-----------------------------  -----------------------------  ----
invalid_credentials            login                          401
forbidden                      access policy / guards         403
invalid_customer               register (mapping)             400
invalid_product                create-product (mapping)       400
invalid_inventory              create-product (mapping)       400
invalid_order                  create-order (mapping)         400
invalid_payment                process-payment (mapping)      400
invalid_cart_quantity          cart domain/use cases          400
cart_currency_mismatch         cart domain                    409
insufficient_stock             inventory domain               409
email_already_registered       register                       409
order_already_paid             order transitions              409
order_cancelled                order transitions              409
empty_cart                     create-order                   409
item_not_in_cart               cart domain                    409
payment_declined               payment gateway                402
payment_gateway_unavailable    payment gateway                502
idempotency_unavailable        checkout                       503
checkout_in_progress           checkout                      409

not-found family (404)         any use case:
  customer_not_found, product_not_found, cart_not_found, order_not_found,
  inventory_not_found

invalid_* family (400) except invalid_credentials:
  invalid_email, invalid_customer_name, invalid_password, invalid_role,
  invalid_quantity, invalid_product_name, invalid_amount, invalid_currency,
  invalid_order_item, invalid_payment_amount, invalid_configuration

server faults (500, generic message):
  transaction_failed, payment_requires_reconciliation, repository_error,
  outbox_unavailable, payment_already_resolved

Mapping rule (implement exactly, in this order):
  1. explicit table above wins (this is why invalid_credentials is 401, not 400);
  2. type ends with `_not_found`            -> 404;
  3. type starts with `invalid_`            -> 400;
  4. otherwise                              -> 500.

Response bodies:
  4xx -> { "message": <error.message> }
  5xx -> { "message": "Internal server error" }  (log the real cause once)
  401 also sets `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer realm="api"`.

2.5 PORTS (APPLICATION-OWNED INTERFACES)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Every port below is expressed neutrally. Concrete adapters are in Part 7.

Clock
    now(): ISO instant
    Why: deterministic tests; no direct clock access in the core.

IdGenerator
    nextId(): string
    Why: deterministic ids in tests; no randomUUID in the core.

UnitOfWork
    execute<T,E>(work: () => Promise<Result<T,E>>) -> Result<T, E | UnitOfWorkError>

    Contract:
      - all writes performed inside `work` commit together;
      - returning `err` MUST roll back and MUST NOT commit partial writes;
      - throwing also rolls back and becomes `transaction_failed`;
      - domain rules run inside so they see consistent data;
      - nested calls reuse the same transaction (reference: AsyncLocalStorage).
    UnitOfWorkError = { type: "transaction_failed", message }.

CustomerRepository
    save(customer)
    findById(id) -> Customer | null
    findByEmail(email) -> Customer | null

AccountRepository
    save(account)                    (upsert semantics)
    findByCustomerId(customerId) -> Account | null

ProductRepository
    save(product)                    (upsert)
    findById(id) -> Product | null
    findAll() -> Product[]

InventoryRepository
    save(item)                       (upsert)
    findByProductId(productId) -> InventoryItem | null

CartRepository
    save(cart)                       (upsert)
    findById(id) -> Cart | null
    findByCustomerId(customerId) -> Cart | null   (unique index enforces one)

OrderRepository
    save(order)                      (upsert)
    findById(id) -> Order | null
    findByCustomerId(customerId) -> Order[]

OutboxRepository
    add(message: OutboxMessage)      MUST join the caller's transaction
    findPending(limit, now) -> OutboxMessage[]
        due = publishedAt IS NULL AND deadLetteredAt IS NULL
              AND nextAttemptAt <= now, oldest first
    markPublished(id, publishedAt)
    markFailed(id, { error, nextAttemptAt, deadLetteredAt })
    findDeadLettered(limit) -> OutboxMessage[]

    OutboxMessage = {
      id, event: { name, occurredAt, payload },
      createdAt, publishedAt|null, attempts,
      lastError|null, nextAttemptAt, deadLetteredAt|null
    }

InboxStore
    markProcessed({ id, eventName, processedAt }) -> boolean
        true  = first delivery, handler should run
        false = duplicate, acknowledge and skip
    hasProcessed(id) -> boolean
    The insert-and-see must be atomic (unique key / ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING).

Cache
    get<T>(key) -> T | null
    set<T>(key, value, ttlSeconds)
    delete(key)
    Failures are tolerated by callers; the cache is never a source of truth.

IdempotencyStore
    find<TResult>(key) -> { result: TResult, createdAt } | null
    save<TResult>(key, record, ttlSeconds)     (also releases the claim)
    acquire(key, ttlSeconds) -> "acquired" | "busy"   (atomic)
    release(key)
    Only successful outcomes are saved. A claim protects in-flight work.

RateLimiter
    consume(key) -> { allowed, limit, remaining, resetInSeconds }
    Fixed window; the key is chosen by the caller (IP + route).

EventPublisher
    publish(event: DomainEvent, envelope: { id: string }) -> void
    At-least-once is acceptable: consumers deduplicate by envelope.id.

PaymentGateway
    charge({ orderId, customerId, amount: Money, idempotencyKey }) ->
        Result<{ providerReference: string }, PaymentGatewayFailure>

    PaymentGatewayFailure =
        { type: "payment_declined", message }          (business outcome)
      | { type: "payment_gateway_unavailable", message } (infrastructure)

PasswordHasher
    hash(password) -> self-describing hash string
    verify(password, storedHash | null) -> boolean
    MUST spend comparable work when storedHash is null.

TokenService
    issue(principal: Principal) -> { token, expiresAt }
    verify(token) -> Principal | null   (never throws for bad tokens)

2.6 APPLICATION USE CASES
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Each use case is a function factory: dependencies in, function out. Inputs are
plain data, outputs are `Result`. No HTTP types, no SQL, no broker types.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
UC-01 register
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Input:  { email: string, name: string, password: string }
Output: AuthenticatedSession { customer, role, accessToken }
Deps:   customers, accounts, hasher, tokens, ids, clock, outbox,
        unitOfWork, bootstrapAdminEmails[]

Steps:
  1. validatePassword(password) -> 12..128, else invalid_password.
  2. passwordHash = hasher.hash(password)             [BEFORE the transaction:
     key derivation is slow and must not hold a serializable transaction].
  3. transaction:
     a. now = clock.now()
     b. createCustomer({ id: ids.nextId(), email, name, createdAt: now })
        -> invalid_customer (wraps invalid_email/invalid_customer_name)
     c. customers.findByEmail(normalised email)
        -> if exists: email_already_registered (409)
     d. role = bootstrapAdminEmails includes(email) ? "admin" : "customer"
     e. createAccount({ customerId, passwordHash, role, createdAt: now })
        -> invalid_password/invalid_role
     f. customers.save(customer); accounts.save(account)
     g. stageEvent(customerRegistered(customer, now))
     h. return ok({ customer, role })
  4. if transaction failed, return its error (NO token is issued).
  5. issue accessToken for { customerId, email, role } and return the session.

Invariants:
  - This is the only way a customer is created. There is no "create customer"
    endpoint; a profile without a credential must be impossible.
  - Token issuance happens only after commit.
  - Duplicate email relies on the use-case check plus a DB unique index; under a
    race the unique violation surfaces as a transaction failure (500) — an
    acceptable trade-off documented in Part 13.
Errors: invalid_password, invalid_customer, email_already_registered,
        invalid_role, transaction_failed, repository_* via transaction.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
UC-02 login
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Input:  { email: string, password: string }
Output: AuthenticatedSession
Deps:   customers, accounts, hasher, tokens

Steps:
  1. customer = customers.findByEmail(email)  (repository errors ->
     repository_error). The domain normalises on registration; login should
     normalise (trim/lowercase) before lookup to be safe.
  2. account = customer ? accounts.findByCustomerId(customer.id) : null.
  3. matches = hasher.verify(password, account?.passwordHash ?? null)
     — this call happens even when account is null, so an unknown address costs
     one key derivation.
  4. If customer/account missing OR !matches: invalid_credentials
     ("Email or password is incorrect"). Never reveal which part failed.
  5. Issue token with the stored role and return { customer, role, token }.
Errors: repository_error, invalid_credentials.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
UC-03 getCustomer
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Input:  { id }
Output: Customer
Steps: findById; null -> customer_not_found; repository throw ->
       repository_error. No authorization here (HTTP layer owns it).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
UC-04 createProduct
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Input:  { name, amountInMinorUnits, currency, availableQuantity? }
Output: Product
Deps:   products, inventory, ids, clock, unitOfWork

Steps (one transaction):
  1. createMoney(amount, currency) -> invalid_product (400).
  2. createProduct({ id, name, price, createdAt }) -> invalid_product.
  3. createInventoryItem({ id, productId, quantity: availableQuantity ?? 0 })
     -> invalid_inventory.
  4. products.save(product); inventory.save(stock).
  5. return ok(product).
Why one transaction: a product without a stock row cannot be sold; splitting
them would create unsellable catalogue entries.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
UC-05 getProduct
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Input:  { id }
Output: Product
Deps:   products, cache
Constants: PRODUCT_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS = 60, key = `product:<id>`

Steps:
  1. cache.get(key); hit -> return it. Cache errors are swallowed.
  2. products.findById(id); repository error -> repository_error (500);
     null -> product_not_found (404).
  3. cache.set(key, product, 60); failure ignored.
  4. return product.
Cache invariant: JSON-serialisable domain shape; deleting/updating a product
would need invalidation (not implemented; products are immutable today).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
UC-06 listProducts
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Input:  none
Output: Product[]
Steps: products.findAll(); error -> repository_error. No pagination in v1.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
UC-07 addItem (to cart)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Input:  { customerId, productId, quantity }
Output: Cart
Deps:   carts, products, inventory, ids, clock

Steps:
  1. product = products.findById(productId) -> repository_error / 
     product_not_found.
  2. stock = inventory.findByProductId(productId) -> repository_error /
     inventory_not_found.
  3. checkAvailability(stock, quantity):
       - insufficient_stock -> insufficient_stock (409)
       - invalid_quantity   -> invalid_cart_quantity (400)
  4. cart = carts.findByCustomerId(customerId) or createCart(new id, now).
  5. domain addItem(cart, { productId, quantity, unitPrice: product.price,
     addedAt: now }):
       - cart_currency_mismatch -> 409
       - invalid_cart_quantity -> 400
  6. carts.save(updated) -> repository_error on throw.
  7. return cart.
Important: no reservation, no stock write. Carts are disposable promises.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
UC-08 removeItem
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Input:  { customerId, productId }
Output: Cart
Steps: cart = findByCustomerId; null -> cart_not_found; domain removeItem ->
       item_not_in_cart; save; return. Repository failures -> repository_error.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
UC-09 getCart
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Input:  { customerId }
Output: { cart, total: Money|null, itemCount }
Steps: findByCustomerId; null -> cart_not_found; derive total/itemCount with the
       domain functions (single definition of "cart total"); return.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
UC-10 createOrder
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Input:  { customerId }
Output: Order
Deps:   carts, orders, inventory, ids, clock, outbox, unitOfWork

Steps (single transaction):
  1. now = clock.now().
  2. cart = carts.findByCustomerId(customerId); null -> cart_not_found;
     empty -> empty_cart (409).
  3. map each CartItem to OrderItem via orderItemFromCartItem
     (failures -> invalid_order).
  4. createOrder({ id, customerId, items, createdAt: now })
     -> invalid_order (wraps empty_order/invalid_order_item).
  5. For each order line:
       stock = inventory.findByProductId(productId)
         null -> inventory_not_found
       reserveStock(stock, quantity, now)
         insufficient_stock -> insufficient_stock
         other -> invalid_order
       collect the updated inventory items.
  6. Save all reservations, then save the order, then save
     clearCart(cart, now), then stageEvent(orderCreated(order, now)).
  7. return ok(order).

Ordering matters: all validation and stock checks happen before the first
write, so the transaction does little work after it starts mutating.
Event and state commit together; the broker is never called here.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
UC-11 getOrder
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Input:  { id }
Output: Order
Steps: findById; null -> order_not_found; throw -> repository_error.
Authorization is the caller's job (controller checks ownership).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
UC-12 cancelOrder
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Input:  { orderId }
Output: Order
Deps:   orders, inventory, ids, clock, outbox, unitOfWork

Steps (one transaction):
  1. order = findById -> order_not_found.
  2. domain cancelOrder(order, now) -> order_already_paid / order_cancelled.
  3. For each line: load stock (null -> inventory_not_found), releaseStock
     (failure -> repository_error: order history and storage disagree; do not
     guess).
  4. Save releases, save cancelled order, stageEvent(orderCancelled).
  5. return cancelled order.
Used directly by POST /orders/:id/cancel and as checkout compensation.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
UC-13 processPayment
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Input:  { orderId }
Output: { order, payment }
Deps:   orders, inventory, gateway, ids, clock, outbox, unitOfWork
Errors include an optional `payment` field for the caller's diagnostics.

Steps:
  1. now = clock.now().
  2. order = findById -> order_not_found.
  3. domain payOrder(order, now) -> order_already_paid / order_cancelled.
     (Cheap pre-check so a paid order never reaches the provider.)
  4. createPayment({ id: ids.nextId(), orderId, amount: order.total,
     createdAt: now }) -> invalid_payment.
  5. charge = gateway.charge({ orderId, customerId, amount, idempotencyKey:
     payment.id }).
     - provider idempotency key is the LOCAL payment id; retrying the same
       charge returns the original reference.
     - thrown/network error -> failPayment(..., "Payment gateway did not
       respond") and `payment_gateway_unavailable` (502).
     - charge.ok === false:
         payment_declined -> failPayment(reason), error payment_declined (402)
         otherwise        -> failPayment(reason), error
                             payment_gateway_unavailable (502)
       Both include the failed payment in the error.
  6. succeedPayment(pending, providerReference, now).
  7. transaction (THE COMMIT STEP):
     a. reload order (must still exist) -> order_not_found;
     b. payOrder(current, now) again — protects against a concurrent payment;
     c. for each line: load stock, commitReservation (reserved -= qty);
        failure -> repository_error;
     d. save inventory, save paid order;
     e. stageEvent(orderPaid(order, providerReference, now));
     f. return ok(order).
  8. If step 7 fails after a successful charge: return
     `payment_requires_reconciliation` (500) with the succeeded payment and the
     cause. NEVER retry the charge automatically, never report success.
  9. return { order, payment: succeeded }.

Why the charge is outside the transaction: a database rollback cannot reverse a
card charge. The design makes that distributed boundary explicit.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
UC-14 checkout
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Input:  { customerId, idempotencyKey?: string }
Output: { order, payment, replayed: boolean }
Deps:   createOrder, processPayment, cancelOrder, idempotency, clock
Constants: RECORD_TTL = 24h, CLAIM_TTL = 30s,
           key = `checkout:<customerId>:<idempotencyKey>`

Steps:
  1. key = null when no idempotencyKey.
  2. If key:
     a. existing = idempotency.find(key):
          found -> return ok({ ...existing.result, replayed: true })
          (HTTP: 200 + `idempotent-replay: true`).
        Store error -> idempotency_unavailable (503) — FAIL CLOSED, because a
        retry could charge twice.
     b. acquire(key, 30s):
          "busy"   -> checkout_in_progress (409)
          "acquired"-> claimed = true
        Store error -> idempotency_unavailable (503).
  3. order = createOrder({ customerId }); failure -> return it.
  4. payment = processPayment({ orderId: order.id }).
     Failure -> await cancelOrder({ orderId }) as compensation (best effort,
     original error is returned even if compensation fails), then return the
     payment error.
  5. outcome = { order: payment.order, payment: payment.payment, replayed:false }.
  6. If key: idempotency.save(key, { result: {order, payment}, createdAt },
     24h). Save failure is swallowed (money already moved; a retry would create
     a second order) — the adapter logs it.
  7. finally: if claimed, idempotency.release(key). Release failure is
     swallowed; the claim expires by itself.
  8. return ok(outcome).

Guarantees:
  - Replay of a completed request returns the original order/payment.
  - Two simultaneous requests with the same NEW key: exactly one proceeds, the
    other gets 409; never two charges.
  - Declines are not stored: the claim is released so the customer may retry.
  - Keys are scoped per customer; two customers cannot collide.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
UC-15 dispatchOutbox
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Input:  none (optional batchSize, default 50)
Output: { published, failed, deadLettered }
Deps:   outbox, publisher, clock

Steps:
  1. now = clock.now(); pending = outbox.findPending(50, now).
     Read failure -> outbox_unavailable (500).
  2. For each message, in order:
     a. publish(message.event, { id: message.id }); then
        outbox.markPublished(message.id, clock.now()); published += 1.
        (Publish BEFORE mark: a crash may duplicate, never lose.)
     b. on publish failure: failed += 1; attempts = message.attempts + 1;
        deadLettered = attempts >= 5;
        nextAttemptAt = deadLettered ? now
                       : now + min(60s, 1s * 2^(attempts-1));
        outbox.markFailed(id, { error: message, nextAttemptAt,
          deadLetteredAt: deadLettered ? now : null }).
        If markFailed itself fails, the row stays pending with its old
        schedule — safe, it will be reconsidered.
  3. Return the summary. Dead letters are never retried automatically.
Backoff schedule: attempt 1 -> 1s, 2 -> 2s, 3 -> 4s, 4 -> 8s (cap 60s),
5 -> dead-lettered.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
UC-16 stageEvent (helper, not an HTTP use case)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Input:  (deps { outbox, ids, clock }, event)
Steps:  outbox.add({ id: ids.nextId(), event, createdAt: now, publishedAt:null,
        attempts:0, lastError:null, nextAttemptAt: now, deadLetteredAt:null }).
MUST be called inside the business transaction.

2.7 AUTHORIZATION POLICY (APPLICATION LAYER, PURE)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Principal = { customerId, email, role } derived from a verified token.

  canActForCustomer(principal, customerId) =
      principal.role === "admin" || principal.customerId === customerId

  canAccessOrder(principal, order) =
      canActForCustomer(principal, order.customerId)

  FORBIDDEN = { type: "forbidden",
                message: "You are not allowed to access this resource" }

Rules:
  - One refusal message for every case; it reveals neither existence nor owner.
  - Controllers call the policy; routes only enforce "authenticated" and
    "admin". Ownership is a business rule, not a route table.
  - Cross-customer access returns 403, not 404. (Documented trade-off: it
    confirms the resource exists, but keeps the rule and the message uniform.)

================================================================================
PART 3 — HTTP CONTRACT, PERSISTENCE, ADAPTERS, OPERATIONS, PORTING
================================================================================

3.1 HTTP CONVENTIONS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Base URL        http://localhost:3000 (PORT)
Content type    application/json for every request/response body
Auth            Authorization: Bearer <access token>
Idempotency     Idempotency-Key: <string, 1..200 chars> (checkout only)
Request id      x-request-id is honoured (1..128 chars) and always echoed;
                otherwise a UUID is generated
Errors          { "message": "..." } with status 4xx, or the generic 500 body

Headers set on every response:
  x-request-id, x-content-type-options: nosniff, x-frame-options: DENY,
  referrer-policy: no-referrer
  cache-control: no-store on /auth/*
  www-authenticate: Bearer realm="api" on 401
  x-ratelimit-limit / -remaining / -reset on rate-limited routes
  retry-after on 429
  idempotent-replay: true on a replayed checkout

Access rules:
  public   POST /auth/register, POST /auth/login, GET /products,
           GET /products/:id, GET /health, GET /ready, GET /metrics
  auth     everything else
  admin    POST /products, and any cross-customer access

3.2 ENDPOINT SPECIFICATIONS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
POST /auth/register                                            [public, 10/min]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Request:
  { "email": "owner@example.com",
    "name": "Owner",
    "password": "correct horse battery staple" }

Validation at the edge (strict object, unknown keys rejected):
  email     string 3..320 (real format checked by the domain)
  name      string 1..120
  password  string 1..1024 (policy checked by the domain)
  malformed -> 400 { "message": "Invalid request body" }

201 Created:
  { "customer": { "id": "...", "email": "owner@example.com",
                  "name": "Owner", "createdAt": "2026-..." },
    "role": "admin",
    "accessToken": { "token": "<jwt>", "tokenType": "Bearer",
                     "expiresAt": "2026-...Z" } }

Errors: 400 invalid_password | invalid_email | invalid_customer_name,
        409 email_already_registered, 429, 500.
Notes: role is "admin" only for BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_EMAILS; otherwise "customer".
       No password hash is ever returned.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
POST /auth/login                                               [public, 10/min]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Request:  { "email": "...", "password": "..." }   (strict; 3..320 / 1..1024)
200 OK:   same body as register.
Errors:   400 invalid request shape,
          401 invalid_credentials (unknown email, missing account, or bad
          password — identical message), 429, 500.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
GET /auth/me                                                   [auth]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
200 OK:
  { "customer": { "id", "email", "name", "createdAt" },
    "role": "customer" }
Errors: 401 (no/invalid token), 404 customer_not_found (deleted profile).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
GET /customers/:id                                             [auth, self|admin]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
200 OK:   { "id", "email", "name", "createdAt" }
Errors:   400 invalid customer id, 401, 403 forbidden, 404 customer_not_found.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
POST /products                                                 [auth, admin]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Request:
  { "name": "Mechanical keyboard",
    "price": { "amountInMinorUnits": 1999, "currency": "usd" },
    "availableQuantity": 5 }              // optional, default 0

Validation: name string; price object strict with integer >= 0 and string
currency (domain enforces > 0 and ISO shape); availableQuantity integer >= 0.
"usd" is accepted and normalised to "USD".

201 Created:
  { "id": "...", "name": "Mechanical keyboard",
    "price": { "amountInMinorUnits": 1999, "currency": "USD" },
    "createdAt": "2026-...Z" }

Errors: 400 invalid request body | invalid_product, 401, 403, 500.
Side effects: creates the InventoryItem in the same transaction.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
GET /products                                                  [public]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
200 OK:   [ ProductResponse, ... ]      (no pagination in v1)
Errors:   500 repository_error.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
GET /products/:id                                              [public]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
200 OK:   ProductResponse
Errors:   400 invalid product id, 404 product_not_found.
Notes:    read-through cache, 60 s TTL.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
GET /customers/:customerId/cart                                [auth, self|admin]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
200 OK:
  { "id": "...", "customerId": "...",
    "items": [ { "productId": "...", "quantity": 2,
                 "unitPrice": { "amountInMinorUnits": 1999,
                                "currency": "USD" } } ],
    "itemCount": 2,
    "total": { "amountInMinorUnits": 3998, "currency": "USD" },
    "updatedAt": "2026-...Z" }
  `total` is null for an empty cart.
Errors:   401, 403, 404 cart_not_found (a cart exists only after the first
          add), 500.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
POST /customers/:customerId/cart/items                         [auth, self|admin]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Request:  { "productId": "...", "quantity": 2 }     (strict, integer > 0)
200 OK:   updated CartResponse (same shape as GET cart).
Errors:   400 invalid request body | invalid_cart_quantity,
          401, 403, 404 product_not_found | inventory_not_found,
          409 insufficient_stock | cart_currency_mismatch,
          500 repository_error.
Notes:    no stock is reserved; quantity merges with an existing line and the
          merged total must stay <= 99.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
DELETE /customers/:customerId/cart/items/:productId            [auth, self|admin]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
200 OK:   updated CartResponse
Errors:   400, 401, 403, 404 cart_not_found, 409 item_not_in_cart, 500.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
POST /orders                                                   [auth, self|admin]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Request:  { "customerId": "..." }
201 Created:
  { "id": "...", "customerId": "...",
    "items": [ { "productId": "...", "quantity": 2,
                 "unitPrice": { "amountInMinorUnits": 1999, "currency": "USD" },
                 "lineTotal": { "amountInMinorUnits": 3998,
                                "currency": "USD" } } ],
    "total": { "amountInMinorUnits": 3998, "currency": "USD" },
    "status": "pending_payment",
    "createdAt": "...", "updatedAt": "...",
    "paidAt": null, "cancelledAt": null }
Errors:   400 invalid request body | invalid_order,
          401, 403, 404 cart_not_found | inventory_not_found,
          409 empty_cart | insufficient_stock, 500 transaction_failed.
Side effects: reserves stock, clears cart, stages order.created.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
GET /orders/:id                                                [auth, self|admin]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
200 OK:   OrderResponse
Errors:   400 invalid order id, 401, 403, 404 order_not_found.
Notes:    load-then-authorize: a non-owner learns 403, not 404.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
POST /orders/:id/cancel                                        [auth, self|admin]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
200 OK:   OrderResponse with status "cancelled", cancelledAt set.
Errors:   400, 401, 403, 404 order_not_found,
          409 order_already_paid | order_cancelled,
          500 inventory mismatch / transaction_failed.
Side effects: releases reserved stock, stages order.cancelled.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
POST /orders/:id/pay                                           [auth, self|admin]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Request:  no body.
200 OK:
  { "order": OrderResponse, "payment": PaymentResponse }
PaymentResponse:
  { "id", "orderId",
    "amount": { "amountInMinorUnits", "currency" },
    "status": "succeeded",
    "providerReference": "test_1",
    "failureReason": null,
    "createdAt", "updatedAt" }
Errors:   400, 401, 403, 404 order_not_found,
          402 payment_declined, 409 order_already_paid | order_cancelled,
          500 payment_requires_reconciliation | transaction_failed,
          502 payment_gateway_unavailable.
Side effects: commits reserved stock, marks order paid, stages order.paid.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
POST /checkout                                                 [auth, self|admin]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Headers:  Idempotency-Key: order-42   (optional; trimmed; 1..200 chars)
Request:  { "customerId": "..." }
201 Created (new):
  { "order": OrderResponse, "payment": PaymentResponse,
    "replayed": false }
200 OK (replay): same body with "replayed": true and header
                 idempotent-replay: true
Errors:   400 invalid request body / invalid Idempotency-Key,
          401, 403,
          404 cart_not_found | inventory_not_found | order_not_found,
          409 empty_cart | insufficient_stock | checkout_in_progress,
          402 payment_declined,
          500 payment_requires_reconciliation | transaction_failed,
          502 payment_gateway_unavailable,
          503 idempotency_unavailable (store unreachable: fail closed).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
GET /health                                                    [public, exempt]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
200 OK:  { "status": "ok", "uptimeSeconds": 123 }

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
GET /ready                                                     [public, exempt]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
200 OK / 503:
  { "status": "ok" | "degraded",
    "checks": { "postgres": "up" | "down",
                "redis": "up" | "down",
                "kafka": "up" | "down" } }
Only configured dependencies appear. Any "down" -> 503.
Probes: PostgreSQL SELECT 1, Redis PING, Kafka producer connect.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
GET /metrics                                                   [public, exempt]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
200 OK: Prometheus text exposition (registry.contentType).
Series: http_requests_total{method,route,status}
        http_request_duration_seconds{method,route,status} histogram
        checkout_total{outcome=completed|replayed|declined|in_progress|failed}
        outbox_messages_total{result=published|failed|dead_lettered}
        plus Node.js process/runtime defaults.

3.3 PERSISTENCE MODEL
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PostgreSQL 16. All timestamps `TIMESTAMPTZ(3)`. All ids `TEXT`. Enums are text
columns validated in the domain (closed sets above). Money is stored as an
integer plus a currency code. Line items are JSON snapshots.

  Customer
    id          TEXT      PK
    email       TEXT      NOT NULL, UNIQUE (index Customer_email_key)
    name        TEXT      NOT NULL
    createdAt   TIMESTAMPTZ(3) NOT NULL

  Account
    customerId   TEXT     PK, FK -> Customer(id) ON DELETE CASCADE
                          ON UPDATE CASCADE (Account_customerId_fkey)
    passwordHash TEXT     NOT NULL
    role         TEXT     NOT NULL DEFAULT 'customer'
    createdAt    TIMESTAMPTZ(3) NOT NULL
    updatedAt    TIMESTAMPTZ(3) NOT NULL

  Product
    id                 TEXT  PK
    name               TEXT  NOT NULL
    amountInMinorUnits INTEGER NOT NULL
    currency           TEXT  NOT NULL
    createdAt          TIMESTAMPTZ(3) NOT NULL

  InventoryItem
    id        TEXT PK
    productId TEXT NOT NULL, UNIQUE (InventoryItem_productId_key)
    available INTEGER NOT NULL
    reserved  INTEGER NOT NULL
    updatedAt TIMESTAMPTZ(3) NOT NULL

  Cart
    id         TEXT PK
    customerId TEXT NOT NULL, UNIQUE (Cart_customerId_key)
    items      JSONB NOT NULL        -- array of CartItem
    createdAt  TIMESTAMPTZ(3) NOT NULL
    updatedAt  TIMESTAMPTZ(3) NOT NULL

  "Order"                              -- quoted: reserved word
    id                 TEXT PK
    customerId         TEXT NOT NULL
    items              JSONB NOT NULL  -- array of OrderItem
    totalAmountInMinor INTEGER NOT NULL
    currency           TEXT NOT NULL
    status             TEXT NOT NULL
    createdAt          TIMESTAMPTZ(3) NOT NULL
    updatedAt          TIMESTAMPTZ(3) NOT NULL
    paidAt             TIMESTAMPTZ(3) NULL
    cancelledAt        TIMESTAMPTZ(3) NULL
    INDEX Order_customerId_idx (customerId)

  OutboxMessage
    id             TEXT PK
    eventName      TEXT NOT NULL
    occurredAt     TIMESTAMPTZ(3) NOT NULL
    payload        JSONB NOT NULL
    createdAt      TIMESTAMPTZ(3) NOT NULL
    publishedAt    TIMESTAMPTZ(3) NULL
    attempts       INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0
    lastError      TEXT NULL
    nextAttemptAt  TIMESTAMPTZ(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP
    deadLetteredAt TIMESTAMPTZ(3) NULL
    INDEX OutboxMessage_publishedAt_deadLetteredAt_nextAttemptAt_idx
          (publishedAt, deadLetteredAt, nextAttemptAt)

  ProcessedEvent                       -- durable inbox
    id          TEXT PK
    eventName   TEXT NOT NULL
    processedAt TIMESTAMPTZ(3) NOT NULL

Deliberate choices:
  - No foreign keys from Cart/Order to Customer: orders are historical records
    and must survive profile changes; the application enforces ownership.
  - Cart/order lines are JSON snapshots, not child tables, because they are
    immutable by design and never queried individually.
  - `Account` is separate from `Customer` so credentials can be rotated or
    removed without touching the profile, and so password hashes are not read
    on profile queries.
  - Enum-like columns are TEXT + domain validation, so adding a status is a
    code change, not a database type migration.

Mapping rules (implement in one mapper module per side):
  - Date/Time <-> ISO string: store absolute instants; the domain never sees a
    timezone-naive value.
  - Role read from storage: if not in the closed set, downgrade to "customer" —
    never guess upward.
  - JSON arrays are written as-is (they already match the domain shape).
  - Repository `save` is an upsert by primary key. Use a real UPSERT
    (`INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (id) DO UPDATE`) or ORM `merge`; do not do
    read-then-insert without handling the race.
  - ProcessedEvent insert: `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (id) DO NOTHING`, and the
    affected-rows count is the return value. A zero count means duplicate.

Transaction requirements:
  - Isolation: SERIALIZABLE for the multi-write use cases.
  - Retry: on PostgreSQL serialization failure (SQLSTATE 40001) / Prisma P2034,
    retry the whole transaction up to 3 attempts.
  - maxWait 5 s, timeout 15 s (reference values).
  - A business `err` MUST roll back. In ORMs that would commit a returned value,
    throw a private sentinel carrying the Result and catch it outside.
  - Repositories must join the ambient transaction; do not open a second one.

Indexes are exactly those listed above. Add no others without a measured need.

3.4 ADAPTER SPECIFICATIONS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

SCRYPT PASSWORD HASHER
  Stored format (single string, self-describing):
      scrypt$<N>$<r>$<p>$<salt base64url>$<key base64url>
  Defaults: N = 32768 (2^15), r = 8, p = 1, keyLength = 64 bytes,
            salt = 16 random bytes.
  maxmem = 256 * N * r bytes (Node needs headroom above 128 * N * r).
  Verification:
    - parse strictly: exactly 6 parts, first part "scrypt";
    - bound parameters: 1024 <= N <= 1048576 (integer), 1 <= r <= 32,
      1 <= p <= 16; salt >= 8 bytes; key 32..128 bytes; anything else -> false;
    - derive with the PARAMETERS FROM THE STORED HASH (old hashes stay valid);
    - compare with a constant-time comparison (`crypto.timingSafeEqual`);
    - when storedHash is null: derive using a fixed dummy salt and a cached
      dummy key, then return false — this spends one derivation so unknown
      accounts cannot be distinguished by timing.
  Tests lower N to 1024 through an option; production defaults are unchanged.
  Porting: any memory-hard KDF with embedded parameters is acceptable
  (Argon2id is a fine replacement), but keep `verify(null)` costing real work.

JWT TOKEN SERVICE
  Algorithm: HS256 only (pinned on verify).
  Claims: sub = customerId, email, role, iss, aud, iat, exp.
