  TTL: ACCESS_TOKEN_TTL_SECONDS (default 900).
  Verify: signature + issuer + audience + exp/nbf with 5 s clock tolerance;
          reject when sub/email/role are missing or role is not in the closed
          set; any failure -> null (never throw at the edge).
  Response: { token, expiresAt: ISO(exp * 1000) }.
  Secret: >= 32 chars; REQUIRED when NODE_ENV=production (config refuses to
  start otherwise). Development uses "development-only-secret-change-before-
  deploying" and logs "development-secret" at start-up.

REQUEST IDS + LOGGING
  Honour x-request-id (string, 1..128 chars) as the Fastify request id; else
  UUID v4. Echo it on every response. Log one structured error line for 5xx
  with the request id; never log passwords, hashes, tokens, or card data.

RATE LIMITING (two independent fixed windows)
  General: key `<client ip>:<route pattern>`, limit RATE_LIMIT_MAX (120) per
           RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_SECONDS (60). Exempt /health, /ready, /metrics.
           Emits x-ratelimit-limit/remaining/reset on all limited responses.
  Auth:    key `auth:<client ip>:<route pattern>`, limit
           AUTH_RATE_LIMIT_MAX (10) per AUTH_RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_SECONDS (60).
           Applied additionally to POST /auth/register and /auth/login.
  On exceed: 429, Retry-After = resetInSeconds, body
           { "message": "Too many requests" } or
           { "message": "Too many authentication attempts" }.
  Fail-open: if the counter store throws, log and allow the request.
  Route pattern (e.g. /orders/:id), never the raw URL, keeps cardinality bounded.

REDIS KEYS AND ALGORITHMS
  Cache:       key as supplied (`product:<id>`), JSON value, `SET ... EX ttl`.
               Corrupt JSON -> treat as a miss.
  Idempotency: record key `idempotency:record:<key>`,
               claim key  `idempotency:claim:<key>`.
               acquire: SET claim "1" EX ttl NX -> "OK" = acquired, else busy.
               save:    MULTI [SET record json EX ttl, DEL claim] EXEC.
               release: DEL claim.
  Rate limit:  key `ratelimit:<caller key>`; Lua:
                 local c = redis.call('INCR', KEYS[1])
                 if c == 1 then redis.call('EXPIRE', KEYS[1], ARGV[1]) end
                 local ttl = redis.call('TTL', KEYS[1])
                 return { c, ttl }
               allowed = count <= limit; remaining = max(0, limit - count);
               resetInSeconds = ttl > 0 ? ttl : windowSeconds.

IN-MEMORY ADAPTERS (defaults; also the test doubles)
  Store: one object of Maps keyed by aggregate (customers, accounts, products,
         inventory, carts, orders, outbox, inbox). Reads and writes deep-clone
         so callers cannot mutate stored state by reference.
  Unit of work: serialise executions through a promise queue; take a deep
         snapshot before the work; restore the snapshot when the work returns
         err or throws. This is the behavioural contract every other adapter
         must match.
  Cache/idempotency/rate limiter: TTL maps with lazy expiry; `acquire` checks
         and writes without yielding, which is atomic in a single-threaded
         runtime and matches Redis SET NX semantics.
  Inbox: a Set; markProcessed returns false when the id was already present.

OUTBOX WORKER
  Interval poll (OUTBOX_POLL_INTERVAL_MS, default 1000, min 100). Only one
  dispatch runs at a time: if a batch is in flight, the tick is skipped.
  stop() clears the interval and awaits the in-flight batch. runOnce() exists
  for tests and shutdown flushing. A dispatch error is logged, never fatal.

KAFKA
  Topics:
      order.created, order.paid, order.cancelled -> ecommerce.orders.v1
      customer.registered                        -> ecommerce.customers.v1
      anything else                              -> ecommerce.events.v1
  All three are created at start-up (idempotent); failure is logged and
  auto-create is the fallback.
  Partition key: payload.orderId, else payload.customerId, else null.
  Message body: { id, name, occurredAt, payload } as UTF-8 JSON.
  Headers: x-event-id = outbox id, x-event-name, x-event-version = "1".
  Producer: idempotent, retries 5, connect before first publish.
  Consumer: group KAFKA_CONSUMER_GROUP, topics [orders, customers],
    fromBeginning false, allowAutoTopicCreation true.
    For each message: parse JSON; id = header x-event-id (fallback body id);
    inbox.markProcessed({ id, eventName, processedAt: now ISO });
    duplicate -> acknowledge and skip; otherwise run the handler (default:
    one structured log line). Inbox failure must surface as a consumer error
    so the offset is not committed blindly.
  Porting note: persist the inbox row in the same transaction as the
  projection when a real projection is added.

METRICS
  Registry per process, default Node metrics collected.
  HTTP observation uses the route pattern (never raw URL) and reply elapsed
  time. Checkout outcomes are recorded by the composition root wrapper:
  success -> completed/replayed; error payment_declined -> declined;
  checkout_in_progress -> in_progress; anything else -> failed.
  Outbox summary increments only non-zero counters.

3.5 CROSS-CUTTING BEHAVIOUR
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

CONFIGURATION (parsed once, at the edge; nothing else reads env)
  Variable                          Default                  Notes
  --------------------------------  -----------------------  -----------------
  PORT                              3000                     1..65535
  NODE_ENV                          development              development|test|production
  DATABASE_URL                      unset                    unset -> memory
  REDIS_URL                         unset                    unset -> memory
  KAFKA_BROKERS                     unset                    CSV; unset -> stdout
  KAFKA_CLIENT_ID                   ecommerce-backend        non-empty
  KAFKA_CONSUMER_GROUP              ecommerce-order-events   non-empty
  RATE_LIMIT_MAX                    120                      > 0
  RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_SECONDS         60                       > 0
  JWT_SECRET                        dev fallback             >= 32; required in production
  JWT_ISSUER                        ecommerce-backend        verified claim
  JWT_AUDIENCE                      ecommerce-api            verified claim
  ACCESS_TOKEN_TTL_SECONDS          900                      60..86400
  AUTH_RATE_LIMIT_MAX               10                       > 0
  AUTH_RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_SECONDS    60                       > 0
  BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_EMAILS            empty                    CSV, lowercased
  OUTBOX_POLL_INTERVAL_MS           1000                     >= 100

  Empty strings are treated as unset (compose passes empty values for optional
  services). Invalid configuration prints one message listing every problem and
  exits with code 1. A loaded `.env` file is read only when present.

SECURITY BEHAVIOUR
  - Password hashes and plaintext passwords never leave the process; no
    response type contains them.
  - Enumeration resistance: identical 401 body for unknown email / missing
    account / wrong password, plus the dummy-derivation cost.
  - Ownership failures share one 403 message.
  - 5xx bodies are generic; details are logged server-side once.
  - Auth endpoints are no-store and rate limited harder.
  - No customer-creation endpoint exists.
  - Role changes take effect at the next login (the role is a token claim) —
    document this; do not try to re-read the account on every request.

START-UP AND SHUTDOWN
  Start: load config -> build adapters (fail fast) -> build use cases ->
  log selected adapters -> start Kafka topics/consumer (non-fatal) -> start
  outbox worker -> listen on 0.0.0.0:PORT.
  Shutdown on SIGINT/SIGTERM: stop worker -> close HTTP -> close adapters in
  reverse acquisition order (Kafka consumer+producer, Redis, Prisma).
  Every close is best-effort; errors are logged, remaining steps still run.

3.6 TESTING STRATEGY
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Principle: the domain and use cases are tested without infrastructure; the
adapters are tested against real services when their URIs are provided.

Test environment helper (`tests/support/test-environment.ts`):
  - builds the real in-memory adapters via buildInMemoryDependencies();
  - fixed clock (2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z) and sequential ids (id-1, id-2, ...);
  - captured EventPublisher (records published events; nothing sent);
  - in-memory gateway with optional decline;
  - real scrypt hasher with N=1024 and a real JWT service;
  - registerTestCustomer() goes through the real register use case;
  - drainOutbox() runs the dispatcher synchronously;
  - createTestHttpApp() builds the real Fastify app with logger off.

Suites and what they must prove:
  domain/account              password floor/ceiling, hash required, role set,
                              role change keeps credential
  domain/cart                 merge, 99 cap, currency mismatch, remove missing,
                              totals and item count
  domain/inventory            reserve/release/commit/restock arithmetic,
                              over-reserve refusal, quantity validation
  domain/inventory-availability  checkAvailability is pure and non-mutating
  domain/order                total derivation, empty order, pay/cancel
                              transitions and their refusals
  application/auth            registration transaction, duplicate refusal,
                              hashed-before-transaction, login success/failure,
                              unknown account costs a verification, token claims
  application/checkout        success path (paid + committed + cleared cart),
                              decline compensation (cancelled + released),
                              stock conflict, order.paid staged once
  application/checkout-idempotency  replay, per-customer scoping, no duplicate
                              events, decline not stored
  application/checkout-concurrency  two simultaneous same-key requests -> one
                              winner, loser 409, claim released on failure
  application/outbox          published once, payload broker-agnostic,
                              backoff schedule, dead letter after 5
  application/outbox-backoff  delay table and ceiling
  infrastructure/idempotency-store  claim semantics, TTL, save releases claim
  infrastructure/inbox-store  first delivery wins, duplicate reported
  infrastructure/kafka-messages  topic routing, partition key, headers, body
  infrastructure/rate-limiter fixed window, reset, remaining
  infrastructure/unit-of-work commit, err rollback, thrown rollback, rollback
                              observed through a real use case
  e2e/http/checkout-flow      real routes via inject: 201/200/409/404/400,
                              replay header, ownership 401/403
  e2e/http/auth-flow          token round trip, admin-only product write,
                              customer cannot read another cart/order
  e2e/http/platform-endpoints readiness 200/503, metrics, request-id echo,
                              429 + Retry-After
  integration/prisma          migrations present, real transactions including
                              registration, JSON snapshots, rollback,
                              backoff/DLQ, inbox conflict
  integration/redis           cache TTL, SET NX claim, shared limiter
  architecture/dependency-rules  inner layers import no frameworks/node builtins,
                              no env/clock/uuid access, no infrastructure->main

Gating:
  integration suites skip unless DATABASE_TEST_URL / REDIS_TEST_URL are set.
  Run them against disposable databases; migrations must be applied first.

Recommended test commands:
  unit + e2e + architecture: npm test
  typecheck:                 npm run typecheck
  lint:                      npm run lint
  format:                    npm run format:check
  build:                     npm run build
  integration:               DATABASE_TEST_URL=... REDIS_TEST_URL=... npm test

3.7 OPERATIONS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PACKAGE SCRIPTS
  dev            tsx watch src/main/server.ts
  build          clean dist, then tsc -p tsconfig.build.json
  start          node dist/main/server.js
  test           vitest run
  test:watch     vitest
  db:migrate     prisma migrate deploy
  db:migrate:dev prisma migrate dev
  db:generate    prisma generate
  admin:grant    tsx src/main/grant-admin.ts <email>
  typecheck      tsc --noEmit
  lint           eslint .
  format         prettier --write .
  format:check   prettier --check .

CONTAINER IMAGE (three stages)
  deps     node:22-alpine, copy package*.json + prisma, `npm ci`
           (postinstall runs prisma generate)
  build    copy tsconfigs + src, `npm run build`
  runtime  node:22-alpine, NODE_ENV=production, production deps only,
           dist + prisma, USER node, EXPOSE 3000,
           HEALTHCHECK with Node fetch against /health,
           CMD `npx prisma migrate deploy && node dist/main/server.js`.
  Migration deploy is idempotent, so a fresh volume converges on the schema in
  the image. The image contains no tests, no toolchain, and no curl.

COMPOSE (local full stack)
  postgres  postgres:16-alpine, app/app/ecommerce, port 5432, volume,
            pg_isready healthcheck
  redis     redis:7-alpine with AOF, port 6379, volume, redis-cli ping
  kafka     apache/kafka:3.9.0 KRaft single node, external listener 29092,
            3 partitions, auto-create on, topic-list healthcheck
  prometheus prom/prometheus:v2.55.1, scrapes app:3000/metrics every 15 s,
            UI 9090, depends on app
  app       built from Dockerfile, waits for healthy postgres/redis/kafka,
            production env, JWT_SECRET from the environment with a local
            default, BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_EMAILS default owner@example.com,
            port 3000, restart unless-stopped
  volumes   postgres-data, redis-data

CI (GitHub Actions, working directory ecommerce-backend)
  Trigger: push to main, every pull request.
  Service: postgres:16-alpine with DATABASE_URL and DATABASE_TEST_URL pointing
  at ecommerce_test, so integration tests run for real on every push.
  Steps: checkout -> setup node 22 with npm cache -> npm ci ->
         npm run db:migrate -> format:check -> lint -> typecheck -> test ->
         build -> docker build.

DATABASE MIGRATIONS
  Two migrations, applied in order:
    20261125120537_init        customer, product, inventory, cart, order,
                               outbox, processed event + indexes
    20261125143000_add_account account table + cascade FK
  Never edit an applied migration; add a new one. `prisma migrate deploy` in
  the container is the only automatic application of migrations.

OPERATOR COMMAND
  npm run admin:grant -- ada@example.com
  Requires DATABASE_URL. Looks up the customer by lowercased email, requires an
  existing credential, assigns role "admin" with a fresh updatedAt, saves.
  Prints the email and customer id, and warns that existing tokens keep their
  old role until expiry. Never creates accounts; never runs without a database.

3.8 PORTING GUIDE — PYTHON (FastAPI)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Suggested stack:
  HTTP            FastAPI + Uvicorn, Pydantic v2 models for edge validation
  Persistence     SQLAlchemy 2.0 (async) + asyncpg, Alembic migrations
  Transactions    AsyncSession + `async with session.begin()`, or a UnitOfWork
                  class; isolation_level="SERIALIZABLE"; retry on
                  SerializationFailure (SQLSTATE 40001)
  Redis           redis.asyncio
  Kafka           aiokafka (producer + consumer)
  Passwords       argon2-cffi (Argon2id) or hashlib.scrypt with the same
                  embedded-parameter format
  JWT             PyJWT (HS256, verify iss/aud/exp, algorithms pinned)
  Metrics         prometheus_client
  Tests           pytest + pytest-asyncio + httpx.AsyncClient(ASGITransport)

Layout (mirror the layers, not the file names):
  app/domain/shared/{result.py, errors.py, events.py}
  app/domain/{customer,product,inventory,cart,order,payment,auth}/
  app/application/ports/            (Protocol classes)
  app/application/{auth,customer,product,cart,order,payment,checkout,outbox}/
  app/infrastructure/{config,auth,http,persistence,redis,kafka,outbox,
                      observability,payments,system,transactions}/
  app/main/{container.py,server.py,grant_admin.py}
  tests/{unit,integration,e2e,architecture,support}/

Mapping notes:
  - Result: a frozen dataclass `Ok(value)` / `Err(error)` or a `Result` union;
    use `match` on error type in controllers. Do not raise for expected errors.
  - Domain models: frozen dataclasses; Money as `(amount_minor: int,
    currency: str)`; ISO strings via `datetime` + `.isoformat()` only in mappers.
  - Ports: `typing.Protocol` with async methods; repositories take the session
    from a contextvar set by the UnitOfWork (same trick as AsyncLocalStorage).
  - UoW: async context manager that yields a session and rolls back when the
    returned Result is an Err. A returned Err must trigger `rollback()`
    explicitly — Python will happily commit otherwise.
  - FastAPI: one router per resource; dependencies for `principal` and guards;
    exception handler mapping application error `type` to HTTP status using the
    same ordered rule (explicit table -> `_not_found` -> `invalid_` -> 500).
  - Idempotency: `redis.set(claim_key, "1", nx=True, ex=30)`; save via
    pipeline (SET record EX ttl, DEL claim); release via DEL.
  - Rate limit: register the Lua script with `client.register_script`, same
    INCR/EXPIRE/TTL body.
  - Outbox worker: asyncio task with a sleep loop, single in-flight guard,
    cancel and await on shutdown; `asyncio.Event` for stop.
  - Kafka: aiokafka producer with `enable_idempotence=True`; consumer with
    `enable_auto_commit=False` and manual commit after inbox handling.
  - Pydantic edge schemas: `model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid")` to match
    Zod `.strict()`.
  - Health: `/health` plain; `/ready` returns 503 on any failed probe; metrics
    via `generate_latest()` with the correct content type.
  - Alembic: two revisions matching the two SQL migrations; do not let
    autogenerate invent a different schema (especially `TIMESTAMPTZ(3)` and
    JSONB columns).
  - Watch out: Python's `bool` is an `int`; reject booleans where integers are
    required. Enforce the 12..128 password bounds in the domain, not the edge.

3.9 PORTING GUIDE — JAVA (SPRING BOOT)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Suggested stack:
  HTTP            Spring Boot 3 Web (spring-boot-starter-web), Jackson
  Validation      Jakarta Bean Validation at the edge; domain rules in plain
                  Java records/classes
  Persistence     Spring Data JDBC or JPA (Hibernate) + Flyway migrations
  Transactions    @Transactional(isolation = SERIALIZABLE) on the use-case
                  service method; retry on `CannotSerializeTransactionException`
                  / SQLState 40001 with a small loop (3 attempts)
  Redis           Spring Data Redis (Lettuce); DefaultRedisScript for the Lua
                  limiter; `setIfAbsent(key, "1", Duration)` for claims
  Kafka           spring-kafka (DefaultKafkaProducerFactory, @KafkaListener)
  Passwords       Spring Security Crypto BCrypt/Argon2, or Bouncy Castle scrypt
  JWT             Spring Security OAuth2 Resource Server + Nimbus JOSE
  Metrics         Micrometer + Actuator Prometheus registry
  Tests           JUnit 5, AssertJ, MockMvc/WebTestClient, Testcontainers

Layout (packages):
  com.example.shop.domain.{shared,customer,product,inventory,cart,order,
                            payment,auth}
  com.example.shop.application.ports
  com.example.shop.application.{auth,customer,product,cart,order,payment,
                                checkout,outbox}
  com.example.shop.infrastructure.{config,auth,http,persistence,redis,kafka,
                                   outbox,observability,payments,system,
                                   transactions}
  com.example.shop.main (Spring Boot application + AdminGrantRunner)

Mapping notes:
  - Result: `sealed interface Result<T,E>` with records `Ok<T,E>` and
    `Err<T,E>`; use pattern matching switches. Do not use exceptions for
    declines/validation.
  - Domain: immutable records; `Money(BigInteger amountMinor, String currency)`
    with compact-constructor validation. `Order` transitions return
    `Result<Order, OrderError>` rather than mutating.
  - Ports: plain interfaces in `application.ports`; adapters annotated
    `@Repository`/`@Component`. Keep controllers free of JPA entities.
  - UnitOfWork: a `TransactionTemplate` with SERIALIZABLE; run the work and if
    the Result is Err throw a private `RollbackSignal` caught outside the
    template (Spring rolls back on RuntimeException). Repositories must join
    the current transaction (`Propagation.MANDATORY`); never open a new one.
  - Ambient transaction client: Spring's transaction-bound EntityManager is
    already thread-bound; if using a custom client, use a ThreadLocal.
  - JSON snapshot columns: map with `@JdbcTypeCode(SqlTypes.JSON)` +
    `columnDefinition = "jsonb"`, or store as String and convert in mappers.
  - Edge validation: `@Valid` DTO records with `@NotBlank`, `@Size`,
    `@Positive`; use `@JsonIgnoreProperties(ignoreUnknown = false)`-style
    strictness (Jackson `FAIL_ON_UNKNOWN_PROPERTIES`) to match Zod strict.
  - Guards: a `OncePerRequestFilter` that parses the bearer token and sets a
    request-scoped Principal; method-level checks via a small `AccessPolicy`
    service. Ownership stays a business rule, not `@PreAuthorize` on paths.
  - Error mapping: `@RestControllerAdvice` with the same ordered rule.
  - Idempotency: Redis `setIfAbsent(claimKey, "1", Duration.ofSeconds(30))`;
    save in a `SessionCallback` MULTI (SET record EX ttl + DEL claim).
  - Rate limiting: a `HandlerInterceptor` or servlet filter before controllers,
    keyed by client IP + route pattern; fail-open on Redis errors.
  - Outbox worker: `@Scheduled(fixedDelayString = "${OUTBOX_POLL_INTERVAL_MS}")`
    with an `AtomicBoolean` in-flight guard; `@PreDestroy` stops it.
  - Kafka: `KafkaTemplate` with `acks=all`, `enable.idempotence=true`;
    `@KafkaListener` with manual ack after the inbox insert; duplicate ->
    acknowledge without handling.
  - Metrics: Micrometer counters `checkout_total{outcome}`,
    `outbox_messages_total{result}`, `http_requests_total` via a filter using
    the route pattern; expose `/actuator/prometheus` or a custom `/metrics`.
  - Flyway: `V1__init.sql`, `V2__add_account.sql` mirroring the reference SQL.
    Use `TIMESTAMPTZ(3)`? PostgreSQL accepts `timestamptz(3)`; JPA maps
    `OffsetDateTime`/`Instant`.
  - Watch out: Java's `int` overflows around 2.1 billion minor units; use
    `long`/`BigInteger` and a `BIGINT` column if you change the schema (the
    reference uses INTEGER, which is fine for this exercise but worth noting).

3.10 BUILD ORDER (IMPLEMENT AND VERIFY IN THIS SEQUENCE)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  1. Project skeleton, strict compiler settings, test runner, linter.
     Verify: an empty test suite and a typecheck/build pass.
  2. domain/shared (Result, DomainError, DomainEvent) + unit tests for Result.
  3. Money, Customer, Product, InventoryItem + their rules and tests.
     Verify: all boundary cases (0, negative, 99, 100, currency mismatch).
  4. Cart and Order aggregates with transitions + tests.
  5. Payment and Account/Role + tests.
  6. Ports (interfaces only) and the in-memory store + repositories.
  7. UnitOfWork (in-memory) and its rollback tests.
  8. Use cases in this order: getCustomer, listProducts, createProduct,
     getProduct (cache), addItem, removeItem, getCart, createOrder, getOrder,
     cancelOrder, processPayment, register, login, checkout, dispatchOutbox.
     After each: use-case tests with the in-memory adapters.
  9. In-memory rate limiter, idempotency store, inbox store, payment gateway,
     console publisher + tests.
 10. HTTP edge: error mapping, auth guards, schemas, presenters, controllers,
     routes, app builder. Wire the composition root and start the server.
     Verify: e2e tests for checkout/auth/platform endpoints via inject.
 11. Auth adapters (scrypt, JWT) and the full auth flow.
     Verify: 401/403 matrix, no hash in any response, token claims.
 12. PostgreSQL: schema, migrations, mappers, repositories, inbox, serializable
     unit of work, health check. Verify with a real database:
     migrations apply, `migrate diff` reports no drift, integration tests pass.
 13. Redis adapters; verify TTL, NX claim, shared windows.
 14. Kafka producer/topics/consumer + inbox wiring; verify with a real broker
     (produce, consume, duplicate delivery is skipped).
 15. Outbox worker + metrics + readiness + graceful shutdown.
 16. Container, compose, Prometheus scrape config, CI, README, env template.
 17. Final gate: format, lint, typecheck, tests, build, image build, live smoke
     (register -> product -> cart -> checkout -> login -> forbidden checks).

3.11 ACCEPTANCE CHECKLIST
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Identity
  [ ] Only POST /auth/register can create a customer.
  [ ] Password < 12 or > 128 rejected with invalid_password.
  [ ] Unknown email, missing account, wrong password all -> 401 with the same
      message; unknown email still spends one KDF.
  [ ] Registration commits profile + credential + event atomically; a failure
      produces no customer and no token.
  [ ] Token verifies iss/aud/alg/exp; tampered/expired tokens -> 401.
  [ ] Role is a closed set; corrupted stored role downgrades to customer.
  [ ] Non-admin cannot create products; non-owner cannot read/modify a cart or
      order; admins can act for anyone.

Catalogue and inventory
  [ ] Product name trimmed, 1..160; price > 0; currency normalised to 3 upper.
  [ ] Creating a product also creates its stock row in one transaction.
  [ ] available/reserved never go negative; reserve/release/commit/restock
      arithmetic matches the rules; checkAvailability mutates nothing.

Cart
  [ ] One cart per customer; adding merges and caps at 99.
  [ ] Mixed currencies rejected with cart_currency_mismatch.
  [ ] Cart total is null when empty, otherwise sum(price*qty).
  [ ] Adding to a cart never writes stock.

Orders and payment
  [ ] Order total is derived, never accepted from the client.
  [ ] Placing an order reserves stock, snapshots prices, clears the cart, and
      stages order.created in one transaction.
  [ ] Empty cart -> 409; insufficient stock -> 409 and no partial reservation.
  [ ] paid/cancelled transitions follow the state machine exactly.
  [ ] Cancelling releases reserved stock in the same transaction.
  [ ] Charge happens outside the DB transaction with a provider idempotency key.
  [ ] Decline -> 402 and compensating cancel (stock released).
  [ ] Charge succeeded but commit failed -> 500 payment_requires_reconciliation,
      never a silent retry.

Checkout
  [ ] Same key replays the stored result: 200, replayed true, replay header.
  [ ] Same new key concurrently: one 201, one 409, exactly one charge.
  [ ] Keys are scoped per customer.
  [ ] Declines are not stored; retry is allowed.
  [ ] Idempotency store failure -> 503, no charge attempted.

Events
  [ ] Outbox row is written in the same transaction as the state change.
  [ ] Publish happens before marking; crash can duplicate, never lose.
  [ ] Backoff: 1s, 2s, 4s, 8s (cap 60s); dead letter after 5 attempts.
  [ ] Dead-lettered rows are never picked up again.
  [ ] Kafka body/headers/topic/partition key match the spec.
  [ ] Consumer claims x-event-id; duplicate delivery does not run the handler.

Operations
  [ ] /health 200; /ready 200 only when all configured dependencies are up,
      otherwise 503 with a per-dependency report.
  [ ] /metrics exposes the four custom series with bounded labels.
  [ ] x-request-id echoes a provided id, generates one otherwise.
  [ ] Rate limits: 429 + Retry-After, correct headers, exemptions, fail-open.
  [ ] 5xx bodies are generic; real causes are logged once.
  [ ] Graceful shutdown stops worker, HTTP, then adapters.
  [ ] Configuration errors prevent start-up with a clear message; production
      refuses to start without a >= 32-char JWT_SECRET.

Persistence
  [ ] Migrations apply cleanly to an empty database; schema diff is empty.
  [ ] Save is an upsert; one cart per customer and one stock row per product
      are enforced by unique indexes.
  [ ] Serializable transactions retry write conflicts up to 3 times.
  [ ] A business err rolls back every write, including the outbox row.

3.12 KNOWN LIMITATIONS (document these; do not silently fix by guessing)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Dead letters have no admin/replay endpoint; replay is manual SQL.
  - Inbox rows are never pruned; add retention later.
  - No reconciliation job for payment_requires_reconciliation.
  - Payment gateway is an in-memory stub; real provider webhooks are future
    work.
  - No refresh tokens, revocation, logout, email verification, password reset,
    or MFA; role changes lag one login.
  - HS256 shared secret; multi-service deployments should use asymmetric keys
    with JWKS.
  - Login throttling is per IP + route; distributed credential stuffing needs
    a per-account counter.
  - /metrics is unauthenticated; keep it on the internal network.
  - Single PostgreSQL and single Kafka broker in compose; not production
    durability.
  - No pagination on GET /products; no order listing endpoint.
  - Products are immutable apart from creation, so the 60 s cache needs no
    invalidation yet.
  - Cross-customer access returns 403 (existence is effectively confirmed).

Binary/asset conventions: none. The service has no file uploads, no static
assets, no background media processing.

PORT=3000
# development | test | production
NODE_ENV=development

# PostgreSQL + Prisma. Leave DATABASE_URL unset (or empty) to use the in-memory
# adapters. Run migrations with `npm run db:migrate` (or `npm run db:migrate:dev`).
# DATABASE_URL=postgresql://app:app@127.0.0.1:5432/ecommerce?schema=public

# Cache, idempotency, and shared rate limiting. Unset uses in-process adapters.
# REDIS_URL=redis://127.0.0.1:6379

# Outbox delivery to Kafka plus the idempotent consumer. Unset publishes to stdout.
# KAFKA_BROKERS=127.0.0.1:29092
KAFKA_CLIENT_ID=ecommerce-backend
KAFKA_CONSUMER_GROUP=ecommerce-order-events

# JWT signing. Required in production (at least 32 characters); development
# falls back to a clearly-marked local secret. Generate one with:
#   node -e "console.log(require('node:crypto').randomBytes(48).toString('base64url'))"
# JWT_SECRET=change-me-to-a-random-48-byte-secret
JWT_ISSUER=ecommerce-backend
JWT_AUDIENCE=ecommerce-api
ACCESS_TOKEN_TTL_SECONDS=900

# First administrator: this address gets the admin role when it registers.
# Remove it after the account exists; `npm run admin:grant` handles later ones.
# BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_EMAILS=owner@example.com

# Fixed-window rate limit per caller and route.
RATE_LIMIT_MAX=120
RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_SECONDS=60

# Stricter budget for /auth/register and /auth/login (credential stuffing).
AUTH_RATE_LIMIT_MAX=10
AUTH_RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_SECONDS=60

# How often the outbox worker drains a batch.
OUTBOX_POLL_INTERVAL_MS=1000

# Later adapters read their own settings here:
# STRIPE_SECRET_KEY=sk_test_...

name: CI

on:
  push:
    branches: [main]
  pull_request:

jobs:
  verify:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    defaults:
      run:
        working-directory: ecommerce-backend

    # The integration suite runs against a real PostgreSQL, not a mock, so the
    # transaction, backoff, and inbox SQL is exercised on every push.
    services:
      postgres:
        image: postgres:16-alpine
        env:
          POSTGRES_USER: app
          POSTGRES_PASSWORD: app
          POSTGRES_DB: ecommerce_test
        ports:
          - 5432:5432
        options: >-
          --health-cmd "pg_isready -U app"
          --health-interval 5s
          --health-timeout 5s
          --health-retries 20

    env:
      DATABASE_URL: postgresql://app:app@127.0.0.1:5432/ecommerce_test?schema=public
      DATABASE_TEST_URL: postgresql://app:app@127.0.0.1:5432/ecommerce_test?schema=public

    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with:
          node-version: 22
          cache: npm
          cache-dependency-path: ecommerce-backend/package-lock.json

      - run: npm ci

      - run: npm run db:migrate

      - run: npm run format:check
      - run: npm run lint
      - run: npm run typecheck
      - run: npm test
      - run: npm run build

      - name: Build the production image
        run: docker build -t ecommerce-backend:ci .

name: ecommerce-backend

services:
  postgres:
    image: postgres:16-alpine
    environment:
      POSTGRES_USER: app
      POSTGRES_PASSWORD: app
      POSTGRES_DB: ecommerce
    ports:
      - "5432:5432"
    volumes:
      - postgres-data:/var/lib/postgresql/data
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD-SHELL", "pg_isready -U app -d ecommerce"]
      interval: 5s
      timeout: 5s
      retries: 20

  redis:
    image: redis:7-alpine
    command: ["redis-server", "--appendonly", "yes"]
    ports:
      - "6379:6379"
    volumes:
      - redis-data:/data
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "redis-cli", "ping"]
      interval: 5s
      timeout: 3s
      retries: 20

  kafka:
    image: apache/kafka:3.9.0
    ports:
      - "29092:29092"
    environment:
      KAFKA_NODE_ID: 1
      KAFKA_PROCESS_ROLES: broker,controller
      KAFKA_LISTENERS: PLAINTEXT://:9092,CONTROLLER://:9093,EXTERNAL://:29092
      KAFKA_ADVERTISED_LISTENERS: PLAINTEXT://kafka:9092,EXTERNAL://localhost:29092
      KAFKA_LISTENER_SECURITY_PROTOCOL_MAP: CONTROLLER:PLAINTEXT,PLAINTEXT:PLAINTEXT,EXTERNAL:PLAINTEXT
      KAFKA_CONTROLLER_LISTENER_NAMES: CONTROLLER
      KAFKA_CONTROLLER_QUORUM_VOTERS: 1@kafka:9093
      KAFKA_INTER_BROKER_LISTENER_NAME: PLAINTEXT
      KAFKA_OFFSETS_TOPIC_REPLICATION_FACTOR: 1
      KAFKA_TRANSACTION_STATE_LOG_REPLICATION_FACTOR: 1
      KAFKA_TRANSACTION_STATE_LOG_MIN_ISR: 1
      KAFKA_GROUP_INITIAL_REBALANCE_DELAY_MS: 0
      KAFKA_NUM_PARTITIONS: 3
      KAFKA_AUTO_CREATE_TOPICS_ENABLE: "true"
    healthcheck:
      test:
        [
          "CMD-SHELL",
          "/opt/kafka/bin/kafka-topics.sh --bootstrap-server localhost:9092 --list >/dev/null 2>&1",
        ]
      interval: 10s
      timeout: 10s
      retries: 20

  prometheus:
    image: prom/prometheus:v2.55.1
    volumes:
      - ./docker/prometheus/prometheus.yml:/etc/prometheus/prometheus.yml:ro
    ports:
      - "9090:9090"
    depends_on:
      - app

  app:
    build:
      context: .
    depends_on:
      postgres:
        condition: service_healthy
      redis:
        condition: service_healthy
      kafka:
        condition: service_healthy

      NODE_ENV: production
      PORT: "3000"
      DATABASE_URL: postgresql://app:app@postgres:5432/ecommerce?schema=public
      REDIS_URL: redis://redis:6379
      KAFKA_BROKERS: kafka:9092
      KAFKA_CLIENT_ID: ecommerce-backend
      KAFKA_CONSUMER_GROUP: ecommerce-order-events
      RATE_LIMIT_MAX: "120"
      RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_SECONDS: "60"
      AUTH_RATE_LIMIT_MAX: "10"
      AUTH_RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_SECONDS: "60"
      # Local compose only. A real deployment must inject a random secret.
      JWT_SECRET: ${JWT_SECRET:-local-compose-secret-change-me-32-chars}
      JWT_ISSUER: ecommerce-backend
      JWT_AUDIENCE: ecommerce-api
      ACCESS_TOKEN_TTL_SECONDS: "900"
      BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_EMAILS: ${BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_EMAILS:-owner@example.com}
      OUTBOX_POLL_INTERVAL_MS: "1000"
    ports:

FILE: ecommerce-backend/Dockerfile
39 lines | 1,370 bytes
================================================================================
# syntax=docker/dockerfile:1

# 1. Install every dependency (including dev) for the build. The Prisma schema
#    is copied first because `postinstall` runs `prisma generate`.
FROM node:22-alpine AS deps
WORKDIR /app
COPY package.json package-lock.json ./
COPY prisma ./prisma
RUN npm ci --no-audit --no-fund

# 2. Compile TypeScript. Tests and vitest config are excluded by
#    tsconfig.build.json, so the image never contains them.
FROM deps AS build
COPY tsconfig.json tsconfig.build.json ./
COPY src ./src
RUN npm run build

# 3. Runtime: production dependencies only, non-root, no toolchain.
#    `prisma` is a production dependency so migrations can run here.
FROM node:22-alpine AS runtime
ENV NODE_ENV=production
WORKDIR /app

COPY package.json package-lock.json ./
COPY prisma ./prisma
RUN npm ci --omit=dev --no-audit --no-fund \
  && npm cache clean --force

COPY --from=build /app/dist ./dist

USER node
EXPOSE 3000

# Uses Node's own fetch, so the image needs no curl or wget.
HEALTHCHECK --interval=10s --timeout=3s --start-period=10s --retries=5 \
  CMD node -e "fetch('http://127.0.0.1:' + (process.env.PORT || 3000) + '/health').then((r) => process.exit(r.ok ? 0 : 1)).catch(() => process.exit(1))"

# `migrate deploy` is idempotent; it applies pending migrations, then serves.
CMD ["sh", "-c", "npx prisma migrate deploy && node dist/main/server.js"]

FILE: ecommerce-backend/eslint.config.js
73 lines | 1,902 bytes
================================================================================
import js from "@eslint/js";
import globals from "globals";
import tseslint from "typescript-eslint";

/**
 * The Clean Architecture dependency rule, enforced rather than documented.
 * Inner layers may not import outer layers, frameworks, or SDKs.
 *
 * `main` is deliberately not restricted to "no domain imports": the
 * composition root is the outermost layer, and dependencies point inward, so
 * it is allowed to know every layer. What it must not do is host business
 * rules, which no import rule can express — `tests/architecture` and review
 * cover that.
 */
function forbidImports(patterns, message) {
  return {
    "no-restricted-imports": [
      "error",
      {
        patterns: [{ group: patterns, message }],
      },
    ],
  };
}

const frameworks = [
  "fastify",
  "zod",
  "ioredis",
  "kafkajs",
  "jose",
  "prom-client",
  "@prisma/client",
];

export default tseslint.config(
  { ignores: ["dist/", "coverage/", "node_modules/", "prisma/"] },
  js.configs.recommended,
  ...tseslint.configs.recommended,
  {
    files: ["**/*.ts"],
    languageOptions: {
      globals: globals.node,
    },
  },
  {
    files: ["src/domain/**/*.ts"],
    rules: forbidImports(
      [
        "**/application/**",
        "**/infrastructure/**",
        "**/main/**",
        ...frameworks,
        "node:*",
      ],
      "Domain must not depend on outer layers, frameworks, or the Node runtime.",
    ),
  },
  {
    files: ["src/application/**/*.ts"],
    rules: forbidImports(
      ["**/infrastructure/**", "**/main/**", ...frameworks, "node:*"],
      "Application must not depend on infrastructure, the composition root, frameworks, or the Node runtime; depend on a port instead.",
    ),
  },
  {
    files: ["src/infrastructure/**/*.ts"],
    rules: forbidImports(
      ["**/main/**"],
      "Adapters must not depend on the composition root that wires them.",
    ),
  },
);

FILE: ecommerce-backend/prisma/schema.prisma
100 lines | 3,120 bytes
================================================================================
// PostgreSQL schema. Domain timestamps are ISO-8601 strings in TypeScript;
// they are stored as timestamptz here and converted by the mappers, so the
// database compares absolute instants and never guesses a zone.
generator client {
  provider = "prisma-client-js"
}

datasource db {
  provider = "postgresql"
  url      = env("DATABASE_URL")
}

model Customer {
  id        String   @id
  email     String   @unique
  name      String
  createdAt DateTime @db.Timestamptz(3)
  account   Account?
}

// One credential record per customer. The hash is opaque here: the algorithm
// and its parameters live inside the string, owned by the hashing adapter.
// `role` is a closed set in the domain, and self-registration can only store
// "customer"; elevation is an explicit operator command.
model Account {
  customerId   String   @id
  passwordHash String
  role         String   @default("customer")
  createdAt    DateTime @db.Timestamptz(3)
  updatedAt    DateTime @db.Timestamptz(3)
  customer     Customer @relation(fields: [customerId], references: [id], onDelete: Cascade)
}

model Product {
  id                 String   @id
  name               String
  amountInMinorUnits Int
  currency           String
  createdAt          DateTime @db.Timestamptz(3)
}

model InventoryItem {
  id        String   @id
  productId String   @unique
  available Int
  reserved  Int
  updatedAt DateTime @db.Timestamptz(3)
}

// Cart and order lines are immutable snapshots, so they are stored as JSON
// rather than a second schema that has to stay in sync with the snapshot.
model Cart {
  id         String   @id
  customerId String   @unique
  items      Json
  createdAt  DateTime @db.Timestamptz(3)
  updatedAt  DateTime @db.Timestamptz(3)
}

model Order {
  id                 String    @id
  customerId         String
  items              Json
  totalAmountInMinor Int
  currency           String
  status             String
  createdAt          DateTime  @db.Timestamptz(3)
  updatedAt          DateTime  @db.Timestamptz(3)
  paidAt             DateTime? @db.Timestamptz(3)
  cancelledAt        DateTime? @db.Timestamptz(3)

  @@index([customerId])
}

// Transactional outbox with backoff and a dead-letter state. `nextAttemptAt`
// is what makes retries exponential instead of a hot loop; `deadLetteredAt`
// stops a poison message from being retried forever.
model OutboxMessage {
  id             String    @id
  eventName      String
  occurredAt     DateTime  @db.Timestamptz(3)
  payload        Json
  createdAt      DateTime  @db.Timestamptz(3)
  publishedAt    DateTime? @db.Timestamptz(3)
  attempts       Int       @default(0)
  lastError      String?
  nextAttemptAt  DateTime  @default(now()) @db.Timestamptz(3)
  deadLetteredAt DateTime? @db.Timestamptz(3)

  @@index([publishedAt, deadLetteredAt, nextAttemptAt])
}

// Durable inbox: a consumer records an event id before handling it, so a
// redelivery after a restart is recognised and skipped. The unique id is the
// whole guarantee.
model ProcessedEvent {
  id          String   @id
  eventName   String
  processedAt DateTime @db.Timestamptz(3)
}

FILE: ecommerce-backend/README.md
433 lines | 33,478 bytes
================================================================================
# E-commerce Backend

An e-commerce backend that demonstrates Clean Architecture with **functional TypeScript**, plus the operational pieces a real service needs. It covers the full purchase path — registration, login, product, stock, cart, order, payment — with Fastify for HTTP, Zod at the edge, Vitest for tests, PostgreSQL through Prisma at the persistence edge, and no classes and no DI container in the application.

Identity is part of the model, not a route decorator: registration creates the profile and its credential in one transaction, passwords are scrypt-hashed, access tokens are short-lived HS256 JWTs, and authorization (ownership plus roles) is decided by a pure application policy.

Every external dependency sits behind a port with an in-process adapter, so the whole flow runs and is tested with no infrastructure. Real adapters are selected by configuration, without changing a single use case:

| Capability     | In-process default | Production adapter                   |
| -------------- | ------------------ | ------------------------------------ |
| Persistence    | shared maps        | PostgreSQL 16 (Prisma, transactions) |
| Cache          | TTL map            | Redis                                |
| Idempotency    | TTL map + claims   | Redis `SET NX`                       |
| Rate limiting  | fixed window       | Redis + Lua                          |
| Event delivery | stdout publisher   | Kafka producer (via outbox)          |
| Consumer       | —                  | Kafka, idempotent by event id        |
| Passwords      | scrypt (Node)      | scrypt (Node), same format           |
| Access tokens  | HS256 JWT (`jose`) | HS256 JWT, secret from the platform  |
| Metrics        | —                  | Prometheus `/metrics`                |

The second goal is the part most Clean Architecture examples skip: **runtime consistency**. Multi-write workflows have explicit transaction boundaries, events leave through a transactional outbox, checkout is idempotent with an atomic claim, and delivery is at-least-once with an idempotent consumer.

```text
POST   /auth/register          (email, name, password → customer + bearer token)
POST   /auth/login             (email, password → bearer token)
GET    /auth/me                (current profile and role)
GET    /customers/:id          (self or admin)
POST   /products               (admin)
GET    /products               (public)
GET    /products/:id           (public)
GET    /customers/:customerId/cart
POST   /customers/:customerId/cart/items
DELETE /customers/:customerId/cart/items/:productId
POST   /orders
GET    /orders/:id
POST   /orders/:id/cancel
POST   /orders/:id/pay
POST   /checkout               (optional Idempotency-Key header)
GET    /health                 (liveness)
GET    /ready                  (readiness: postgres, redis, kafka)
GET    /metrics                (Prometheus)
```

Everything except `/auth/register`, `/auth/login`, the health/metrics endpoints, and the product catalogue requires `Authorization: Bearer <token>`. A customer may only touch its own cart, orders, and profile; an administrator may act for anyone.

## Quick start

### In-memory, no services

```sh
npm install
BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_EMAILS=owner@example.com npm run dev
```

The API listens on `http://localhost:3000` (override with `PORT`). With nothing configured, the composition root picks every in-process adapter and state resets on restart. The bootstrap variable is what lets the very first registration create a product; without it every account is a customer.

### Docker: API + PostgreSQL + Redis + Kafka + Prometheus

```sh
docker compose up --build
```

| Service      | What it is                                                         |
| ------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `postgres`   | `postgres:16-alpine`; data in a named volume                       |
| `redis`      | `redis:7-alpine` with AOF persistence                              |
| `kafka`      | `apache/kafka:3.9.0` in KRaft mode, topics created at app start-up |
| `prometheus` | Scrapes `app:3000/metrics` every 15s, UI on `:9090`                |
| `app`        | The API image; runs `prisma migrate deploy`, then serves           |

```sh
docker compose ps
docker compose logs -f app
docker compose down        # keep data
docker compose down -v     # delete data
```

Migrations are versioned SQL under `prisma/migrations/`. The container applies them with `prisma migrate deploy` before the server starts, so a fresh volume converges on the schema in the image. Locally, use `npm run db:migrate` (or `npm run db:migrate:dev` while changing the schema).

The image is a three-stage build (dependencies → compile → runtime), runs as the non-root `node` user, installs production dependencies only, ships `dist/` plus `prisma/` (for migrations), and healthchecks itself with Node's `fetch` (no `curl` in the image).

### Golden path

`owner@example.com` is the bootstrap administrator in `compose.yaml`; for a local run set it with `BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_EMAILS` as in the quick start.

```sh
SESSION=$(curl -s -X POST localhost:3000/auth/register \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"email":"owner@example.com","name":"Owner","password":"correct horse battery staple"}')

CUSTOMER=$(printf '%s' "$SESSION" | node -pe 'JSON.parse(require("fs").readFileSync(0)).customer.id')
TOKEN=$(printf '%s' "$SESSION" | node -pe 'JSON.parse(require("fs").readFileSync(0)).accessToken.token')

PRODUCT=$(curl -s -X POST localhost:3000/products \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" \
  -d '{"name":"Mechanical keyboard","price":{"amountInMinorUnits":1999,"currency":"usd"},"availableQuantity":5}' \
  | node -pe 'JSON.parse(require("fs").readFileSync(0)).id')

curl -s -X POST localhost:3000/customers/$CUSTOMER/cart/items \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" \
  -d "{\"productId\":\"$PRODUCT\",\"quantity\":2}"

curl -s -X POST localhost:3000/checkout \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" \
  -H 'Idempotency-Key: order-42' \
  -d "{\"customerId\":\"$CUSTOMER\"}"
```

The response contains a `paid` order and a `succeeded` payment, the cart is emptied, and the reserved stock is committed. Logging in again with the same address and password returns a fresh token via `/auth/login`. `order.created` / `order.paid` are written to the outbox in the same transaction, delivered by the worker, and (when Kafka is configured) consumed by the order-events consumer.

Repeating the same `Idempotency-Key` returns the original order with `200`, an `idempotent-replay: true` header, and `"replayed": true`. Firing two requests with the same _new_ key simultaneously yields one `201` and one `409 checkout_in_progress` — never two charges.

## Commands

```sh
npm run dev         # tsx watch src/main/server.ts
npm run build       # tsc -p tsconfig.build.json (cleans dist first)
npm start           # run compiled dist/main/server.js
npm test            # vitest run (integration suites skip without a URI)
npm run typecheck   # tsc --noEmit
npm run lint        # eslint .
npm run format      # prettier --write .
npm run format:check

npm run db:generate                     # regenerate the Prisma client after schema edits
npm run db:migrate                      # apply pending migrations
npm run db:migrate:dev                  # create a migration while developing
npm run admin:grant -- ada@example.com  # grant the admin role to an existing account

docker compose up --build               # everything
docker compose up -d postgres redis kafka          # only the dependencies
docker compose logs -f app
docker compose down -v
```

## Configuration

Parsed once, at the edge (`infrastructure/config/environment.ts`); nothing else reads `process.env`.

| Variable                         | Default                  | Meaning                                                     |
| -------------------------------- | ------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------- |
| `PORT`                           | `3000`                   | HTTP port                                                   |
| `NODE_ENV`                       | `development`            | `development \| test \| production`                         |
| `DATABASE_URL`                   | _(unset)_                | Unset → in-memory repositories; set → PostgreSQL            |
| `REDIS_URL`                      | _(unset)_                | Unset → in-process cache/idempotency/limiter; set → Redis   |
| `KAFKA_BROKERS`                  | _(unset)_                | Comma separated; unset → stdout publisher, no consumer      |
| `KAFKA_CLIENT_ID`                | `ecommerce-backend`      | Kafka client id                                             |
| `KAFKA_CONSUMER_GROUP`           | `ecommerce-order-events` | Consumer group                                              |
| `RATE_LIMIT_MAX`                 | `120`                    | Requests per caller per route per window                    |
| `RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_SECONDS`      | `60`                     | Window length                                               |
| `JWT_SECRET`                     | dev fallback             | HS256 signing secret; **required in production**, ≥32 chars |
| `JWT_ISSUER`                     | `ecommerce-backend`      | Issuer claim, verified on every request                     |
| `JWT_AUDIENCE`                   | `ecommerce-api`          | Audience claim, verified on every request                   |
| `ACCESS_TOKEN_TTL_SECONDS`       | `900`                    | Access token lifetime                                       |
| `AUTH_RATE_LIMIT_MAX`            | `10`                     | Register/login attempts per caller per window               |
| `AUTH_RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_SECONDS` | `60`                     | Window for the auth budget                                  |
| `BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_EMAILS`         | _(empty)_                | Comma-separated addresses that register as admin            |
| `OUTBOX_POLL_INTERVAL_MS`        | `1000`                   | Outbox worker poll interval                                 |

From the host, point at the published ports and apply the migrations once:

```sh
docker compose up -d postgres redis kafka
DATABASE_URL="postgresql://app:app@127.0.0.1:5432/ecommerce?schema=public" npm run db:migrate
DATABASE_URL="postgresql://app:app@127.0.0.1:5432/ecommerce?schema=public" \
REDIS_URL="redis://127.0.0.1:6379" \
KAFKA_BROKERS="127.0.0.1:29092" \
npm run dev
```

## The dependency rule

| Layer            | May import                        | May **not** import                                            |
| ---------------- | --------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `domain`         | `domain`                          | `application`, `infrastructure`, `main`, frameworks, `node:*` |
| `application`    | `application`, `domain`           | `infrastructure`, `main`, frameworks, `node:*`                |
| `infrastructure` | `application`, `domain`, adapters | `main`                                                        |
| `main`           | anything inward                   | anything outside the graph; no business rules                 |

`tests/architecture/dependency-rules.test.ts` reads the sources and fails on a framework import in `domain`/`application` (Fastify, Zod, Prisma, ioredis, KafkaJS, jose, prom-client), an `infrastructure` → `main` import, or `process.env` / `Date.now` / `randomUUID` in the inner layers. The rules survive someone editing the ESLint config. That is why hashing and JWT signing live in `infrastructure/auth` and the application only sees `PasswordHasher` and `TokenService`.

## Authentication and authorization

```text
POST /auth/register ──► password policy (domain) ──► scrypt hash (outside the transaction)
                          │
                          ▼
                    transaction { Customer + Account + customer.registered }
                          │
                          ▼
                    access token (sub, email, role, iss, aud, exp)

request ──► onRequest: verify bearer token ──► request.principal
             │
             ├─ requireAuth      → 401 without a valid token
             ├─ requireAdmin     → 403 for a non-admin
             └─ access policy    → 403 when the principal does not own the resource
```

- **One registration path.** `POST /auth/register` is the only way a customer is created; there is no endpoint that produces a profile without a credential. The profile, the credential row, and the `customer.registered` event commit together, so a half-registered account is impossible.
- **Passwords.** scrypt with `N=2^15, r=8, p=1`, a random 16-byte salt per hash, and parameters embedded in the stored string so the cost can rise later without a migration. Verification uses `timingSafeEqual`. The policy is OWASP's 12-character floor and a 128-character ceiling; no composition rules.
- **No account enumeration.** Unknown address, missing credential, and wrong password all return the same `401 invalid_credentials` message, and a missing account still spends one key derivation.
- **Tokens.** HS256 JWTs with `sub` (customer id), `email`, `role`, plus `iss`/`aud`/`iat`/`exp`. Issuer and audience are verified, the algorithm is pinned, and a 5-second clock tolerance is allowed. Lifetime defaults to 15 minutes (`ACCESS_TOKEN_TTL_SECONDS`). The secret must be at least 32 characters and is **required in production**; development uses an obviously labelled fallback.
- **Roles.** `customer` and `admin`, a closed set in the domain. Self-registration is customer-only, except addresses listed in `BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_EMAILS` (the first administrator). Later elevation is `npm run admin:grant -- <email>`, which updates the `Account` row. A role change reaches the API at the next login, because the role is carried in the token.
- **Ownership is a business rule.** `application/auth/access-policy.ts` decides whether a principal may act for a customer or read an order; controllers ask it, so the rule is unit-testable and not duplicated across routes. Route guards only answer "is there a principal" and "is it an admin".
- **Credential endpoints are rate limited harder.** Register and login use `AUTH_RATE_LIMIT_MAX` (default 10/min per caller and route) on top of the general limiter, in-process or shared through Redis.
- **Nothing sensitive is echoed.** Responses contain the profile and a token; password hashes never leave the process, and security headers (`nosniff`, `DENY`, `no-referrer`) are set on every response.
- **Refusals are deliberately vague.** A caller who does not own a resource gets `403` with one message that reveals neither the resource nor its owner.

## Transactions

`application/ports/unit-of-work.ts`:

```ts
execute<T, E>(work: () => Promise<Result<T, E>>): Promise<Result<T, E | UnitOfWorkError>>;
```

The work returns a `Result`, and **a failure value rolls back**. Two adapters honour it: the in-memory snapshot (restore on failure, serialised queue) and PostgreSQL (Prisma `$transaction` at serializable isolation; a failure result throws a sentinel so the partial writes are discarded, and `P2034` write conflicts are retried). No use case changes when the adapter changes.

The Prisma adapter propagates the transaction client through an `AsyncLocalStorage`, so repositories join the transaction without the application threading a client argument through every port. The boundary stays explicit at the use case.

## Checkout, idempotency, and concurrency

```text
POST /checkout
      │
      ├─ key? ──► stored outcome? ──► replay (200, idempotent-replay: true)
      │      └──► acquire key ──► busy? ──► 409 checkout_in_progress
      │
      ▼
create-order ──► transaction { reserve stock, snapshot prices, clear cart, queue order.created }
      │
      ▼
process-payment ──► charge (outside the transaction, provider idempotency key)
      │
 success ────┤──── decline
   │                │
   ▼                ▼
commit stock     cancel-order ──► transaction { release stock, queue order.cancelled }
+ order.paid
```

- The key is scoped `checkout:<customerId>:<key>`, so customers cannot collide.
- Only successful outcomes are stored (24h). A decline is a real answer, so the claim is released and the caller may retry.
- The claim is atomic: the in-memory store checks and writes without yielding; Redis uses `SET NX EX`. Two simultaneous requests cannot both charge.
- If the store fails on lookup, checkout **fails closed** with `503 idempotency_unavailable`.
- A successful charge whose local commit fails returns `payment_requires_reconciliation` — surfaced, never silently retried.

## The outbox and Kafka

```text
transaction { state + outbox row } ──commit──► outbox worker
                                                   │
                                     dispatch primitive (at-least-once)
                                                   │
                                   EventPublisher ─┴─► stdout | Kafka producer
                                                   │
                          published │ failed → nextAttemptAt (backoff)
                                    │        → dead letter after 5 attempts

Kafka topic (partitioned by order id) ──► consumer group
                                                   │
                                   inbox: INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING
```

- `OutboxMessage` carries a `DomainEvent`, an id, timestamps, `attempts`, `lastError`, `nextAttemptAt`, and `deadLetteredAt` — no topic, header, group, or offset.
- `dispatch-outbox.ts` publishes one message at a time; a failure is durable, retried with exponential backoff (1s, 2s, 4s, 8s, capped at 60s), and dead-lettered after 5 attempts instead of blocking the queue. The worker polls every `OUTBOX_POLL_INTERVAL_MS`.
- The due-message query is `publishedAt IS NULL AND deadLetteredAt IS NULL AND nextAttemptAt <= now`, so backoff survives a restart and a second worker agrees.
- `infrastructure/kafka/kafka-messages.ts` owns the broker vocabulary: topic routing (`ecommerce.orders.v1`, `ecommerce.customers.v1`, catch-all), partition key (order id, then customer id), JSON body, and `x-event-id` / `x-event-name` / `x-event-version` headers.
- `kafka-topics.ts` creates missing topics at start-up, so a consumer never races auto-creation.
- The consumer claims each `x-event-id` in the `ProcessedEvent` table before handling it. A redelivery — after a restart or on another replica — hits the unique key, is acknowledged, and the handler does not run. The port allows the claim to share a transaction with the projection write; today's handler only logs.

## Redis

One connection, three adapters:

- **Cache** — `Cache` port used by `get-product`; JSON values with TTL. A miss or corrupt entry is just a miss.
- **Idempotency** — `SET NX EX` claim plus a TTL record; `save` writes the record and deletes the claim atomically in a `MULTI`.
- **Rate limiting** — `INCR` + `EXPIRE` inside a Lua script so a crash cannot leave a counter without a window.

The in-memory adapters remain the default, which is what lets the unit tests run without Docker; the Redis ones are covered by `tests/integration/redis`.

## Observability and operations

- **`GET /health`** — liveness: the process is up.
- **`GET /ready`** — readiness: pings PostgreSQL (`SELECT 1`), Redis (`PING`), Kafka (producer connect). Returns `503` with a per-dependency report when something is down.
- **`GET /metrics`** — Prometheus: `http_requests_total`, `http_request_duration_seconds` (route pattern, never raw URL), `checkout_total{outcome}`, `outbox_messages_total{result="published|failed|dead_lettered"}`, plus Node defaults. Compose runs Prometheus to scrape it.
- **Request ids** — `x-request-id` is honoured when supplied and echoed on every response, so logs from different services join up.
- **Rate limiting** — a `preHandler` hook keyed by caller IP and route pattern; `x-ratelimit-*` headers on every response, `429` + `Retry-After` when exceeded. Exempt: `/health`, `/ready`, `/metrics`. Fails open if Redis is down (under-limiting beats a total outage).
- **Graceful shutdown** — on `SIGTERM`/`SIGINT`: stop the worker, close HTTP, then close adapters in reverse order.

### Error mapping

| Application error                                                                                                                                                             | HTTP          |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------- |
| `*_not_found`                                                                                                                                                                 | 404           |
| `invalid_credentials`                                                                                                                                                         | 401           |
| `forbidden`                                                                                                                                                                   | 403           |
| `invalid_*` (except `invalid_credentials`)                                                                                                                                    | 400           |
| `insufficient_stock`, `empty_cart`, `email_already_registered`, `order_already_paid`, `order_cancelled`, `item_not_in_cart`, `cart_currency_mismatch`, `checkout_in_progress` | 409           |
| `payment_declined`                                                                                                                                                            | 402           |
| `payment_gateway_unavailable`                                                                                                                                                 | 502           |
| `idempotency_unavailable`                                                                                                                                                     | 503           |
| `transaction_failed`, `payment_requires_reconciliation`, `repository_error`, `outbox_unavailable`                                                                             | 500 (generic) |

Server faults are logged once and answered as `{ "message": "Internal server error" }`; storage, driver, broker, and provider details never reach a client.

| Inbox        | `tests/unit/infrastructure/inbox-store.test.ts`                                     | First delivery wins; redelivery is reported as seen                                     |
| Transactions | `tests/unit/infrastructure/unit-of-work.test.ts`                                    | Commit, rollback, thrown error, rollback inside a real use case                         |

| Auth HTTP    | `tests/e2e/http/auth-flow.test.ts`                                                  | Token round trip, 401/403, admin-only writes, cart/order ownership                      |
| Platform     | `tests/e2e/http/platform-endpoints.test.ts`                                         | Readiness 200/503, metrics, request id echo, 429 + `Retry-After`                        |
| PostgreSQL   | `tests/integration/prisma/prisma-persistence.test.ts`                               | Real transactions (registration included), JSON snapshots, rollback, backoff/DLQ, inbox |
| Redis        | `tests/integration/redis/redis-adapters.test.ts`                                    | Cache TTL, `SET NX` claim, shared limiter                                               |
| Architecture | `tests/architecture/dependency-rules.test.ts`                                       | The dependency rule holds in the sources                                                |
| E2E HTTP     | `tests/e2e/http/checkout-flow.test.ts`                                              | Real routes through `inject`: 201/200/409/404/400, replay header                        |

Integration suites skip automatically unless the URIs are set:

```sh
docker compose up -d postgres redis
docker exec <postgres-container> createdb -U app ecommerce_test   # once
DATABASE_URL="postgresql://app:app@127.0.0.1:5432/ecommerce_test?schema=public" npm run db:migrate
DATABASE_TEST_URL="postgresql://app:app@127.0.0.1:5432/ecommerce_test?schema=public" \
REDIS_TEST_URL="redis://127.0.0.1:6379" \
npm test
```

## CI

`.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs format check, lint, typecheck, tests, build, and `docker build` on Node 22 for every push and pull request.

## Known limitations

- **Dead letters have no operator surface.** They are stored and counted (`outbox_messages_total{result="dead_lettered"}`), but replaying one is a manual SQL update; an admin endpoint is still missing.
- **Inbox rows are kept forever.** Dedupe is correct but unbounded; a retention job needs to delete ids older than the broker's retention window.
- **No reconciliation job.** `payment_requires_reconciliation` is surfaced and logged; nothing retries the commit or refunds the charge yet.
- **Payment is a stub.** `createInMemoryPaymentGateway` approves unless told to decline and remembers charges by idempotency key; a Stripe adapter implements the same port, with webhooks — not the request — confirming payment.
- **No refresh tokens or revocation.** Access tokens are short-lived and stateless; there is no refresh/rotation pair, no logout server-side, and no "revoke all sessions" path. Rotating `JWT_SECRET` invalidates everything at once.
- **No email verification, password reset, or MFA.** Registration trusts the address, and there is no out-of-band channel yet.
- **Role changes lag one login.** The role is a token claim, so an elevation or demotion applies when the current access token expires.
- **HS256 with a shared secret.** Fine for one service; multiple services should move to asymmetric keys (`RS256`/`ES256`) with a published JWKS.
- **Login throttling is per IP and route.** That stops single-source guessing, not a distributed credential-stuffing attack; a per-account counter would be the next step.
- **`/metrics` is unauthenticated.** Keep it on the internal network (or behind the platform's access control); the Prometheus sidecar expects plain HTTP.
- **Single PostgreSQL instance and single Kafka broker.** Enough for correctness on one machine, not for production durability.
- **Metrics, not traces.** Prometheus counters and a scrape config exist; OpenTelemetry spans across HTTP → PostgreSQL → outbox → Kafka do not.

### What to add next

1. **Payment reconciliation** — a job that finds `payment_requires_reconciliation`, re-queries the provider, and commits or refunds.
2. **Session lifecycle** — refresh tokens with rotation and reuse detection, plus a revocation list for logout and incident response.
3. **Account lifecycle** — email verification, password reset, and per-account login throttling.
4. **OpenTelemetry** — spans across HTTP, Prisma, outbox, Kafka, consumer; logs correlated by `x-request-id`.
5. **Grafana** — a provisioned dashboard for checkout outcomes, outbox lag, dead letters, p99 latency, auth failures, rate-limit rejections.
6. **Production deployment** — managed PostgreSQL with backups, multi-broker Kafka, TLS, secrets, horizontal scaling, rollout/rollback.
7. **Retention and admin tooling** — inbox retention and dead-letter replay.

import type { Principal } from "./principal.js";

export type AuthorizationError = {
  type: "forbidden";
  message: string;
};

/**
 * One message for every refusal: it tells the caller that this principal may
 * not perform this operation, and nothing about what exists or who owns it.
 */
export const FORBIDDEN: AuthorizationError = {
  type: "forbidden",
  message: "You are not allowed to access this resource",
};

/**
 * Ownership is a business rule, so it lives here rather than in a route table:
 * a customer may act for itself, and an administrator may act for anyone.
 */
export function canActForCustomer(
  principal: Principal,
  customerId: string,
): boolean {
  return principal.role === "admin" || principal.customerId === customerId;
}

export function canAccessOrder(
  principal: Principal,
  order: { customerId: string },
): boolean {
  return canActForCustomer(principal, order.customerId);
}

import { err, ok } from "../../../domain/shared/result.js";
import type { Login, LoginDependencies, LoginError } from "./login.types.js";

/**
 * Verifies an email/password pair and issues an access token.
 *
 * The account is loaded by email (the address is normalised by the customer
 * domain on registration), then the credential is verified. When either lookup
 * misses, the hasher is still asked to verify against nothing, which spends
 * the same work as a real comparison.
 */
export function loginUseCase({
  customers,
  accounts,
  hasher,
  tokens,
}: LoginDependencies): Login {
  return async ({ email, password }) => {
    let customer: Awaited<ReturnType<typeof customers.findByEmail>>;
    let account: Awaited<ReturnType<typeof accounts.findByCustomerId>>;

    try {
      customer = await customers.findByEmail(email);
      account =
        customer === null ? null : await accounts.findByCustomerId(customer.id);
    } catch {
      return err<LoginError>({
        type: "repository_error",
        message: "Could not load the account",
      });
    }

    const matches = await hasher.verify(
      password,
      account?.passwordHash ?? null,
    );

    if (customer === null || account === null || !matches) {
      return err<LoginError>({
        type: "invalid_credentials",
        message: "Email or password is incorrect",
      });
    }

    return ok({
      customer,
      role: account.role,
      accessToken: await tokens.issue({
        customerId: customer.id,
        email: customer.email,
        role: account.role,
      }),
    });
  };
}

FILE: ecommerce-backend/src/application/auth/register/register.ts

import {
  createAccount,
  validatePassword,
} from "../../../domain/auth/account.js";
import type { Role } from "../../../domain/auth/role.js";
import {
  createCustomer,
  type Customer,
} from "../../../domain/customer/customer.js";
import { customerRegistered } from "../../../domain/customer/customer-events.js";
import { err, ok } from "../../../domain/shared/result.js";
import { stageEvent } from "../../outbox/stage-event.js";
import type { AuthenticatedSession } from "../session.js";
import type {
  Register,
  RegisterDependencies,
  RegisterError,
} from "./register.types.js";

/**
 * Registration is the only way a customer comes into existence: the profile,
 * the credential, and the `customer.registered` event commit together. There is
 * no path that creates a customer who cannot log in.
 *
 * The password is hashed *before* the transaction opens: key derivation is
 * deliberately slow, and holding a serializable transaction open while the CPU
 * works would block other writers for no reason.
 */
export function registerUseCase({
  customers,
  accounts,
  hasher,
  tokens,
  ids,
  clock,
  outbox,
  unitOfWork,
  bootstrapAdminEmails,
}: RegisterDependencies): Register {
  return async (input) => {
    const password = validatePassword(input.password);

    if (!password.ok) {
      return err(password.error);
    }

    const passwordHash = await hasher.hash(password.value);

    const registered = await unitOfWork.execute<
      { customer: Customer; role: Role },
      RegisterError
    >(async () => {
      const now = clock.now();

      const customer = createCustomer({
        id: ids.nextId(),
        email: input.email,
        name: input.name,
        createdAt: now,
      });

      if (!customer.ok) {
        return err({
          type: "invalid_customer",
          message: customer.error.message,
        });
      }

      const existing = await customers.findByEmail(customer.value.email);

      if (existing !== null) {
        return err({
          type: "email_already_registered",
          message: "A customer with this email already exists",
        });
      }

      // Only an address listed by the operator can register as an
      // administrator; every other registration is a customer. Elevating an
      // existing account is a separate, auditable action (`npm run
      // admin:grant`).
      const role: Role = bootstrapAdminEmails.includes(customer.value.email)
        ? "admin"
        : "customer";

      const account = createAccount({
        customerId: customer.value.id,
        passwordHash,
        role,
        createdAt: now,
      });

      if (!account.ok) {
        return err(account.error);
      }

      await customers.save(customer.value);
      await accounts.save(account.value);
      await stageEvent(
        { outbox, ids, clock },
        customerRegistered(customer.value, now),
      );

      return ok({ customer: customer.value, role });
    });

    if (!registered.ok) {
      return registered;
    }

    // Issued only after the commit: a token for a rolled-back registration
    // would be a credential to an account that does not exist.
    const { customer, role } = registered.value;

    const session: AuthenticatedSession = {
      customer,
      role,
      accessToken: await tokens.issue({
        customerId: customer.id,
        email: customer.email,
        role,
      }),
    };

    return ok(session);
  };
}

FILE: ecommerce-backend/src/application/cart/add-item/add-item.ts
129 lines | 3,175 bytes
