import {
  addItem as addItemToCart,
  createCart,
  type Cart,
} from "../../../domain/cart/cart.js";
import {
  checkAvailability,
  type InventoryItem,
} from "../../../domain/inventory/inventory-item.js";
import type { Product } from "../../../domain/product/product.js";
import { err, ok } from "../../../domain/shared/result.js";
import type { AddItem, AddItemDependencies } from "./add-item.types.js";

/**
 * Adding to a cart never touches stock: it only checks that the units could be
 * promised. Stock moves when an order is placed, which keeps carts disposable.
 */
export function addItemUseCase({
  carts,
  products,
  inventory,
  ids,
  clock,
}: AddItemDependencies): AddItem {
  return async (input) => {
    const now = clock.now();

    let product: Product | null;

    try {
      product = await products.findById(input.productId);
    } catch {
      return err({
        type: "repository_error",
        message: "Could not load product",
      });
    }

    if (product === null) {
      return err({
        type: "product_not_found",
        message: `Product ${input.productId} was not found`,
      });
    }

    let stock: InventoryItem | null;

    try {
      stock = await inventory.findByProductId(input.productId);
    } catch {
      return err({
        type: "repository_error",
        message: "Could not load stock",
      });
    }

    if (stock === null) {
      return err({
        type: "inventory_not_found",
        message: `No stock record exists for product ${input.productId}`,
      });
    }

    // A cart is a promise, not a reservation: this asks the inventory rule
    // whether the units could be promised and mutates nothing.
    const availability = checkAvailability(stock, input.quantity);

    if (!availability.ok) {
      return availability.error.type === "insufficient_stock"
        ? err({
            type: "insufficient_stock",
            message: availability.error.message,
          })
        : err({
            type: "invalid_cart_quantity",
            message: availability.error.message,
          });
    }

    let cart: Cart | null;

    try {
      cart = await carts.findByCustomerId(input.customerId);
    } catch {
      return err({
        type: "repository_error",
        message: "Could not load cart",
      });
    }

    const current =
      cart ??
      createCart({
        id: ids.nextId(),
        customerId: input.customerId,
        createdAt: now,
      });

    const updated = addItemToCart(current, {
      productId: product.id,
      quantity: input.quantity,
      unitPrice: product.price,
      addedAt: now,
    });

    if (!updated.ok) {
      return updated.error.type === "cart_currency_mismatch"
        ? err({
            type: "cart_currency_mismatch",
            message: updated.error.message,
          })
        : err({
            type: "invalid_cart_quantity",
            message: updated.error.message,
          });
    }

    try {
      await carts.save(updated.value);
    } catch {
      return err({
        type: "repository_error",
        message: "Could not save cart",
      });
    }

    return ok(updated.value);
  };
}

import {
  removeItem as removeItemFromCart,
  type Cart,
} from "../../../domain/cart/cart.js";
import { err, ok } from "../../../domain/shared/result.js";
import type {
  RemoveItem,
  RemoveItemDependencies,
} from "./remove-item.types.js";

export function removeItemUseCase({
  carts,
  clock,
}: RemoveItemDependencies): RemoveItem {
  return async (input) => {
    let cart: Cart | null;

    try {
      cart = await carts.findByCustomerId(input.customerId);
    } catch {
      return err({
        type: "repository_error",
        message: "Could not load cart",
      });
    }

    if (cart === null) {
      return err({
        type: "cart_not_found",
        message: `No cart exists for customer ${input.customerId}`,
      });
    }

    const updated = removeItemFromCart(cart, input.productId, clock.now());

    if (!updated.ok) {
      return err({
        type: "item_not_in_cart",
        message: updated.error.message,
      });
    }

    try {
      await carts.save(updated.value);
    } catch {
      return err({
        type: "repository_error",
        message: "Could not save cart",
      });
    }

    return ok(updated.value);
  };
}

FILE: ecommerce-backend/src/application/checkout/checkout.ts

import { err, ok } from "../../domain/shared/result.js";
import type {
  Checkout,
  CheckoutDependencies,
  CheckoutOutcome,
  CheckoutResult,
} from "./checkout.types.js";

/** Long enough for a client retry window, short enough to stay disposable. */
const IDEMPOTENCY_TTL_SECONDS = 24 * 60 * 60;

/** A claim protects an in-flight request; it expires if the process dies. */
const IDEMPOTENCY_CLAIM_TTL_SECONDS = 30;

/**
 * Checkout is a composition, not a fifth copy of the same logic: place the
 * order, take the money, and cancel the order if the money never arrives.
 *
 * With an idempotency key it also refuses to do that twice. The key is scoped
 * to the customer, only successful outcomes are stored (a decline is a real
 * answer the caller may retry), and `acquire` closes the concurrency hole: two
 * simultaneous requests with the same new key cannot both start.
 */
export function checkoutUseCase({
  createOrder,
  processPayment,
  cancelOrder,
  idempotency,
  clock,
}: CheckoutDependencies): Checkout {
  return async (input) => {
    const key =
      input.idempotencyKey === undefined
        ? null
        : `checkout:${input.customerId}:${input.idempotencyKey}`;

    let claimed = false;

    if (key !== null) {
      try {
        const existing = await idempotency.find<CheckoutResult>(key);

        if (existing !== null) {
          return ok<CheckoutOutcome>({ ...existing.result, replayed: true });
        }

        const reservation = await idempotency.acquire(
          key,
          IDEMPOTENCY_CLAIM_TTL_SECONDS,
        );

        if (reservation === "busy") {
          return err({
            type: "checkout_in_progress",
            message:
              "A checkout with this idempotency key is already in progress",
          });
        }

        claimed = true;
      } catch {
        // Without a reliable store a retry could charge twice, so this request
        // fails closed rather than gambling with money.
        return err({
          type: "idempotency_unavailable",
          message: "Could not check the idempotency key",
        });
      }
    }

    try {
      const order = await createOrder({ customerId: input.customerId });

      if (!order.ok) {
        return err(order.error);
      }

      const payment = await processPayment({ orderId: order.value.id });

      if (!payment.ok) {
        // Compensating action: an unpaid order would otherwise hold stock
        // forever. The original failure is what the caller must see, even if
        // the compensation cannot be recorded.
        await cancelOrder({ orderId: order.value.id });

        return err(payment.error);
      }

      const outcome: CheckoutOutcome = {
        order: payment.value.order,
        payment: payment.value.payment,
        replayed: false,
      };

      if (key !== null) {
        try {
          await idempotency.save(
            key,
            {
              result: { order: outcome.order, payment: outcome.payment },
              createdAt: clock.now(),
            },
            IDEMPOTENCY_TTL_SECONDS,
          );
        } catch {
          // The money moved and the order exists; reporting a failure here
          // would invite a retry that creates a second order. The outcome is
          // returned as-is, and the missed replay protection is logged by the
          // adapter.
        }
      }

      return ok(outcome);
    } finally {
      if (claimed) {
        try {
          await idempotency.release(key as string);
        } catch {
          // The claim expires on its own; releasing is best effort.
        }
      }
    }
  };
}

import {
  releaseStock,
  type InventoryItem,
} from "../../../domain/inventory/inventory-item.js";
import {
  cancelOrder as cancelOrderDomain,
  type Order,
} from "../../../domain/order/order.js";
import { orderCancelled } from "../../../domain/order/order-events.js";
import { err, ok } from "../../../domain/shared/result.js";
import { stageEvent } from "../../outbox/stage-event.js";
import type {
  CancelOrder,
  CancelOrderDependencies,
  CancelOrderError,
} from "./cancel-order.types.js";

/**
 * Cancelling is the inverse of placing, and it is one transaction: releasing
 * every reserved unit, marking the order cancelled, and recording
 * `order.cancelled` either all commit or none do.
 *
 * That matters most when checkout compensates for a declined payment: a
 * cancelled order whose stock was never released would leak inventory.
 */
export function cancelOrderUseCase({
  orders,
  inventory,
  ids,
  clock,
  outbox,
  unitOfWork,
}: CancelOrderDependencies): CancelOrder {
  return async ({ orderId }) =>
    unitOfWork.execute<Order, CancelOrderError>(async () => {
      const now = clock.now();

      const order = await orders.findById(orderId);

      if (order === null) {
        return err({
          type: "order_not_found",
          message: `Order ${orderId} was not found`,
        });
      }

      const cancelled = cancelOrderDomain(order, now);

      if (!cancelled.ok) {
        return err({
          type: cancelled.error.type,
          message: cancelled.error.message,
        });
      }

      const releases: InventoryItem[] = [];

      for (const item of order.items) {
        const stock: InventoryItem | null = await inventory.findByProductId(
          item.productId,
        );

        if (stock === null) {
          return err({
            type: "inventory_not_found",
            message: `No stock record exists for product ${item.productId}`,
          });
        }

        const released = releaseStock(stock, item.quantity, now);

        if (!released.ok) {
          // The order was payable, so its units must be reserved. Failing here
          // means storage and order history disagree; surface it, do not guess.
          return err({
            type: "repository_error",
            message: `Could not release reserved stock: ${released.error.message}`,
          });
        }

        releases.push(released.value);
      }

      for (const release of releases) {
        await inventory.save(release);
      }

      await orders.save(cancelled.value);
      await stageEvent(
        { outbox, ids, clock },
        orderCancelled(cancelled.value, now),
      );

      return ok(cancelled.value);
    });
}

FILE: ecommerce-backend/src/application/order/create-order/create-order.ts

import { clearCart, type Cart } from "../../../domain/cart/cart.js";
import {
  reserveStock,
  type InventoryItem,
} from "../../../domain/inventory/inventory-item.js";
import { createOrder, type Order } from "../../../domain/order/order.js";
import { orderCreated } from "../../../domain/order/order-events.js";
import {
  orderItemFromCartItem,
  type OrderItem,
} from "../../../domain/order/order-item.js";
import { err, ok } from "../../../domain/shared/result.js";
import { stageEvent } from "../../outbox/stage-event.js";
import type {
  CreateOrder,
  CreateOrderDependencies,
  CreateOrderError,
} from "./create-order.types.js";

/**
 * Places an order inside one transaction: price snapshot, stock reservation,
 * the order itself, the emptied cart, and the `order.created` outbox row all
 * commit together or not at all.
 *
 * Nothing here publishes to a broker. If the write commits and the broker is
 * down, the outbox still holds the event, and the dispatcher retries it.
 */
export function createOrderUseCase({
  carts,
  orders,
  inventory,
  ids,
  clock,
  outbox,
  unitOfWork,
}: CreateOrderDependencies): CreateOrder {
  return async (input) =>
    unitOfWork.execute<Order, CreateOrderError>(async () => {
      const now = clock.now();

      const cart: Cart | null = await carts.findByCustomerId(input.customerId);

      if (cart === null) {
        return err({
          type: "cart_not_found",
          message: `No cart exists for customer ${input.customerId}`,
        });
      }

      if (cart.items.length === 0) {
        return err({
          type: "empty_cart",
          message: "Cart is empty",
        });
      }

      const items: OrderItem[] = [];

      for (const cartItem of cart.items) {
        const item = orderItemFromCartItem(cartItem);

        if (!item.ok) {
          return err({
            type: "invalid_order",
            message: item.error.message,
          });
        }

        items.push(item.value);
      }

      const order = createOrder({
        id: ids.nextId(),
        customerId: input.customerId,
        items,
        createdAt: now,
      });

      if (!order.ok) {
        return err({
          type: "invalid_order",
          message: order.error.message,
        });
      }

      const reservations: InventoryItem[] = [];

      for (const item of order.value.items) {
        const stock: InventoryItem | null = await inventory.findByProductId(
          item.productId,
        );

        if (stock === null) {
          return err({
            type: "inventory_not_found",
            message: `No stock record exists for product ${item.productId}`,
          });
        }

        const reserved = reserveStock(stock, item.quantity, now);

        if (!reserved.ok) {
          return err({
            type:
              reserved.error.type === "insufficient_stock"
                ? "insufficient_stock"
                : "invalid_order",
            message: reserved.error.message,
          });
        }

        reservations.push(reserved.value);
      }

      for (const reservation of reservations) {
        await inventory.save(reservation);
      }

      await orders.save(order.value);
      await carts.save(clearCart(cart, now));
      await stageEvent({ outbox, ids, clock }, orderCreated(order.value, now));

      return ok(order.value);
    });
}

FILE: ecommerce-backend/src/application/outbox/backoff.ts
20 lines | 685 bytes

/** After this many failed deliveries the message is dead-lettered. */
export const MAX_DELIVERY_ATTEMPTS = 5;

const BASE_DELAY_MS = 1_000;
const MAX_DELAY_MS = 60_000;

/**
 * Exponential backoff with a ceiling: 1s, 2s, 4s, 8s, then capped at 60s.
 * The dispatcher asks for this instead of retrying immediately, so a broker
 * outage does not turn into a hot loop against the broker and the database.
 */
export function retryDelayMs(attempts: number): number {
  const exponent = Math.max(0, attempts - 1);

  return Math.min(MAX_DELAY_MS, BASE_DELAY_MS * 2 ** exponent);
}

export function isDeadLettered(attempts: number): boolean {
  return attempts >= MAX_DELIVERY_ATTEMPTS;
}

================================================================================
FILE: ecommerce-backend/src/application/outbox/dispatch-outbox.ts
83 lines | 2,439 bytes
================================================================================
import { err, ok } from "../../domain/shared/result.js";
import { isDeadLettered, retryDelayMs } from "./backoff.js";
import type {
  DispatchOutbox,
  DispatchOutboxDependencies,
  OutboxDispatchSummary,
} from "./dispatch-outbox.types.js";

const DEFAULT_BATCH_SIZE = 50;

/**
 * The read side of the outbox: take messages that are due, hand the
 * broker-agnostic event to the publisher, then mark the message delivered.
 *
 * On failure the message keeps its place with a later `nextAttemptAt`
 * (exponential backoff) and, once the attempt budget is spent, is
 * dead-lettered rather than retried forever. Publishing before marking is
 * deliberate: a crash can duplicate an event, but can never lose one.
 */
export function dispatchOutboxUseCase({
  outbox,
  publisher,
  clock,
  batchSize = DEFAULT_BATCH_SIZE,
}: DispatchOutboxDependencies): DispatchOutbox {
  return async () => {
    const now = clock.now();
    let pending;

    try {
      pending = await outbox.findPending(batchSize, now);
    } catch {
      return err({
        type: "outbox_unavailable",
        message: "Could not read pending outbox messages",
      });
    }

    const summary: OutboxDispatchSummary = {
      published: 0,
      failed: 0,
      deadLettered: 0,
    };

    for (const message of pending) {
      try {
        await publisher.publish(message.event, { id: message.id });
        await outbox.markPublished(message.id, clock.now());
        summary.published += 1;
      } catch (error) {
        summary.failed += 1;

        const attempts = message.attempts + 1;
        const deadLettered = isDeadLettered(attempts);
        const failedAt = clock.now();

        if (deadLettered) {
          summary.deadLettered += 1;
        }

        try {
          await outbox.markFailed(message.id, {
            error:
              error instanceof Error
                ? error.message
                : "Unknown delivery failure",
            nextAttemptAt: deadLettered
              ? failedAt
              : new Date(
                  new Date(failedAt).getTime() + retryDelayMs(attempts),
                ).toISOString(),
            deadLetteredAt: deadLettered ? failedAt : null,
          });
        } catch {
          // The message stays pending with its previous schedule, which is the
          // safe state: it will be considered again on the next poll.
        }
      }
    }

    return ok(summary);
  };
}

import type { Result } from "../../domain/shared/result.js";
import type { Clock } from "../ports/clock.js";
import type { EventPublisher } from "../ports/event-publisher.js";
import type { OutboxRepository } from "../ports/outbox.js";

export type OutboxDispatchSummary = {
  published: number;
  failed: number;
  deadLettered: number;
};

export type OutboxDispatchError = {
  type: "outbox_unavailable";
  message: string;
};

export type DispatchOutboxDependencies = {
  outbox: OutboxRepository;
  publisher: EventPublisher;
  clock: Clock;
  batchSize?: number;
};

/**
 * Drains due messages. A single failed delivery is recorded on the message, its
 * next attempt is scheduled with exponential backoff, and the batch continues
 * so one poison event cannot block the rest. After the attempt budget it is
 * dead-lettered instead of retried forever.

export type DispatchOutbox = () => Promise<

FILE: ecommerce-backend/src/application/outbox/stage-event.ts
33 lines | 912 bytes
================================================================================
import type { DomainEvent } from "../../domain/shared/domain-event.js";
import type { Clock } from "../ports/clock.js";
import type { IdGenerator } from "../ports/id-generator.js";
import type { OutboxRepository } from "../ports/outbox.js";

export type StageEventDependencies = {
  outbox: OutboxRepository;
  ids: IdGenerator;
  clock: Clock;
};

/**
 * Records an event for later delivery. It must be called inside the same
 * transaction as the business write, which is the whole point: either both the
 * state change and the event are committed, or neither is.
 */
export function stageEvent(
  { outbox, ids, clock }: StageEventDependencies,
  event: DomainEvent,
): Promise<void> {
  const now = clock.now();

  return outbox.add({
    id: ids.nextId(),
    event,
    createdAt: now,
    publishedAt: null,
    attempts: 0,
    lastError: null,
    nextAttemptAt: now,
    deadLetteredAt: null,
  });
}

FILE: ecommerce-backend/src/application/payment/process-payment/process-payment.ts
211 lines | 5,792 bytes
================================================================================
import {
  commitReservation,
  type InventoryItem,
} from "../../../domain/inventory/inventory-item.js";
import { payOrder, type Order } from "../../../domain/order/order.js";
import { orderPaid } from "../../../domain/order/order-events.js";
import {
  createPayment,
  failPayment,
  succeedPayment,
} from "../../../domain/payment/payment.js";
import { err, ok } from "../../../domain/shared/result.js";
import { stageEvent } from "../../outbox/stage-event.js";
import type { ChargeResult } from "../../ports/payment-gateway.js";
import type {
  ProcessPayment,
  ProcessPaymentDependencies,
  ProcessPaymentError,
} from "./process-payment.types.js";

/**
 * Payment crosses a distributed-system boundary, and this use case says so
 * instead of hiding it:
 *
 *   1. read and validate the order                  (no writes)
 *   2. charge the provider                          (external, not our data)
 *   3. commit stock, mark paid, stage order.paid     (one transaction)
 *
 * Step 2 is deliberately outside the transaction: a PostgreSQL rollback cannot
 * un-charge a card. The charge carries an idempotency key so a retry of step 2
 * returns the original provider reference instead of taking money twice.
 *
 * If step 3 fails after a successful charge, the caller gets
 * `payment_requires_reconciliation`: money moved but the order was not
 * recorded as paid. That is a real distributed-systems state, and it needs a
 * human or a reconciliation job, not a `try/catch`.
 */
export function processPaymentUseCase({
  orders,
  inventory,
  gateway,
  ids,
  clock,
  outbox,
  unitOfWork,
}: ProcessPaymentDependencies): ProcessPayment {
  return async ({ orderId }) => {
    const now = clock.now();

    const order: Order | null = await orders.findById(orderId);

    if (order === null) {
      return err({
        type: "order_not_found",
        message: `Order ${orderId} was not found`,
      });
    }

    const payable = payOrder(order, now);

    if (!payable.ok) {
      return err({
        type: payable.error.type,
        message: payable.error.message,
      });
    }

    const pending = createPayment({
      id: ids.nextId(),
      orderId: order.id,
      amount: order.total,
      createdAt: now,
    });

    if (!pending.ok) {
      return err({
        type: "invalid_payment",
        message: pending.error.message,
      });
    }

    let charge: ChargeResult;

    try {
      charge = await gateway.charge({
        orderId: order.id,
        customerId: order.customerId,
        amount: order.total,
        idempotencyKey: pending.value.id,
      });
    } catch {
      const failed = failPayment(
        pending.value,
        "Payment gateway did not respond",
        now,
      );

      return err({
        type: "payment_gateway_unavailable",
        message: "Payment gateway is unavailable",
        payment: failed.ok ? failed.value : pending.value,
      });
    }

    if (!charge.ok) {
      const failed = failPayment(pending.value, charge.error.message, now);
      const payment = failed.ok ? failed.value : pending.value;

      return charge.error.type === "payment_declined"
        ? err({
            type: "payment_declined",
            message: charge.error.message,
            payment,
          })
        : err({
            type: "payment_gateway_unavailable",
            message: charge.error.message,
            payment,
          });
    }

    const succeeded = succeedPayment(
      pending.value,
      charge.value.providerReference,
      now,
    );

    if (!succeeded.ok) {
      return err({
        type: "invalid_payment",
        message: succeeded.error.message,
      });
    }

    const recorded = await unitOfWork.execute<Order, ProcessPaymentError>(
      async () => {
        const current: Order | null = await orders.findById(orderId);

        if (current === null) {
          return err({
            type: "order_not_found" as const,
            message: `Order ${orderId} was not found`,
          });
        }

        const transition = payOrder(current, now);

        if (!transition.ok) {
          return err({
            type: transition.error.type,
            message: transition.error.message,
          });
        }

        // The charge succeeded, so held units become sold units.
        const commits: InventoryItem[] = [];

        for (const item of current.items) {
          const stock: InventoryItem | null = await inventory.findByProductId(
            item.productId,
          );

          if (stock === null) {
            return err({
              type: "inventory_not_found" as const,
              message: `No stock record exists for product ${item.productId}`,
            });
          }

          const committed = commitReservation(stock, item.quantity, now);

          if (!committed.ok) {
            return err({
              type: "repository_error" as const,
              message: `Could not commit reserved stock: ${committed.error.message}`,
            });
          }

          commits.push(committed.value);
        }

        for (const commit of commits) {
          await inventory.save(commit);
        }

        await orders.save(transition.value);
        await stageEvent(
          { outbox, ids, clock },
          orderPaid(transition.value, charge.value.providerReference, now),
        );

        return ok(transition.value);
      },
    );

    if (!recorded.ok) {
      return err({
        type: "payment_requires_reconciliation",
        message:
          "The payment was taken but the order could not be recorded as paid",
        payment: succeeded.value,
        cause: recorded.error.type,
      });
    }

    return ok({
      order: recorded.value,
      payment: succeeded.value,
    });
  };
}

import type { Order, OrderId } from "../../../domain/order/order.js";
import type { Payment } from "../../../domain/payment/payment.js";
import type { Result } from "../../../domain/shared/result.js";
import type { Clock } from "../../ports/clock.js";
import type { IdGenerator } from "../../ports/id-generator.js";
import type { OutboxRepository } from "../../ports/outbox.js";
import type { PaymentGateway } from "../../ports/payment-gateway.js";
import type { InventoryRepository } from "../../ports/repositories/inventory-repository.js";
import type { OrderRepository } from "../../ports/repositories/order-repository.js";
import type { UnitOfWork, UnitOfWorkError } from "../../ports/unit-of-work.js";

export type ProcessPaymentInput = {
  orderId: OrderId;
};

/**
 * Declines carry the failed payment so a caller can persist or report the
 * attempt; the amount of an order is never invented at the boundary.
 *
 * `payment_requires_reconciliation` is the honest state after a successful
 * charge whose local commit failed. It is not a retryable "try again" error:
 * the money has already moved, so the transaction id is what an operator needs.
 */
export type ProcessPaymentError =
  | {
      type: "order_not_found";
      message: string;
    }
  | {
      type: "order_already_paid";
      message: string;
    }
  | {
      type: "order_cancelled";
      message: string;
    }
  | {
      type: "invalid_payment";
      message: string;
    }
  | {
      type: "inventory_not_found";
      message: string;
    }
  | {
      type: "repository_error";
      message: string;
    }
  | {
      type: "payment_declined";
      message: string;
      payment: Payment;
    }
  | {
      type: "payment_gateway_unavailable";
      message: string;
      payment: Payment;
    }
  | {
      type: "payment_requires_reconciliation";
      message: string;
      payment: Payment;
      cause: string;
    };

export type ProcessPaymentResult = {
  order: Order;
  payment: Payment;
};

export type ProcessPaymentDependencies = {
  orders: OrderRepository;
  inventory: InventoryRepository;
  gateway: PaymentGateway;
  ids: IdGenerator;
  clock: Clock;
  outbox: OutboxRepository;
  unitOfWork: UnitOfWork;
};

export type ProcessPayment = (
  input: ProcessPaymentInput,
) => Promise<
  Result<ProcessPaymentResult, ProcessPaymentError | UnitOfWorkError>
>;

FILE: ecommerce-backend/src/application/ports/idempotency-store.ts
35 lines | 1,323 bytes
================================================================================
/**
 * The recorded outcome of an operation that already happened, keyed by the
 * caller's idempotency key. Replays return this instead of doing the work
 * again, so a retried checkout cannot create a second order.
 */
export type IdempotencyRecord<TResult> = {
  result: TResult;
  createdAt: string;
};

/**
 * `acquired` means this request owns the key and may do the work; `busy`
 * means another request is already doing it, so this one must not start.
 */
export type IdempotencyReservation = "acquired" | "busy";

/**
 * Short-lived storage for idempotent request outcomes. Redis implements this
 * in production with `SET NX` and a TTL; the in-memory adapter exists for tests
 * and local runs. The port knows nothing about either.
 *
 * `acquire` is the part that closes the double-charge race: two simultaneous
 * requests with the same unused key cannot both receive `acquired`.
 */
export type IdempotencyStore = {
  find<TResult>(key: string): Promise<IdempotencyRecord<TResult> | null>;
  save<TResult>(
    key: string,
    record: IdempotencyRecord<TResult>,
    ttlSeconds: number,
  ): Promise<void>;
  acquire(key: string, ttlSeconds: number): Promise<IdempotencyReservation>;
  /** Frees a claim that did not produce a stored outcome (a decline, a crash). */
  release(key: string): Promise<void>;
};

FILE: ecommerce-backend/src/application/ports/inbox-store.ts
16 lines | 576 bytes
================================================================================
/**
 * The consumer side of at-least-once delivery: record the event id in the same
 * transaction as the work it triggers, so a redelivery is recognised.
 *
 * `markProcessed` returns false when the id was already recorded, which is the
 * signal to skip the handler. It is an insert-and-see, not a read-then-write:
 * the unique constraint is what makes it safe under concurrency.
 */
export type InboxStore = {
  markProcessed(input: {
    id: string;
    eventName: string;
    processedAt: string;
  }): Promise<boolean>;
  hasProcessed(id: string): Promise<boolean>;
};

FILE: ecommerce-backend/src/application/ports/outbox.ts
44 lines | 1,656 bytes
================================================================================
import type { DomainEvent } from "../../domain/shared/domain-event.js";

/**
 * A domain event captured for later delivery, written in the same transaction
 * as the state it describes.
 *
 * It deliberately contains no Kafka topic, partition key, header, or offset:
 * the outbox is a persistence reliability pattern, not a message broker. A
 * Kafka producer is one possible *reader* of these rows.
 */
export type OutboxMessage = {
  id: string;
  event: DomainEvent;
  createdAt: string;
  publishedAt: string | null;
  attempts: number;
  lastError: string | null;
  /** Retry scheduling: a failed message waits before it is picked up again. */
  nextAttemptAt: string;
  /** Set once the attempt budget is exhausted; the message is never retried. */
  deadLetteredAt: string | null;
};

export type OutboxFailure = {
  error: string;
  /** When the next attempt becomes eligible. */
  nextAttemptAt: string;
  /** Non-null when this failure exhausted the attempt budget. */
  deadLetteredAt: string | null;
};

/**
 * Storage contract for the outbox. `add` must run inside the same transaction
 * as the business write, otherwise the dual-write problem returns.
 */
export type OutboxRepository = {
  add(message: OutboxMessage): Promise<void>;
  /** Messages that are due: unpublished, not dead-lettered, past their backoff. */
  findPending(limit: number, now: string): Promise<OutboxMessage[]>;
  markPublished(id: string, publishedAt: string): Promise<void>;
  markFailed(id: string, failure: OutboxFailure): Promise<void>;
  /** Dead-lettered messages, for operators and tests. */
  findDeadLettered(limit: number): Promise<OutboxMessage[]>;
};

================================================================================
/**
 * Password hashing is deliberately a port: the algorithm, its cost, and the
 * encoding are infrastructure decisions, while the application only needs
 * "store this safely" and "does this match".
 */
export type PasswordHasher = {
  /** Returns a self-describing hash (algorithm and parameters included). */
  hash(password: string): Promise<string>;
  /**
   * A missing credential (`null`) still costs one verification in the
   * adapter, so an unknown account and a wrong password take the same time and
   * cannot be distinguished by an attacker.
   */
  verify(password: string, storedHash: string | null): Promise<boolean>;
};

FILE: ecommerce-backend/src/application/ports/unit-of-work.ts
26 lines | 863 bytes
================================================================================
import type { Result } from "../../domain/shared/result.js";

/**
 * The one failure infrastructure adds to a transaction: the boundary itself
 * could not be opened, committed, or rolled back. Business failures come from
 * the work, not from here.
 */
export type UnitOfWorkError = {
  type: "transaction_failed";
  message: string;
};

/**
 * "These changes commit together, or none of them do."
 *
 * The work returns a Result instead of throwing, which lets the port decide
 * from the *business* outcome whether to commit: a failure value rolls back.
 * Infrastructure implements this with a database transaction; the application
 * never learns whether that means PostgreSQL, an in-memory snapshot, or
 * anything else.
 */
export type UnitOfWork = {
  execute<T, E>(
    work: () => Promise<Result<T, E>>,
  ): Promise<Result<T, E | UnitOfWorkError>>;
};

FILE: ecommerce-backend/src/application/product/create-product/create-product.ts
72 lines | 1,859 bytes
================================================================================
import { createInventoryItem } from "../../../domain/inventory/inventory-item.js";
import { createMoney } from "../../../domain/product/money.js";
import {
  createProduct,
  type Product,
} from "../../../domain/product/product.js";
import { err, ok } from "../../../domain/shared/result.js";
import type {
  CreateProduct,
  CreateProductDependencies,
  CreateProductError,
} from "./create-product.types.js";

/**
 * A product without stock cannot be sold, so creating one also opens its
 * inventory record. Splitting them would allow a product that no cart can fill,
 * which is why both rows go in one transaction.
 */
export function createProductUseCase({
  products,
  inventory,
  ids,
  clock,
  unitOfWork,
}: CreateProductDependencies): CreateProduct {
  return async (input) =>
    unitOfWork.execute<Product, CreateProductError>(async () => {
      const now = clock.now();

      const price = createMoney(input.amountInMinorUnits, input.currency);

      if (!price.ok) {
        return err({
          type: "invalid_product",
          message: price.error.message,
        });
      }

      const product = createProduct({
        id: ids.nextId(),
        name: input.name,
        price: price.value,
        createdAt: now,
      });

      if (!product.ok) {
        return err({
          type: "invalid_product",
          message: product.error.message,
        });
      }

      const stock = createInventoryItem({
        id: ids.nextId(),
        productId: product.value.id,
        quantity: input.availableQuantity ?? 0,
        updatedAt: now,
      });

      if (!stock.ok) {
        return err({
          type: "invalid_inventory",
          message: stock.error.message,
        });
      }

      await products.save(product.value);
      await inventory.save(stock.value);

      return ok(product.value);
    });
}

FILE: ecommerce-backend/src/application/product/get-product/get-product.ts

import type { Product } from "../../../domain/product/product.js";
import { err, ok } from "../../../domain/shared/result.js";
import type {
  GetProduct,
  GetProductDependencies,
} from "./get-product.types.js";

export const PRODUCT_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS = 60;

const cacheKey = (id: string): string => `product:${id}`;

/**
 * Read-through: a hit shortens the path, a miss falls back to the repository,
 * and a cache failure is ignored. The cache can only ever make this slower, so
 * its errors are not treated as request failures.
 */
export function getProductUseCase({
  products,
  cache,
}: GetProductDependencies): GetProduct {
  return async ({ id }) => {
    try {
      const cached = await cache.get<Product>(cacheKey(id));

      if (cached !== null) {
        return ok(cached);
      }
    } catch {
      // Cache is an optimisation, not a source of truth.
    }

    let product: Product | null;

    try {
      product = await products.findById(id);
    } catch {
      return err({
        type: "repository_error",
        message: "Could not load product",
      });
    }

    if (product === null) {
      return err({
        type: "product_not_found",
        message: `Product ${id} was not found`,
      });
    }

    try {
      await cache.set(cacheKey(id), product, PRODUCT_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS);
    } catch {
      // Losing a cache write costs a future repository read, nothing more.
    }

    return ok(product);
  };
}

import { err, ok, type Result } from "../shared/result.js";
import type { AuthDomainError } from "./auth-errors.js";
import { isRole, type Role } from "./role.js";

/**
 * Length is the property that actually resists guessing, so the policy is a
 * length floor (OWASP's 12-character minimum) and a ceiling that keeps a
 * request from forcing an unbounded key derivation. Composition rules are
 * deliberately absent: they push people toward predictable substitutions.
 */
export const MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH = 12;
export const MAX_PASSWORD_LENGTH = 128;

export type Account = {
  customerId: string;
  /** Produced and read by the hashing adapter; never a plaintext password. */
  passwordHash: string;
  role: Role;
  createdAt: string;
  updatedAt: string;
};

export function validatePassword(
  password: string,
): Result<string, AuthDomainError> {
  if (password.length < MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH) {
    return err({
      type: "invalid_password",
      message: `Password must be at least ${MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH} characters`,
    });
  }

  if (password.length > MAX_PASSWORD_LENGTH) {
    return err({
      type: "invalid_password",
      message: `Password cannot exceed ${MAX_PASSWORD_LENGTH} characters`,
    });
  }

  return ok(password);
}

/**
 * The credential record for a customer. The hash is supplied by the caller,
 * because hashing is an infrastructure concern; the domain only decides
 * whether the result is structurally valid and which roles may exist.
 */
export function createAccount(input: {
  customerId: string;
  passwordHash: string;
  role: Role;
  createdAt: string;
}): Result<Account, AuthDomainError> {
  if (input.passwordHash.trim().length === 0) {
    return err({
      type: "invalid_password",
      message: "A password hash is required",
    });
  }

  if (!isRole(input.role)) {
    return err({
      type: "invalid_role",
      message: `Unknown role: ${String(input.role)}`,
    });
  }

  return ok({
    customerId: input.customerId,
    passwordHash: input.passwordHash,
    role: input.role,
    createdAt: input.createdAt,
    updatedAt: input.createdAt,
  });
}

/** Changes the role without touching the stored credential. */
export function assignRole(
  account: Account,
  role: Role,
  updatedAt: string,
): Result<Account, AuthDomainError> {
  if (!isRole(role)) {
    return err({
      type: "invalid_role",
      message: `Unknown role: ${String(role)}`,
    });
  }

  return ok({ ...account, role, updatedAt });
}

/**
 * The closed set of roles. Authorization is written against these values, so a
 * typo in a token or a row becomes a rejected role rather than a silent grant.
 */
export const ROLES = ["customer", "admin"] as const;

export type Role = (typeof ROLES)[number];

export function isRole(value: string): value is Role {
  return (ROLES as readonly string[]).includes(value);
}

import type { Money } from "../product/money.js";
import type { ProductId } from "../product/product-id.js";
import { err, ok, type Result } from "../shared/result.js";
import type { CartDomainError } from "./cart-errors.js";

export const MAX_ITEM_QUANTITY = 99;

/**
 * A cart line remembers the price it was added at, so a price change does not
 * silently rewrite what the customer saw.
 */
export type CartItem = {
  productId: ProductId;
  quantity: number;
  unitPrice: Money;
};

export function createCartItem(input: {
  productId: ProductId;
  quantity: number;
  unitPrice: Money;
}): Result<CartItem, CartDomainError> {
  if (
    !Number.isInteger(input.quantity) ||
    input.quantity < 1 ||
    input.quantity > MAX_ITEM_QUANTITY
  ) {
    return err({
      type: "invalid_cart_quantity",
      message: `Quantity must be a whole number between 1 and ${MAX_ITEM_QUANTITY}`,
    });
  }

  return ok({
    productId: input.productId,
    quantity: input.quantity,
    unitPrice: input.unitPrice,
  });
}

export function cartItemTotal(item: CartItem): Money {
  return {
    amountInMinorUnits: item.unitPrice.amountInMinorUnits * item.quantity,
    currency: item.unitPrice.currency,
  };
}

FILE: ecommerce-backend/src/domain/cart/cart.ts
156 lines | 3,716 bytes
================================================================================
import type { CustomerId } from "../customer/customer-id.js";
import type { Money } from "../product/money.js";
import type { ProductId } from "../product/product-id.js";
import { ok, type Result } from "../shared/result.js";
import {
  cartItemTotal,
  createCartItem,
  MAX_ITEM_QUANTITY,
  type CartItem,
} from "./cart-item.js";
import type { CartDomainError } from "./cart-errors.js";

export type CartId = string;

/** One open cart per customer, holding priced lines and nothing else. */
export type Cart = {
  id: CartId;
  customerId: CustomerId;
  items: CartItem[];
  createdAt: string;
  updatedAt: string;
};

export function createCart(input: {
  id: CartId;
  customerId: CustomerId;
  createdAt: string;
}): Cart {
  return {
    id: input.id,
    customerId: input.customerId,
    items: [],
    createdAt: input.createdAt,
    updatedAt: input.createdAt,
  };
}

/**
 * Adds a line, merging into an existing line for the same product. All lines
 * must share one currency, otherwise the cart total would be undefined.
 */
export function addItem(
  cart: Cart,
  input: {
    productId: ProductId;
    quantity: number;
    unitPrice: Money;
    addedAt: string;
  },
): Result<Cart, CartDomainError> {
  const candidate = createCartItem({
    productId: input.productId,
    quantity: input.quantity,
    unitPrice: input.unitPrice,
  });

  if (!candidate.ok) {
    return candidate;
  }

  const cartCurrency = cart.items[0]?.unitPrice.currency;

  if (cartCurrency !== undefined && cartCurrency !== input.unitPrice.currency) {
    return {
      ok: false,
      error: {
        type: "cart_currency_mismatch",
        message: `This cart is priced in ${cartCurrency}`,
      },
    };
  }

  const existing = cart.items.find(
    (item) => item.productId === input.productId,
  );

  if (existing === undefined) {
    return ok({
      ...cart,
      items: [...cart.items, candidate.value],
      updatedAt: input.addedAt,
    });
  }

  const quantity = existing.quantity + candidate.value.quantity;

  if (quantity > MAX_ITEM_QUANTITY) {
    return {
      ok: false,
      error: {
        type: "invalid_cart_quantity",
        message: `A cart line cannot exceed ${MAX_ITEM_QUANTITY} units`,
      },
    };
  }

  return ok({
    ...cart,
    items: cart.items.map((item) =>
      item.productId === input.productId
        ? { ...item, quantity, unitPrice: input.unitPrice }
        : item,
    ),
    updatedAt: input.addedAt,
  });
}

export function removeItem(
  cart: Cart,
  productId: ProductId,
  removedAt: string,
): Result<Cart, CartDomainError> {
  const exists = cart.items.some((item) => item.productId === productId);

  if (!exists) {
    return {
      ok: false,
      error: {
        type: "item_not_in_cart",
        message: `Product ${productId} is not in this cart`,
      },
    };
  }

  return ok({
    ...cart,
    items: cart.items.filter((item) => item.productId !== productId),
    updatedAt: removedAt,
  });
}

export function clearCart(cart: Cart, clearedAt: string): Cart {
  return { ...cart, items: [], updatedAt: clearedAt };
}

/** Total of a cart, or `null` when it is empty (so no currency is implied). */
export function cartTotal(cart: Cart): Money | null {
  const first = cart.items[0];

  if (first === undefined) {
    return null;
  }

  return cart.items.reduce<Money>(
    (total, item) => ({
      amountInMinorUnits:
        total.amountInMinorUnits + cartItemTotal(item).amountInMinorUnits,
      currency: total.currency,
    }),
    { amountInMinorUnits: 0, currency: first.unitPrice.currency },
  );
}

export function cartItemCount(cart: Cart): number {
  return cart.items.reduce((count, item) => count + item.quantity, 0);
}

FILE: ecommerce-backend/src/domain/customer/customer.ts

import { err, ok, type Result } from "../shared/result.js";
import type { CustomerDomainError } from "./customer-errors.js";
import type { CustomerId } from "./customer-id.js";

export const MAX_CUSTOMER_NAME_LENGTH = 120;

/**
 * Deliberately permissive: the goal is to reject obvious garbage, not to
 * reimplement RFC 5322. Verification belongs to a later flow.
 */
const EMAIL_PATTERN = /^[^\s@]+@[^\s@]+\.[^\s@]+$/;

export type Customer = {
  id: CustomerId;
  email: string;
  name: string;
  createdAt: string;
};

/**
 * Registers a customer. Emails are normalised so the same address cannot be
 * registered twice with different casing, and the id/clock stay injected.
 */
export function createCustomer(input: {
  id: CustomerId;
  email: string;
  name: string;
  createdAt: string;
}): Result<Customer, CustomerDomainError> {
  const email = input.email.trim().toLowerCase();
  const name = input.name.trim();

  if (!EMAIL_PATTERN.test(email)) {
    return err({
      type: "invalid_email",
      message: "Customer email is not a valid address",
    });
  }

  if (name.length === 0) {
    return err({
      type: "invalid_customer_name",
      message: "Customer name cannot be empty",
    });
  }

  if (name.length > MAX_CUSTOMER_NAME_LENGTH) {
    return err({
      type: "invalid_customer_name",
      message: `Customer name cannot exceed ${MAX_CUSTOMER_NAME_LENGTH} characters`,
    });
  }

  return ok({
    id: input.id,
    email,
    name,
    createdAt: input.createdAt,
  });
}

/** Renames a customer, keeping identity and registration date intact. */
export function renameCustomer(
  customer: Customer,
  name: string,
): Result<Customer, CustomerDomainError> {
  const trimmed = name.trim();

  if (trimmed.length === 0) {
    return err({
      type: "invalid_customer_name",
      message: "Customer name cannot be empty",
    });
  }

  if (trimmed.length > MAX_CUSTOMER_NAME_LENGTH) {
    return err({
      type: "invalid_customer_name",
      message: `Customer name cannot exceed ${MAX_CUSTOMER_NAME_LENGTH} characters`,
    });
  }

  return ok({ ...customer, name: trimmed });
}

FILE: ecommerce-backend/src/domain/inventory/inventory-item.ts

import type { ProductId } from "../product/product-id.js";
import { err, ok, type Result } from "../shared/result.js";
import type { InventoryDomainError } from "./inventory-errors.js";
import type { InventoryId } from "./inventory-id.js";

/**
 * Stock is split into what is free to promise (`available`) and what is held
 * for an open order (`reserved`). The split is what makes a reservation
 * reversible without losing the fact that units exist.
 */
export type InventoryItem = {
  id: InventoryId;
  productId: ProductId;
  available: number;
  reserved: number;
  updatedAt: string;
};

function isValidQuantity(quantity: number, minimum: number): boolean {
  return Number.isInteger(quantity) && quantity >= minimum;
}

export function createInventoryItem(input: {
  id: InventoryId;
  productId: ProductId;
  quantity: number;
  updatedAt: string;
}): Result<InventoryItem, InventoryDomainError> {
  if (!isValidQuantity(input.quantity, 0)) {
    return err({
      type: "invalid_quantity",
      message: "Initial quantity must be a non-negative integer",
    });
  }

  return ok({
    id: input.id,
    productId: input.productId,
    available: input.quantity,
    reserved: 0,
    updatedAt: input.updatedAt,
  });
}

/**
 * Answers "could these units be promised?" without changing anything.
 *
 * A check and a reservation are different operations: reading stock to price a
 * cart is not a mutation, so it must not look like one. `reserveStock` uses
 * this function, which keeps the definition of "enough" in exactly one place.
 */
export function checkAvailability(
  item: InventoryItem,
  quantity: number,
): Result<true, InventoryDomainError> {
  if (!isValidQuantity(quantity, 1)) {
    return err({
      type: "invalid_quantity",
      message: "Requested quantity must be a positive integer",
    });
  }

  if (item.available < quantity) {
    return err({
      type: "insufficient_stock",
      message: `Only ${item.available} unit(s) of product ${item.productId} are available`,
    });
  }

  return ok(true);
}

/** Holds units for an order. Fails when not enough stock is free. */
export function reserveStock(
  item: InventoryItem,
  quantity: number,
  reservedAt: string,
): Result<InventoryItem, InventoryDomainError> {
  const availability = checkAvailability(item, quantity);

  if (!availability.ok) {
    return err({
      type: availability.error.type,
      message: availability.error.message.replace(
        "Requested quantity",
        "Reserved quantity",
      ),
    });
  }

  return ok({
    ...item,
    available: item.available - quantity,
    reserved: item.reserved + quantity,
    updatedAt: reservedAt,
  });
}

/** Returns held units to the free pool, used when an order is cancelled. */
export function releaseStock(
  item: InventoryItem,
  quantity: number,
  releasedAt: string,
): Result<InventoryItem, InventoryDomainError> {
  if (!isValidQuantity(quantity, 1)) {
    return err({
      type: "invalid_quantity",
      message: "Released quantity must be a positive integer",
    });
  }

  if (item.reserved < quantity) {
    return err({
      type: "invalid_quantity",
      message: "Cannot release more units than are reserved",
    });
  }

  return ok({
    ...item,
    available: item.available + quantity,
    reserved: item.reserved - quantity,
    updatedAt: releasedAt,
  });
}

/** Consumes held units for good, used once payment has succeeded. */
export function commitReservation(
  item: InventoryItem,
  quantity: number,
  committedAt: string,
): Result<InventoryItem, InventoryDomainError> {
  if (!isValidQuantity(quantity, 1)) {
    return err({
      type: "invalid_quantity",
      message: "Committed quantity must be a positive integer",
    });
  }

  if (item.reserved < quantity) {
    return err({
      type: "invalid_quantity",
      message: "Cannot commit more units than are reserved",
    });
  }

  return ok({
    ...item,
    reserved: item.reserved - quantity,
    updatedAt: committedAt,
  });
}

/** Adds newly delivered units to the free pool. */
export function restock(
  item: InventoryItem,
  quantity: number,
  restockedAt: string,
): Result<InventoryItem, InventoryDomainError> {
  if (!isValidQuantity(quantity, 1)) {
    return err({
      type: "invalid_quantity",
      message: "Restocked quantity must be a positive integer",
    });
  }

  return ok({
    ...item,
    available: item.available + quantity,
    updatedAt: restockedAt,
  });
}

export type OrderDomainError =

import type { CartItem } from "../cart/cart-item.js";
import { multiplyMoney, type Money } from "../product/money.js";
import type { ProductId } from "../product/product-id.js";
import { err, ok, type Result } from "../shared/result.js";
import type { OrderDomainError } from "./order-errors.js";

/**
 * A line of a placed order. It snapshots name-free price data, so later
 * product edits cannot change what the customer agreed to pay.
 */
export type OrderItem = {
  productId: ProductId;
  quantity: number;
  unitPrice: Money;
  lineTotal: Money;
};

export function createOrderItem(input: {
  productId: ProductId;
  quantity: number;
  unitPrice: Money;
}): Result<OrderItem, OrderDomainError> {
  if (!Number.isInteger(input.quantity) || input.quantity < 1) {
    return err({
      type: "invalid_order_item",
      message: `Order item quantity for product ${input.productId} must be a positive integer`,
    });
  }

  return ok({
    productId: input.productId,
    quantity: input.quantity,
    unitPrice: input.unitPrice,
    lineTotal: multiplyMoney(input.unitPrice, input.quantity),
  });
}

export function orderItemFromCartItem(
  item: CartItem,
): Result<OrderItem, OrderDomainError> {
  return createOrderItem({
    productId: item.productId,
    quantity: item.quantity,
    unitPrice: item.unitPrice,
  });
}

/**
 * The order lifecycle. There is no `shipped`/`delivered` yet: adding those is
 * a new state transition, not a new order shape.
 */
export type OrderStatus = "pending_payment" | "paid" | "cancelled";

export function isPendingPayment(status: OrderStatus): boolean {
  return status === "pending_payment";
}

FILE: ecommerce-backend/src/domain/order/order.ts

import type { CustomerId } from "../customer/customer-id.js";
import { addMoney, type Money } from "../product/money.js";
import { err, ok, type Result } from "../shared/result.js";
import type { OrderDomainError, OrderTransitionError } from "./order-errors.js";
import type { OrderItem } from "./order-item.js";
import type { OrderStatus } from "./order-status.js";

export type OrderId = string;

export type Order = {
  id: OrderId;
  customerId: CustomerId;
  items: OrderItem[];
  total: Money;
  status: OrderStatus;
  createdAt: string;
  updatedAt: string;
  paidAt: string | null;
  cancelledAt: string | null;
};

/**
 * The total is always derived from the lines. Accepting a total from the
 * caller would allow an order whose price does not match its contents.
 */
function sumOrderItems(items: OrderItem[]): Result<Money, OrderDomainError> {
  const first = items[0];

  if (first === undefined) {
    return err({
      type: "empty_order",
      message: "An order must contain at least one item",
    });
  }

  let total: Money = {
    amountInMinorUnits: 0,
    currency: first.unitPrice.currency,
  };

  for (const item of items) {
    const sum = addMoney(total, item.lineTotal);

    if (!sum.ok) {
      return err({
        type: "invalid_order_item",
        message: sum.error.message,
      });
    }

    total = sum.value;
  }

  return ok(total);
}

export function createOrder(input: {
  id: OrderId;
  customerId: CustomerId;
  items: OrderItem[];
  createdAt: string;
}): Result<Order, OrderDomainError> {
  const total = sumOrderItems(input.items);

  if (!total.ok) {
    return total;
  }

  return ok({
    id: input.id,
    customerId: input.customerId,
    items: input.items,
    total: total.value,
    status: "pending_payment",
    createdAt: input.createdAt,
    updatedAt: input.createdAt,
    paidAt: null,
    cancelledAt: null,
  });
}

export function payOrder(
  order: Order,
  paidAt: string,
): Result<Order, OrderTransitionError> {
  if (order.status === "paid") {
    return err({
      type: "order_already_paid",
      message: "Order has already been paid",
    });
  }

  if (order.status === "cancelled") {
    return err({
      type: "order_cancelled",
      message: "A cancelled order cannot be paid",
    });
  }

  return ok({
    ...order,
    status: "paid",
    paidAt,
    updatedAt: paidAt,
  });
}

export function cancelOrder(
  order: Order,
  cancelledAt: string,
): Result<Order, OrderTransitionError> {
  if (order.status === "paid") {
    return err({
      type: "order_already_paid",
      message: "A paid order cannot be cancelled",
    });
  }

  if (order.status === "cancelled") {
    return err({
      type: "order_cancelled",
      message: "Order has already been cancelled",
    });
  }

  return ok({
    ...order,
    status: "cancelled",
    cancelledAt,
    updatedAt: cancelledAt,
  });
}

/** A payment is only ever pending once; it then resolves exactly once. */
export type PaymentStatus = "pending" | "succeeded" | "failed";

export function isPending(status: PaymentStatus): boolean {
  return status === "pending";
}

import type { OrderId } from "../order/order.js";
import type { Money } from "../product/money.js";
import { err, ok, type Result } from "../shared/result.js";
import type { PaymentDomainError } from "./payment-errors.js";
import type { PaymentStatus } from "./payment-status.js";

export type PaymentId = string;

export type Payment = {
  id: PaymentId;
  orderId: OrderId;
  amount: Money;
  status: PaymentStatus;
  providerReference: string | null;
  failureReason: string | null;
  createdAt: string;
  updatedAt: string;
};

export function createPayment(input: {
  id: PaymentId;
  orderId: OrderId;
  amount: Money;
  createdAt: string;
}): Result<Payment, PaymentDomainError> {
  if (input.amount.amountInMinorUnits <= 0) {
    return err({
      type: "invalid_payment_amount",
      message: "A payment amount must be greater than zero",
    });
  }

  return ok({
    id: input.id,
    orderId: input.orderId,
    amount: input.amount,
    status: "pending",
    providerReference: null,
    failureReason: null,
    createdAt: input.createdAt,
    updatedAt: input.createdAt,
  });
}

export function succeedPayment(
  payment: Payment,
  providerReference: string,
  succeededAt: string,
): Result<Payment, PaymentDomainError> {
  if (payment.status !== "pending") {
    return err({
      type: "payment_already_resolved",
      message: `Payment is already ${payment.status}`,
    });
  }

  return ok({
    ...payment,
    status: "succeeded",
    providerReference,
    updatedAt: succeededAt,
  });
}

export function failPayment(
  payment: Payment,
  failureReason: string,
  failedAt: string,
): Result<Payment, PaymentDomainError> {
  if (payment.status !== "pending") {
    return err({
      type: "payment_already_resolved",
      message: `Payment is already ${payment.status}`,
    });
  }

  return ok({
    ...payment,
    status: "failed",
    failureReason,
    updatedAt: failedAt,
  });
}

import { err, ok, type Result } from "../shared/result.js";
import type { ProductDomainError } from "./product-errors.js";

/**
 * Money as integer minor units plus a currency code. Floats are never used for
 * prices: 19.99 is stored as 1999, so totals cannot drift.
 */
export type Money = {
  amountInMinorUnits: number;
  currency: string;
};

const CURRENCY_PATTERN = /^[A-Z]{3}$/;

export function createMoney(
  amountInMinorUnits: number,
  currency: string,
): Result<Money, ProductDomainError> {
  const normalizedCurrency = currency.trim().toUpperCase();

  if (!Number.isSafeInteger(amountInMinorUnits) || amountInMinorUnits < 0) {
    return err({
      type: "invalid_amount",
      message: "Amount must be a non-negative integer in minor units",
    });
  }

  if (!CURRENCY_PATTERN.test(normalizedCurrency)) {
    return err({
      type: "invalid_currency",
      message: "Currency must be a three-letter ISO code",
    });
  }

  return ok({
    amountInMinorUnits,
    currency: normalizedCurrency,
  });
}

/** Adds two amounts, refusing to mix currencies silently. */
export function addMoney(
  left: Money,
  right: Money,
): Result<Money, ProductDomainError> {
  if (left.currency !== right.currency) {
    return err({
      type: "currency_mismatch",
      message: `Cannot combine ${left.currency} with ${right.currency}`,
    });
  }

  return createMoney(
    left.amountInMinorUnits + right.amountInMinorUnits,
    left.currency,
  );
}

/** Multiplies an amount by a whole quantity; the caller has bounded quantity. */
export function multiplyMoney(money: Money, quantity: number): Money {
  return {
    amountInMinorUnits: money.amountInMinorUnits * quantity,
    currency: money.currency,
  };
}

export function formatMoney(money: Money): string {
  return `${(money.amountInMinorUnits / 100).toFixed(2)} ${money.currency}`;
}

import { err, ok, type Result } from "../shared/result.js";
import type { Money } from "./money.js";
import type { ProductDomainError } from "./product-errors.js";
import type { ProductId } from "./product-id.js";

export const MAX_PRODUCT_NAME_LENGTH = 160;

/**
 * What the customer buys. Availability is not a product property: it is the
 * inventory aggregate's business, so nothing is duplicated here.
 */
export type Product = {
  id: ProductId;
  name: string;
  price: Money;
  createdAt: string;
};

/** A product must be named and priced above zero, or payment means nothing. */
export function createProduct(input: {
  id: ProductId;
  name: string;
  price: Money;
  createdAt: string;
}): Result<Product, ProductDomainError> {
  const name = input.name.trim();

  if (name.length === 0) {
    return err({
      type: "invalid_product_name",
      message: "Product name cannot be empty",
    });
  }

  if (name.length > MAX_PRODUCT_NAME_LENGTH) {
    return err({
      type: "invalid_product_name",
      message: `Product name cannot exceed ${MAX_PRODUCT_NAME_LENGTH} characters`,
    });
  }

  if (input.price.amountInMinorUnits <= 0) {
    return err({
      type: "invalid_amount",
      message: "Product price must be greater than zero",
    });
  }

  return ok({
    id: input.id,
    name,
    price: input.price,
    createdAt: input.createdAt,
  });
}

/**
 * Shape of an expected domain failure. Each module narrows it with a
 * discriminated union of literal `type` values, so callers switch safely and
 * no layer has to throw or inspect exception classes.
 */
export type DomainError<TType extends string = string> = {
  type: TType;
  message: string;
};

export type Result<T, E> =
  | {
      ok: true;
      value: T;
    }
  | {
      ok: false;
      error: E;
    };

export const ok = <T>(value: T): Result<T, never> => ({
  ok: true,
  value,
});

export const err = <E>(error: E): Result<never, E> => ({
  ok: false,
  error,
});

import { SignJWT, jwtVerify } from "jose";
import type { Principal } from "../../application/auth/principal.js";
import type {
  AccessToken,
  TokenService,
} from "../../application/ports/token-service.js";
import { isRole } from "../../domain/auth/role.js";

export type JwtTokenServiceOptions = {
  /** Shared secret; at least 32 characters. In production, a managed secret. */
  secret: string;
  issuer: string;
  audience: string;
  ttlSeconds: number;
};

/**
 * HS256 access tokens.
 *
 * Only the claims the application needs are carried: the customer id (`sub`),
 * the email, and the role. Issuer and audience are verified on the way in, so
 * a token minted for another service with the same secret is not accepted.
 */
export function createJwtTokenService({
  secret,
  issuer,
  audience,
  ttlSeconds,
}: JwtTokenServiceOptions): TokenService {
  const key = new TextEncoder().encode(secret);

  return {
    async issue(principal: Principal): Promise<AccessToken> {
      const issuedAt = Math.floor(Date.now() / 1000);
      const expiresAt = issuedAt + ttlSeconds;

      const token = await new SignJWT({
        email: principal.email,
        role: principal.role,
      })
        .setProtectedHeader({ alg: "HS256", typ: "JWT" })
        .setSubject(principal.customerId)
        .setIssuer(issuer)
        .setAudience(audience)
        .setIssuedAt(issuedAt)
        .setExpirationTime(expiresAt)
        .sign(key);

      return {
        token,
        expiresAt: new Date(expiresAt * 1000).toISOString(),
      };
    },

    async verify(token: string): Promise<Principal | null> {
      try {
        const { payload } = await jwtVerify(token, key, {
          issuer,
          audience,
          algorithms: ["HS256"],
          clockTolerance: 5,
        });

        const email = payload["email"];
        const role = payload["role"];

        if (
          typeof payload.sub !== "string" ||
          payload.sub.length === 0 ||
          typeof email !== "string" ||
          typeof role !== "string" ||
          !isRole(role)
        ) {
          return null;
        }

        return { customerId: payload.sub, email, role };
      } catch {
        // Expired, malformed, wrong signature, wrong issuer/audience: all the
        // same answer to the caller.
        return null;
      }
    },
  };
}

FILE: ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/auth/scrypt-password-hasher.ts

import type { PasswordHasher } from "../../application/ports/password-hasher.js";
import { randomBytes, scrypt, timingSafeEqual } from "node:crypto";

export type ScryptCost = {
  /** CPU/memory cost; must be a power of two. */
  n?: number;
  r?: number;
  p?: number;
};

export type ScryptPasswordHasherOptions = {
  /**
   * Lowered in tests only. The defaults follow OWASP's scrypt guidance
   * (N=2^15, r=8, p=1) and cost roughly 32 MiB and ~100 ms per verification,
   * which is the point: guessing must be expensive.
   */
  cost?: ScryptCost;
};

const DEFAULT_COST = { n: 32_768, r: 8, p: 1 } as const;
const KEY_LENGTH = 64;
const SALT_BYTES = 16;
const ALGORITHM = "scrypt";

type ResolvedCost = { n: number; r: number; p: number };

type ParsedHash = {
  cost: ResolvedCost;
  salt: Buffer;
  key: Buffer;
};

function resolveCost(cost: ScryptCost | undefined): ResolvedCost {
  return {
    n: cost?.n ?? DEFAULT_COST.n,
    r: cost?.r ?? DEFAULT_COST.r,
    p: cost?.p ?? DEFAULT_COST.p,
  };
}

function derive(
  password: string,
  salt: Buffer,
  keyLength: number,
  cost: ResolvedCost,
): Promise<Buffer> {
  return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
    scrypt(
      password,
      salt,
      keyLength,
      {
        N: cost.n,
        r: cost.r,
        p: cost.p,
        // Node requires maxmem above 128 * N * r; headroom avoids a resize
        // failure at the edge of the limit.
        maxmem: 256 * cost.n * cost.r,
      },
      (error, key) => {
        if (error) {
          reject(error);
        } else {
          resolve(key);
        }
      },
    );
  });
}

function format(cost: ResolvedCost, salt: Buffer, key: Buffer): string {
  return [
    ALGORITHM,
    cost.n,
    cost.r,
    cost.p,
    salt.toString("base64url"),
    key.toString("base64url"),
  ].join("$");
}

/**
 * Parses only hashes this adapter could have produced, with bounded
 * parameters: a corrupted or hostile row cannot make verification allocate
 * unbounded memory.
 */
function parse(stored: string): ParsedHash | null {
  const parts = stored.split("$");

  if (parts.length !== 6 || parts[0] !== ALGORITHM) {
    return null;
  }

  const n = Number(parts[1]);
  const r = Number(parts[2]);
  const p = Number(parts[3]);

  const powerOfTwo = Number.isInteger(n) && n >= 1_024 && n <= 1_048_576;
  const bounded = Number.isInteger(r) && r >= 1 && r <= 32;
  const parallel = Number.isInteger(p) && p >= 1 && p <= 16;

  if (!powerOfTwo || !bounded || !parallel) {
    return null;
  }

  const salt = Buffer.from(parts[4] ?? "", "base64url");
  const key = Buffer.from(parts[5] ?? "", "base64url");

  if (salt.length < 8 || key.length < 32 || key.length > 128) {
    return null;
  }

  return { cost: { n, r, p }, salt, key };
}

/**
 * scrypt from the Node standard library: no native build step, memory-hard,
 * and parameterised so the work factor can rise without changing the format.
 * The stored value carries its own parameters, so old hashes stay verifiable.
 */
export function createScryptPasswordHasher(
  options: ScryptPasswordHasherOptions = {},
): PasswordHasher {
  const cost = resolveCost(options.cost);

  // A fixed salt for the "no such account" path; the derived key is computed
  // lazily, once per process, and never compared successfully.
  const dummySalt = randomBytes(SALT_BYTES);
  let dummyKey: Promise<Buffer> | null = null;

  const dummy = (): Promise<Buffer> => {
    dummyKey ??= derive(
      "no-such-account-placeholder",
      dummySalt,
      KEY_LENGTH,
      cost,
    );

    return dummyKey;
  };

  return {
    async hash(password) {
      const salt = randomBytes(SALT_BYTES);
      const key = await derive(password, salt, KEY_LENGTH, cost);

      return format(cost, salt, key);
    },

    async verify(password, storedHash) {
      const parsed = storedHash === null ? null : parse(storedHash);

      if (storedHash !== null && parsed === null) {
        return false;
      }

      const expected = parsed?.key ?? (await dummy());
      const actual = await derive(
        password,
        parsed?.salt ?? dummySalt,
        expected.length,
        parsed?.cost ?? cost,
      );

      return (
        parsed !== null &&
        actual.length === expected.length &&
        timingSafeEqual(actual, expected)
      );
    },
  };
}

FILE: ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/cache/in-memory-cache.ts

import type { Cache } from "../../application/ports/cache.js";

type Entry = {
  value: unknown;
  expiresAt: number;
};

/**
 * A disposable cache. The clock is injectable so expiry is testable without
 * waiting, and entries are removed on read once they are stale.
 */
export function createInMemoryCache(now: () => number = Date.now): Cache {
  const entries = new Map<string, Entry>();

  return {
    async get<T>(key: string): Promise<T | null> {
      const entry = entries.get(key);

      if (entry === undefined) {
        return null;
      }

      if (entry.expiresAt <= now()) {
        entries.delete(key);
        return null;
      }

      return entry.value as T;
    },

    async set<T>(key: string, value: T, ttlSeconds: number): Promise<void> {
      entries.set(key, {
        value,
        expiresAt: now() + ttlSeconds * 1000,
      });
    },

    async delete(key: string): Promise<void> {
      entries.delete(key);
    },
  };
}

import { existsSync } from "node:fs";
import { z } from "zod";
import { err, ok, type Result } from "../../domain/shared/result.js";

export type AuthConfig = {
  jwtSecret: string;
  jwtIssuer: string;
  jwtAudience: string;
  accessTokenTtlSeconds: number;
  /** True when the development fallback is in use; never true in production. */
  usingDevelopmentSecret: boolean;
  /** Brute-force budget for register/login, per caller and route. */
  rateLimit: {
    max: number;
    windowSeconds: number;
  };
  /** Addresses that receive `admin` on registration; empty after bootstrap. */
  bootstrapAdminEmails: string[];
};

export type Config = {
  port: number;
  nodeEnv: "development" | "test" | "production";
  /** Set to use PostgreSQL (Prisma); null keeps the in-memory adapters. */
  databaseUrl: string | null;
  /** Set to use Redis for cache, idempotency, and rate limiting. */
  redisUrl: string | null;
  /** Set to publish outbox events to Kafka and run the consumer. */
  kafkaBrokers: string[] | null;
  kafkaClientId: string;
  kafkaConsumerGroup: string;
  rateLimit: {
    max: number;
    windowSeconds: number;
  };
  auth: AuthConfig;
  outboxPollIntervalMs: number;
};

export type ConfigError = {
  type: "invalid_configuration";
  message: string;
};

const optionalString = z.preprocess(
  (value) => (value === "" ? undefined : value),
  z.string().min(1).optional(),
);

/**
 * A development-only fallback, long enough for HS256 and obviously not a
 * production secret. Production refuses to start without a real one.
 */
const DEVELOPMENT_JWT_SECRET =
  "development-only-secret-change-before-deploying";

const MIN_JWT_SECRET_LENGTH = 32;

const envSchema = z
  .object({
    PORT: z.coerce.number().int().min(1).max(65535).default(3000),
    NODE_ENV: z
      .enum(["development", "test", "production"])
      .default("development"),
    // An empty value (docker compose passes one for an unset variable) means
    // "use the fallback adapter", not "connect to an empty URI".
    DATABASE_URL: optionalString,
    REDIS_URL: optionalString,
    KAFKA_BROKERS: optionalString,
    KAFKA_CLIENT_ID: z.string().min(1).default("ecommerce-backend"),
    KAFKA_CONSUMER_GROUP: z.string().min(1).default("ecommerce-order-events"),
    RATE_LIMIT_MAX: z.coerce.number().int().positive().default(120),
    RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_SECONDS: z.coerce.number().int().positive().default(60),
    JWT_SECRET: optionalString,
    JWT_ISSUER: z.string().min(1).default("ecommerce-backend"),
    JWT_AUDIENCE: z.string().min(1).default("ecommerce-api"),
    ACCESS_TOKEN_TTL_SECONDS: z.coerce
      .number()
      .int()
      .min(60)
      .max(86_400)
      .default(900),
    AUTH_RATE_LIMIT_MAX: z.coerce.number().int().positive().default(10),
    AUTH_RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_SECONDS: z.coerce
      .number()
      .int()
      .positive()
      .default(60),
    BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_EMAILS: optionalString,
    OUTBOX_POLL_INTERVAL_MS: z.coerce.number().int().min(100).default(1_000),
  })
  .superRefine((data, context) => {
    if (
      data.JWT_SECRET !== undefined &&
      data.JWT_SECRET.length < MIN_JWT_SECRET_LENGTH
    ) {
      context.addIssue({
        code: "custom",
        path: ["JWT_SECRET"],
        message: `must be at least ${MIN_JWT_SECRET_LENGTH} characters`,
      });
    }

    if (data.NODE_ENV === "production" && data.JWT_SECRET === undefined) {
      context.addIssue({
        code: "custom",
        path: ["JWT_SECRET"],
        message: "is required in production",
      });
    }
  });

/** Loads a local `.env` when present, then hands back `process.env`. */
export function loadEnvironment(): NodeJS.ProcessEnv {
  if (existsSync(".env")) {
    process.loadEnvFile();
  }

  return process.env;
}

function parseList(value: string | undefined): string[] {
  if (value === undefined) {
    return [];
  }

  return value
    .split(",")
    .map((entry) => entry.trim().toLowerCase())
    .filter((entry) => entry.length > 0);
}

function parseBrokers(value: string | undefined): string[] | null {
  if (value === undefined) {
    return null;
  }

  const brokers = value
    .split(",")
    .map((broker) => broker.trim())
    .filter((broker) => broker.length > 0);

  return brokers.length === 0 ? null : brokers;
}

/**
 * Validates the environment once, at the edge. No other module reads
 * `process.env`, so defaults and failure messages cannot drift apart.
 */
export function parseEnvironment(
  env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv,
): Result<Config, ConfigError> {
  const parsed = envSchema.safeParse(env);

  if (!parsed.success) {
    const details = parsed.error.issues
      .map(
        (issue) => `${issue.path.join(".") || "environment"}: ${issue.message}`,
      )
      .join("; ");

    return err({
      type: "invalid_configuration",
      message: `Invalid configuration: ${details}`,
    });
  }

  return ok({
    port: parsed.data.PORT,
    nodeEnv: parsed.data.NODE_ENV,
    databaseUrl: parsed.data.DATABASE_URL ?? null,
    redisUrl: parsed.data.REDIS_URL ?? null,
    kafkaBrokers: parseBrokers(parsed.data.KAFKA_BROKERS),
    kafkaClientId: parsed.data.KAFKA_CLIENT_ID,
    kafkaConsumerGroup: parsed.data.KAFKA_CONSUMER_GROUP,
    rateLimit: {
      max: parsed.data.RATE_LIMIT_MAX,
      windowSeconds: parsed.data.RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_SECONDS,
    },
    auth: {
      jwtSecret: parsed.data.JWT_SECRET ?? DEVELOPMENT_JWT_SECRET,
      jwtIssuer: parsed.data.JWT_ISSUER,
      jwtAudience: parsed.data.JWT_AUDIENCE,
      accessTokenTtlSeconds: parsed.data.ACCESS_TOKEN_TTL_SECONDS,
      usingDevelopmentSecret: parsed.data.JWT_SECRET === undefined,
      rateLimit: {
        max: parsed.data.AUTH_RATE_LIMIT_MAX,
        windowSeconds: parsed.data.AUTH_RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_SECONDS,
      },
      bootstrapAdminEmails: parseList(parsed.data.BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_EMAILS),
    },
    outboxPollIntervalMs: parsed.data.OUTBOX_POLL_INTERVAL_MS,
  });
}

/**
 * The in-process publisher: it writes events to stdout as JSON lines, which is
 * exactly the payload shape a log collector or broker producer expects. A

): EventPublisher {

  };

FILE: ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/health/health-check.ts
39 lines | 1,008 bytes

/**
 * A dependency the process needs in order to serve traffic. Liveness answers
 * "is this process alive"; readiness answers "should a load balancer send it
 * work", which is why it pings the database and the cache.
 */
export type HealthCheck = {
  name: string;
  check(): Promise<void>;
};

export type HealthReport = {
  status: "ok" | "degraded";
  checks: Record<string, "up" | "down">;
};

export async function runHealthChecks(
  checks: HealthCheck[],
): Promise<HealthReport> {
  const results = await Promise.allSettled(
    checks.map(async (check) => {
      await check.check();
      return check.name;
    }),
  );

  const report: Record<string, "up" | "down"> = {};

  results.forEach((result, index) => {
    const name = checks[index]?.name ?? "unknown";
    report[name] = result.status === "fulfilled" ? "up" : "down";
  });

  const healthy = Object.values(report).every((status) => status === "up");

  return {
    status: healthy ? "ok" : "degraded",
    checks: report,
  };
}
